## Section-aware career record suggestions - 2026-11-09

- Added a default automatic history-entry option and inspected-section picker. The local model interprets the section; repeatable rules select a complete checked record across all saved entries. Current work/study, latest definitely completed history and a unique generic entry are supported. Missing/overlapping/future dates, concurrent current entries, mixed sections and unsupported rankings remain reviewable. Explicit selection remains available. This does not assign records globally to every repeated row.
- Browser section paths now retain stable backend identities, names and inference status, qualified across frames and shadow roots. Identical headings do not collapse identities. Section changes participate in approval/advice hashes; stale suggestions stay invalid after a change is reverted. Legacy empty-context payloads retain their serialization. A headless test caught unstable frontend node IDs; corrected backend identities passed stable inspection and moved-field checks.
- The local 12B model selected correct current/previous employment and education records and completed ten visual/live-verified values in one fictional 14-field form; four restricted fields stayed empty. Seven additional intent cases passed, including German and French descriptions and three ambiguous/unsupported requests. These are small controlled checks, not broad site or model qualification. The new 14B run still blocked ordinary career fields and failed completeness; those flags were preserved.
- Final web suite: 726 passed, 397 opt-in checks ignored, no failures. Affected application/browser/inference/workspace library suites passed 34/29/20/60 tests with seven ignored. Managed Windows and affected-crate Clippy passed; the debug app was rebuilt. Clock-boundary, date precision, record-order, subject, scope and stale-result guards passed. See [scope and evidence](docs/HISTORY_SECTION_SELECTION_2026-11-09.md).
- The rebuilt app passed 29 grouped fictional UI checks, including section selection, unchecked proposals, five viewport widths and 12 actual browser-zoom cases from 80–400%. Zoom checks now require visible, unobstructed controls. A screenshot-path mismatch was diagnosed and retained; native compositor capture shows the actual 400% interface. Narrow/wide and zoom captures were inspected; no script errors occurred. This is scoped Chromium evidence, not universal UI qualification.
- No real application was interacted with or submitted. No release was created. Broad live-site completion, proven no-write isolation, history-based document tailoring and clean-Windows runtime qualification remain open.

Profile conflict/legacy preservation and malformed-history refusal were checked through the application. Clippy, formatting and the managed SQLCipher build passed. No model was loaded, employer form interacted with or application/email sent. Work remains local; no push or release.

## Expanded browser transport qualification - 2026-11-09

- Extended the fictional receiver matrix to all nine scripted channels on IPv6 and actual WebRTC TURN Allocate attempts over UDP/TCP on IPv4/IPv6. Every case requires a working unrestricted control using matching engine file hashes and version; isolated attempts added no observed traffic. Loopback-only observations do not establish Internet isolation.
- Added an explicit opt-in free public HTTPS canary, with random fictional markers, bounded complete-history validation, positive browser controls before/after, a ten-second receiver observation window, 15-minute expiry and confirmed deletion. The combined run passed five Rust integration cases, one Rust owner-failure case and six Python lifecycle methods. Both public controls delivered one POST; isolation had zero receiver arrivals. No employer form, candidate data, email or application was involved. This remains experimental and unavailable in the application interface.
- Early relay runs exposed Chromium utility logging beside the selected engine and were correctly rejected as inventory drift. Controls now execute from a separate hash-verified private copy; the source inventory remains strict. Windows launch ownership preserves environment overrides/removals and working directory. The successful run confirmed unchanged runtime bytes and cleanup of the receiver and control copy; no owned isolated browser or recovery record remained in the post-run inventory.
- Validation also passed 29 browser-library tests (four ignored), seven HTTP-fixture tests, two relay receiver/protocol tests, 22 development-tool tests, three focused headless browser cleanup checks, browser Clippy and the managed Windows build. Four minimal lifecycle sessions measured a maximum browser-job commit peak of 59,408,384 bytes and minimum sampled free host RAM of 27,055,849,472 bytes; these exclude full form, model, GPU and whole-host peaks. Exact receipt binding and remaining gates are in `docs/ISOLATED_REHEARSAL.md`. Development remains local, with no push or release.

## Isolated-session recovery and resource evidence - 2026-11-09

- Added private write-ahead ownership records before profile creation, PID/creation-time binding, a per-user global Windows admission mutex and atomic job-list assignment at process creation. The browser remains suspended until its identity is recorded and its zero-capability token checked. Recovery queries original identities and named jobs, retains unknown/corrupt ownership with an explicit denominator, and never terminates by a saved PID. It runs on the next session after parent death and in a separate bounded worker after a Rust transport failure; it does not resume or submit an application.
- A combined headless run passed three Rust checks and six Python lifecycle methods, with explicit executed/ignored counts. Cases include all previous field/PDF checks, refusal to read a protected host-only PDF, event-consumer disconnection, active-session recovery refusal, abandoned-mutex acquisition, hard kill followed by automatic next-session cleanup, and three startup interruption points. The latter include atomic browser creation before its stamp is saved or its first thread resumed. Production recovery replaced the former test-only profile deletion. Thirteen portable checks and two qualification-denominator checks passed; zero-test filters, skipped and ambiguous summaries cannot produce a successful qualification report.
- The nine-channel control is now required to use the exact same headless-shell executable and reported version as isolation. Removed the separately introduced serial receiver and reused the existing bounded concurrent fixture; a new observer waits for complete bodies. Five server regressions and five focused existing browser cases passed. Incomplete worker/image/other baselines and their differing browser versions remain retained as failures, not evidence of isolation. Subsequent matched-engine/shared-fixture runs passed. An additional completion-marker assertion verifies that the isolated script reached its final channel attempt; its focused follow-up is recorded separately from the combined receipt.
- Added periodic, bounded profile-storage inspection and free-disk checks, plus OS browser-job peak commit accounting after termination. Four normal-close minimal lifecycle sessions measured at most 59,547,648 bytes (56.79 MiB) of browser-job peak commit, at least 24,799,088,640 bytes (23.10 GiB) sampled free host RAM and at most 296,337,854 logical profile bytes including the engine. These are not full application, LLM, GPU or whole-host peak measurements. Current resource and recovery limits, remaining schedules and private evidence binding are in `docs/ISOLATED_REHEARSAL.md`.
- Browser library tests passed (28 passed, four opt-in checks ignored), browser Clippy and the managed Windows/SQLCipher build passed. No owned isolated browser remained in the checked post-run inventory. The mode remains unavailable in the application interface pending broader egress/broker and runtime qualification; no employer form was interacted with and no application or email was sent. Changes remain local; no release was created.

## SQLite and encrypted-engine upgrade qualification - 2026-11-09

- Replaced the locked `rusqlite 0.32.1` / `libsqlite3-sys 0.30.1` path with `rusqlite 0.41.1` and a clearly identified local `libsqlite3-sys 0.39.1+smartjobseeker.1` patch. The complete 22-file published binding archive is checksum-bound; its Rust, bindings and C source are unchanged. Only the normalized manifest's local build metadata and exact `sqlcipher-src` pin change. The pin advances to `420.0.0`, supplying SQLCipher 4.20.0 / SQLite 3.54.1 instead of the affected source in the nominally patched upstream binding. A provenance verifier and CI check bind the archive, file map and exact manifest edits. Original licensing notices are retained.
- Centralized the shared Rust SQLite dependency and explicitly enabled its upstream `fallible_uint` feature, preserving checked unsigned SQL conversions after the API upgrade. New store checks reject negative reads and out-of-range writes and require the engine patch baseline. The plain-store regression pass initially found a query-plan assertion tied to the older wording; it now accepts an indexed SEARCH through either the ordinary or covering form of the same requisition index. The new plan actually uses the covering index; no migration or query was weakened to satisfy that check.
- Captured a fictional encrypted database using the preserved old build: SQLCipher `4.5.7 community`, SQLite `3.45.3`. The new build opened a separate copy, preserved Unicode text and binary data, rejected an incorrect key, passed integrity checks and persisted a new row. The preserved old executable then read both rows and passed integrity checks against the upgraded copy. The original fixture's hash remained unchanged. Fixtures, old executable and raw observations remain under ignored `target/`. This establishes that one fictional schema/data fixture round-trips, not compatibility with every historical workspace, cipher setting or database format.
- Actual encrypted-engine checks report SQLCipher `4.20.0 community` / SQLite `3.54.1`; the plain SQLite path also passes its version-floor and store checks. Full workspace validation passed **1079 tests with 550 opt-in cases ignored** across 37 reported test targets, with four test threads; full workspace Clippy passed. The source verifier and agent-tool suite passed, as did the package-helper regressions. `cargo audit --deny warnings` passed without ignores; license/ban/source checks passed after reviewing a package/version-scoped allowance for the native source's complete `MIT AND BSD-3-Clause AND blessing AND WTFPL` expression. No audit finding was suppressed.
- The locked graph remains 353 package versions and 16 workspace members. The embedded license inventory was regenerated. Packaging now follows Cargo metadata for vendored dependencies and includes normal/build dependency closures and nested native-component notices. A private incomplete bundle contained 192 dependency license inventories, including the binding's MIT/SQLite notices and all three native-source license files; it omitted Ollama and LibreOffice and is not a distribution qualification. The canonical SQLCipher notice now matches the selected upstream source. The final compiled app also passed a 16-case headless smoke/interaction pass with zero failures or script errors, using fictional data. These local changes are being accumulated for a larger development checkpoint rather than immediately pushed. Application semantic indexing explicitly excludes `third_party/` to keep the large upstream amalgamation out of application retrieval and embedding memory; exact provenance is checked separately.

## Controllable isolated-browser feasibility and current dependency gate - 2026-11-09

- A new private Windows probe successfully controlled `HeadlessChrome/153.0.8010.12` through inherited CDP pipes inside a zero-capability AppContainer. It verified the root process token before resuming, read a fictional local page, set and read back a fictional field, and captured a screenshot. The normal loopback baseline succeeded; isolated curl timed out with zero received requests. Browser-script attempts using fetch POST, beacon, WebSocket and a button invoking form submission produced zero received loopback requests during the probe's observation window. This is narrow feasibility evidence, not a complete no-egress or no-submission qualification, and no production mode was enabled.
- The successful probe copied the existing headless-shell runtime (283,223,692 bytes) into the new AppContainer's owned directory so its data files were readable. It used a 2 GiB job commit limit, `--no-sandbox` inside the outer AppContainer, an explicit inherited-handle list, separate CDP/stderr handles and a bounded wait for the actual fictional document. An earlier direct-cache launch failed to read ICU data; an earlier combined stderr/CDP stream corrupted protocol parsing. Ordinary Chrome failed in Crashpad named-pipe initialization with access denied, including when its documented testing switch was supplied. These failure records remain private and were not treated as successful isolation checks.
- The root process exited with code zero, the AppContainer profile-deletion API returned zero, and a subsequent process inventory found no browser using the probe's profile prefix. Available host RAM was 30.1 GiB in the post-probe snapshot, not a peak measurement. Descendant-token inventory, additional network channels, control/parent failures, modern-runtime qualification, the production transport and captured-site replay remain unverified. No real form was interacted with and no application was submitted.
- Private probe artifacts remain under ignored `target/`. Their SHA-256 values are recorded below to bind this observation. The screenshot was visually inspected and contains only the fictional heading, field and button. The Microsoft inheritance-page capture succeeded (native SHA-256 `77a5b87aed7ef3d8b01e079eb92508f73dfe34eb5e8c58a3b0fa02b192bdca8d`). Two Google source-page requests returned HTTP 503 and an outdated mirror file path returned 404; those failures were retained. The current Chromium switch definition was read through the native GitHub content API from `chrome/common/chrome_switches.h`, blob `016504b4296051901b8c5ba4c91cab51ce673e22`, without substituting search snippets for source content.
- CI run `42598032235` for `89407eb8` passed build-and-test, browser-e2e, PDF regression and agent-tool contracts. Its supply-chain gate failed on newly reported `RUSTSEC-2026-0459`, affecting the locked `libsqlite3-sys 0.30.1`. This is a current open gate, not an audit exception. The advisory warns that a nominal upgrade to `libsqlite3-sys 0.39.1` still uses affected SQLCipher sources; its published manifest pins `sqlcipher-src =419.0.6`. The published `sqlcipher-src 420.0.0` package identifies SQLite 3.54.1. The repair must verify the actual plain-SQLite and encrypted engine versions and database behavior, rather than merely making the package-version audit green. Native advisory and package metadata captures remain under ignored `target/research/`.

## Browser fixture repair and capability comparison - 2026-11-09

- Reading the actual log for CI run `42582203784` (`12fc1504`) established **133 passed and three failed**, rather than the single fixture failure previously identified in the plan. Failures were the late-field review-state assertion, WebSocket fixture navigation and disconnect-probe baseline evaluation. This corrects the diagnosis; their hosted root causes are not fully established by a local pass.
- Replaced the three affected probes' HTTP servers with a shared bounded fictional fixture. It handles idle speculative connections concurrently, reads fragmented headers and complete bodies before counting requests, reports malformed requests, caps connections and request sizes, and joins its workers on normal cleanup or unwinding. Fixtures start after browser launch rather than consuming their active lifetime during startup. The disconnect probe now verifies its exact document URL and ready state instead of accepting any queued load event. These repairs address concrete fixture defects; they are development infrastructure, not a production network boundary.
- Kept the driver's existing two-consecutive-nonempty-snapshot settling rule and observation budget, extracted its state for deterministic verification, and added value-free snapshot counts to its trace. The late-field browser check now compares the review state with actual observations; a slow first inspection may already see the added field and subsequently settle. Unit checks verify reset on schema change, two required matches and refusal to treat empty forms as settled.
- Local validation passed 27 browser-library tests (three opt-in cases ignored), four new HTTP fixture regressions and five focused headless Chrome cases: input-triggered POST, input-triggered WebSocket, disconnect observation, late-added fields and disappearing fields. The two input-triggered cases and disconnect probe also passed headlessly on Edge. Browser Clippy and the Windows web build passed. Both local disconnect observations still recorded one fictional POST after control disconnected; it explicitly does **not** qualify submission isolation. No employer form was interacted with and no application was sent.
- Added `docs/CAPABILITY_COVERAGE.md` from five retained first-party source captures: a connected acceptance map covering advanced form completion, discovery, documents, tracking, contacts, interviews, follow-ups, portability, resources and interface behavior. Advertised capabilities are comparison inputs, not independent quality measurements or a claim that all listed features are implemented. No third-party code or design assets were copied.
- Hosted run `42595454558` for preceding UI commit `10996953` passed build-and-test, supply-chain, PDF regression and agent-tool contracts. Its downloaded layout artifact binds that exact SHA and a release-profile executable digest and records **296 passed cases, zero failures and zero script errors** on Chrome `156.0.8078.80`. Its browser-e2e job also passed: 136 cases, zero failures and four explicitly excluded opt-in diagnostics. That later green run does not erase the earlier intermittent failures or independently qualify the local repairs above. CI browser jobs now have a 20-minute overall timeout.

## Saved document evidence for a selected job — 2026-11-09

- PDF rows now offer Check for job. The authenticated, read-only `/api/documents/job-check` endpoint binds its response to an existing job, document ID and current PDF-byte hash. It reports matching generated-draft evidence, current/stale/unverifiable draft context, and bounded visual-review inventory. Missing generation evidence is normal for imported PDFs; unreadable or incomplete lookups remain explicit. The response always requires user review and makes no claim of factual accuracy, ATS acceptance, attachment or submission authorization.
- The UI defaults to the selected job, rejects mismatched responses and prevents older requests replacing newer results. Its visual-review and CV-check actions carry the selected job; a pending check for the same document and another job is surfaced instead of silently switching contexts. A visual refinement widened the active evidence panel after screenshot review.
- Backend tests passed for in-memory unavailable records, disk-backed absent records, PDF header validation, missing job/document IDs, authenticated loopback endpoint output, and stale context after a new qualification. UI tests covered incomplete/stale summaries, result races, mismatches and conflicting pending jobs. All 153 UI check scripts passed, including the pending-job guard tests. Windows build and Clippy passed. A headless isolated interface with mocked fictional record summaries showed no script errors or horizontal overflow at the reviewed widths; it did not perform new visual inference, extraction or employer-site actions.

## Document library preparation and filters — 2026-11-09

- Document rendering is now separate from workspace-state refresh. The library supports filename search, PDF and convertible-format filters, and a selected-templates filter based on the saved CV and cover-letter template IDs. Each row distinguishes PDF format (review still required), formats with a conversion action, and formats without that action. Unsupported extraction formats retain download access without an unusable Review text button. No file is declared ready merely because its extension is PDF.
- All 152 JavaScript UI check scripts passed after correcting a listener-placement error found during refactoring and updating the extraction-race harness boundary. The Windows app built successfully. A headless isolated-app check with mocked fictional DOCX, PDF and ZIP metadata exercised selected-template badges, filtering, no-match results, filter preservation after refresh and unsupported-format actions. Desktop and mobile screenshots were reviewed, with a spacing refinement; no script errors or horizontal overflow were observed. This did not run extraction, conversion, PDF visual review or employer-form attachment and does not establish job-specific document readiness.

Screenshots informed a second pass to remove tab wrapping at 390 pixels. The compiled app built successfully; navigation unit checks passed; no script errors or horizontal overflow were observed at widths 390, 768, 1024 and 1440. This is a focused layout and navigation check, not a full accessibility evaluation. No employer pages or candidate documents were involved.

## Bounded closure-check excerpts — 2026-11-03

- Closure advice now scans up to 16,000 bytes in overlapping 4,000-byte excerpts instead of sending the entire capture in one model request. A positive result must quote the excerpt exactly; a failed request or an all-negative multi-excerpt check remains explicitly unavailable because page-wide context may be missing. This can require up to five model calls and may be slower. Fictional boundary, Unicode and failure tests passed, along with Windows web Clippy. A live retry on the previously captured closed listing was stopped by the RAM guard at 10,187 MiB available; the model unloaded. Therefore improved live closure detection is not established.

## Late browser startup cleanup — 2026-11-03

- When Windows browser ownership starts after its caller has timed out, the owner now terminates its unclaimed process group and attempts to remove its private temporary profile. A focused fictional process test forced a delayed handoff and verified profile cleanup; normal browser shutdown, forced-parent termination and browser Clippy also passed. Cleanup remains best-effort if filesystem removal itself fails, and a forcibly terminated application can still leave its profile directory on disk.

## Windows browser process ownership — 2026-11-03

- Newly launched Windows browsers now run inside a `processkit` process group whose job handle is held by a dedicated owner thread. If the Waypoint process is forcibly terminated, Windows closes that job and terminates its contained browser processes. A fictional Windows test recorded the browser and its observed descendants, killed only the helper parent, and confirmed those recorded PIDs exited; normal shutdown and a local form-capture test also passed. This does not cover previously orphaned processes, every browser configuration, or removal of the temporary profile after a forced parent termination. Browser unit tests and Clippy passed; licenses/bans/sources passed. The required advisory check reported three existing unmaintained-crate warnings (RUSTSEC-2026-0370, -0371 and -0372); the lockfile change adds dependency edges only, not package versions. No model or employer application was used.

## Owned browser shutdown — 2026-11-03

- Normal browser shutdown now makes a bounded local DevTools `Browser.close` request before the existing forced-kill fallback and profile cleanup. A fictional local browser test confirmed the private profile was removed; no Waypoint browser process remained in a subsequent process inventory. Browser unit tests passed (21, three ignored), the targeted live shutdown test passed, and browser Clippy passed. This does not prove descendant cleanup in every failure mode and cannot prevent an orphan if the parent is forcibly terminated; OS-level process-tree ownership remains to be implemented and tested.

It leaves location unknown and records a review warning rather than silently dropping those entries. Candidate-inspection tests passed (51 passed, 25 ignored), as did Windows web Clippy. Missing fields that were never supplied are not proof of a complete location; listings still need review. No live source or model was used.

- FreeSerp searches can optionally request recency ranking for individual searches and batches, including selected-page retries. The UI identifies each returned page's ranking preference, including older history where it was not recorded. The provider documents this as ranking, not a publication-date filter; results still require vacancy and application-page verification. On 2026-11-03 a bounded live batch with two non-personal English and Spanish phrases returned 20 candidate links across two successful pages; their relevance and availability were not assessed. Search tests (39 passed, 6 ignored), 147 UI checks, and Windows Clippy passed. FreeSerp documentation was read through the isolated provider; the retained local receipt SHA-256 is `8e4bd87380eb0c1b581c9a3dadd306ee859941c3b3f69c54099c5a045250a90d`.

- Search parsing now omits results explicitly marked as advertisements by supported provider fields and reports their count separately from invalid results. A labelled ad does not suppress a later unlabelled result at the same URL. Fictional parser and coverage UI tests passed; provider labelling varies, so unlabelled ads can remain and listings still require verification. No live provider was contacted for this change.

- The ad filter now retains results whose only signal is a generic `sponsored` field or result type, since that can be ambiguous in job data. It omits explicit `is_ad` flags and ad/advertisement types. A fictional parser test covered both ambiguous cases and explicit ads; historical searches are not reprocessed.

- If loading carried search history fails during same-tab recovery, the interface now reports the issue but still reconnects to the active task. A later terminal response restores its sealed prior history. Focused UI tests covered the transient lookup failure and terminal restoration; this does not guarantee recovery if both backend requests fail.

- When a retry carries earlier pages, exact repeated URLs now appear as one candidate card. Later reports are retained as repeated-result evidence and contribute bounded provider/date metadata; the stored histories are unchanged. Similar URLs remain separate for review, and repeated-report limits remain visible. Focused candidate-selection, retry and restart UI checks passed.

- A fictional local API test now exercises the sealed-history retry end to end: a mismatched prior task is rejected, client-supplied history is ignored, and the matching saved candidate remains in the sealed workspace after the retry and reopen. The test passed against the configured Windows workspace; no public provider was contacted.

- A local API integration test verifies that a selected-page retry rejects a query outside the reviewed phrases, requests only the chosen page from a fictional provider and returns an explicit checkpoint-saved status. A separate constructed sparse checkpoint survived workspace reopening, and the UI prepared only its unfinished pages. Both checks passed; neither forcibly terminated the app or contacted a public provider.

- Completed or stopped web-search batches retain their bounded request plan and provider, allowing “Prepare unfinished searches” to identify failed and unattempted pages without waiting for a restart. A prepared retry requests only those query/page pairs unless the user edits the prepared phrases or page count, in which case the normal full batch runs. Earlier successful groups are loaded from the matching user-sealed workspace history and carried into the retry checkpoint, bounded to 50 groups, 600 candidates and 8 MiB of serialized prior history. A mismatched or unavailable saved source prevents that history-dependent retry; checkpoint failures are reported. Exact repeated URLs are grouped in the retry display, while similar URLs remain separate. A fictional encrypted-workspace reopen test, search-batch suite, UI checks and Windows Clippy passed. This does not automatically switch providers or confirm candidate relevance.

- Later FreeSerp batch reruns failed on their first page because the provider reported that its general web index was unavailable and had fallen back to a different index. Waypoint surfaced that error instead of treating the fallback as general web results; the remaining relevance sample was not collected. The live test now prints a few candidate titles and URLs on successful pages and waits for final coverage before asserting provider success. Provider availability can change between runs.

- The web-search batch worker queried Wiby with three generic job phrases. All three requests succeeded, but two retained no candidates and the six retained for “remote rust jobs” included unrelated pages. A separate FreeSerp test returned 40 retained candidates across two phrases and two pages, with four successful requests. These are provider-returned candidates, not verified current jobs or application routes; the tests did not assess FreeSerp relevance. No candidate details were sent and no applications were opened or submitted.

## Windows web integration check — 2026-11-03

- The current Windows web backend passed its serial suite: 672 passed and 376 ignored. The first run failed one experience-ranking fixture because it used an observation timestamp of zero, which current selection correctly excludes; the fixture was corrected and the full suite rerun. Ignored tests include live model, public network and browser cases, so this is not end-to-end qualification.

- After the recent template, match-coverage and navigation changes, the Windows web-app suite passed with 653 tests passed and 359 ignored. The ignored tests include live-model, browser and network cases; this run does not establish their behavior or end-to-end application capability. Tests ran serially with the model unloaded.

## Template voice review — 2026-11-03

- A scripted full template-suggestion test now exercises a cosmetic first result followed by either a revised or unsupported retry. It checks that the revised result retains the unverified-template provenance warning and that the unsupported retry falls back to the initial suggestion. The focused test passed; it is not a live-model factuality evaluation.

- When a template suggestion differs only in whitespace or apostrophe style, drafting now makes at most one additional attempt, asking for a clearer factual rephrasing only if justified. A retry is used only when it changes more than those details and has a completed evidence check without unsupported claims; otherwise the original suggestion is retained and the interface reports the outcome. Uncertain claims and model evidence judgments still need review. Scripted retry and UI checks and Windows Clippy passed. In one resource-gated fictional `qwen3.5:9b` run the retry was still cosmetic and the first suggestion was retained, so this does not establish improved tailoring. The extra attempt can add several model calls; the model unloaded afterward.

- Template review now calls out suggestions that differ from the original only in whitespace or straight-versus-curly apostrophe style. The UI fixture also checks that changed words and programming-language punctuation are not treated as cosmetic. This does not measure whether other edits improve the document.

- In one resource-gated fictional template test, `qwen3.5:9b` retained first-person voice and the voice-change notice correctly remained off. Its output differed only in apostrophe style, so the test does not demonstrate useful tailoring. The model's evidence-check workflow completed; the model was unloaded afterward. No document was saved or application submitted.

- The template-suggestion voice-change notice now recognizes common English first-person contractions with straight or curly apostrophes, including “I've” and “we’re.” A scripted suggestion and focused tests passed. This is an opening-word heuristic, not a general grammatical or multilingual voice check.

## Qualification coverage in match estimates — 2026-11-03

- The local CV text check now includes uppercase two-letter ASCII acronyms such as AI, ML and QA, while not treating lowercase ordinary words as those terms. It reports this spelling limitation; it remains a literal advisory check, not an ATS result or evidence of a qualification. Fictional CV-check tests passed (9 passed, 1 ignored), along with Windows web Clippy. No model or real document was used.

- Repeatability comparisons now distinguish identical selected qualifications in the same order, the same selections in a different order, changed selections and unavailable selection records. They compare exact recorded text without exposing it in the comparison. The fit suite passed (77 passed, 48 ignored), the focused UI check and Windows web Clippy passed. This does not establish identical later model calls or model accuracy; no model was loaded for the change.

- Qualification coverage now requires the recorded selected-fact list to agree with its reported count before claiming complete, selected or no-input coverage. Missing, malformed or inconsistent records report coverage unavailable. The fit suite (77 passed, 48 ignored), saved-assessment and batch API checks, and Windows web Clippy passed. This checks recorded input consistency, not whether the model understood the facts.

- Comparisons of repeated assessments now separately count changes in weight, judgment and cited qualification for exactly matched source excerpts. Counts can overlap and do not validate the model's decisions or match score. Focused comparison and UI logic tests and Windows web Clippy passed; no live-model repeatability measurement was made in this change.

- Fictional saved-assessment and batch API tests confirm the “no qualifications used” coverage is recomputed after reading a saved result and reaches the job-list response without exposing the qualification text. Both tests passed.

- When reviewed qualifications exist but the selection report says none were supplied to an assessment, the job badge now says “no qualifications used” instead of implying a subset was selected or showing only a generic limit. The distinction is derived from recorded input counts and does not change the score or prove the model used any supplied text. Focused Rust and UI checks passed.

## Application email worker recovery — 2026-11-03

- The background worker retains its latest completed result for ten minutes for repeat polling after automatic collection, scoped to the same disk workspace and attempt ID. Active worker polling also checks the workspace path. The database journal remains authoritative after restart or reconciliation; the replay is not a fresh mailbox check. A disk-backed fixture exercised a simulated transport, server collection, repeat polling and rejection from another workspace. The worker test, a separate local email-contact browser check and Windows web Clippy passed. Application-email sending remains unexposed, and no email was sent.

## Public application control observation — 2026-11-03

- Visible asterisk markers on verified associated or wrapping labels now also mark their fields as required with a manual-review warning. A fictional browser fixture covered visible, hidden and ambiguous labels; the separate 14 label-related browser tests and Windows web Clippy passed. This inference does not cover every required-marker convention or override ambiguity, and no employer page or model was used in this change.

- The read-only public-form model evaluation now requires an actual structured-generation call, rejects deterministic fallback or blocked mapping as a success, and checks recognizable suggested values against a fictional profile. A resource-gated run attempted before this change stopped at startup: 16,491 MiB available versus 16,538 MiB required. The model never loaded and no inference was measured. The revised evaluation compiled under Windows web Clippy but has not yet run with sufficient resources. No candidate data was entered or submitted.

- On the same real form, an empty `for` attribute incorrectly associated the first field's label with an ID-less consent checkbox. Empty associations are now ignored. When an otherwise unlabelled checkbox has one bounded nearby visible text candidate, that text is shown as unverified context and the checkbox remains sensitive for manual review. A read-only live check showed the consent text instead of the unrelated name label; no checkbox or other field was touched. A fictional browser fixture and 14 label-related browser tests passed, as did Windows web Clippy. Nearby text is not an accessible label and may not describe the checkbox on every site.

- On the real payroll application, visual asterisks marked name, email, phone, notice period and résumé as required while the native controls had no required attributes. The browser now makes a bounded inference when a visible nearby label uniquely matches an otherwise unassociated placeholder and ends in an asterisk. It marks the field required and flags the inference for manual review; hidden or ambiguous labels are not treated as confirmed requirements, and ambiguous matching markers require review. A read-only live check observed those five inferred requirements; no fields were entered or submitted. A fictional browser fixture covered visible, hidden and ambiguous markers; the first version of that fixture lacked control IDs and was corrected. Thirteen label-related browser tests and Windows web Clippy passed before the final ambiguity refinement; its focused fixture and Clippy passed afterward. This is a limited heuristic, not visual-model verification or universal required-field detection.

- A read-only application check can now retain earlier loading or obstruction warnings when those specific signals clear and the current URL, form schema and all other warnings remain unchanged. It reports that the earlier signals cleared and still requires manual review; new warnings, changed fields, changed text and changed URLs still stop the check. On one real payroll application retest, the worker reported 13 fields and the application URL with the retained loading warnings; no fields were filled or submitted. Fictional signal and changing-form browser tests passed, application-check tests passed (21 passed, 23 ignored), and Windows web Clippy passed. This does not establish form completion or visual-model reliability.

- Application-check summaries now identify a required disabled or read-only field and unavailable fields, and explicitly report when the form did not settle during its bounded loading check. The text uses fixed messages rather than echoing page content. Application-check tests passed (20 passed, 22 ignored) and Windows web Clippy passed; no employer site or model was used.

- The live visibility check now flags required controls hidden by CSS as needing review, rather than silently omitting them. Separate fictional local pages tested `display: none`, `visibility: hidden`, and an optional hidden control; 12 hidden-control browser tests and browser Clippy passed. The first broader run found an outdated no-warning expectation from the previous change; the fixture was updated to expect the warning while still verifying the visible duplicate can be filled. An inactive hidden required field can still need human interpretation, and uninspected frames remain outside this check. No employer page was opened or application submitted.

- Form inspection now reports required input, select or textarea controls hidden by an ancestor with `aria-hidden` or the HTML `hidden` attribute, as it already did for directly hidden controls. Two fictional local-browser tests covered direct and inherited hiding; browser Clippy passed. CSS-only hiding, controls in uninspected frames and the relevance of inactive hidden sections still require review. No employer site was used and no application was submitted.

- Form review now also flags optional numeric suggestions outside observed minimum or maximum bounds, and clears that warning when the answer is corrected. This makes the existing prefill rejection visible earlier; it does not validate dynamic constraints or numeric steps. The form-preview tests passed (28 passed, 46 ignored) and Windows web Clippy passed; no model or employer site was used.

- Required numeric suggestions outside a form's observed minimum or maximum now remain marked for review before filling. A focused typed-answer test covered boundaries, malformed values and invalid bound attributes; Windows web Clippy passed. This early check does not cover step constraints or replace live browser validity checks.

- The fill preflight now rejects out-of-range numeric answers before consuming the prefill grant or writing any field, including optional numeric answers. A fictional local-browser test verified the value remained unchanged and the grant unconsumed; it also retained the changed-schema check. Browser validity checks remain necessary for step and dynamic constraints. No application was submitted.

- Numeric preflight also uses Chromium's detached-control validity check before any fields are written, covering the observed native step constraint. A fictional browser test rejected an in-range step mismatch without changing the field or consuming the grant, and accepted a valid candidate. The form-preview suite passed (28 passed, 45 ignored) and Windows web Clippy passed. A site can change constraints after preflight, so checks during entry remain necessary.

- Text preflight now checks native email, pattern and length constraints before entering any field. A fictional browser test rejected an invalid email and a pattern mismatch without changing an earlier field or consuming the grant; the form-preview suite passed (28 passed, 46 ignored) and Windows web Clippy passed. One older ignored browser fixture test failed before reaching preflight because its expected initial suggestion no longer matches the current fixture. Dynamic and custom validation still require later checks.

- The older fictional browser test has since been updated for the current model response format, explicit replacement review, preflight behavior and distinct generated selectors. It passed and exercised a valid text fill plus later invalid-input and changed-page handling; no employer site or application submission was involved.

- Date, month, time and local date-time preflight now checks a detached native input against its current browser constraints before any form field is written. A fictional browser test covered a date outside the allowed range and a time-step mismatch without consuming the grant; a separate valid-time fill test and Windows web Clippy passed. Constraints can still change after the check.

- Custom ARIA radio options that reference their group's question before their own label now expose the option text separately in inventory, selection lookup and the final click guard. A fictional browser fixture verified selection and refusal of duplicate options; five custom-radio browser regressions passed, and browser Clippy passed. A read-only inspection of the ENFOS Workable form reported four groups with `YES`/`NO` options, but the overall inventory was incomplete and the page was obstructed, so manual review remained required. The test passed, but this is not confirmation that the application can be completed. No candidate data was entered and no application was submitted.

- A fictional browser test now changes a filled answer inside an injected capture callback. The shared post-capture check rejected navigation, kept the browser on the original page and retained a capture warning. This simulates a change during capture; it does not reproduce a third-party site's timing.

- Reviewed-link navigation now rechecks the page schema, filled values, current attachments and selected link after the potentially lengthy screenshot capture, immediately before opening the destination. These checks narrow the timing window but cannot eliminate later page changes.

- The fictional navigation-worker test also restored the staged file and then cleared the browser's file input. The worker stopped for review and remained on the original page, confirming that an intact staged file alone does not pass the attachment check.

A changed or unverified attachment stops navigation for review. A fictional worker test changed the staged file and confirmed that the browser stayed on the original page. This check does not establish attachment retention or cover manual navigation. A fictional browser fixture verified the check detects a changed value, and the form-link tests (15 passed, 5 ignored) and Windows web Clippy passed. No real application was contacted or submitted.

## Navigation advice coverage — 2026-11-03

- Hidden unlabelled or overlong button labels no longer alone mark the visible button inventory incomplete; visible unlabelled controls still do. The 30-control limit now reports incomplete when an additional visible control is observed, rather than merely because hidden candidates remain. A fictional browser fixture tested hidden and visible unlabelled buttons and the 30/31-visible boundaries; browser Clippy passed. The existing time and DOM-candidate limits still produce incomplete results. No buttons were clicked.

- Button inventory now scans up to 120 DOM candidates to find up to 30 visible controls, instead of letting 30 CSS-hidden candidates exhaust the scan. It retains the four-second visibility budget and reports incomplete when limits are hit. A fictional browser fixture found visible controls after 35 hidden buttons and separately confirmed the 30-visible-control cap; browser Clippy passed. Larger pages can still have omitted controls, and no button was clicked.

- Control advice can now make up to three sequential remaining-control passes when the preceding response reaches its five-item limit, instead of one. A separate overlay-related pass may also occur. The cap or a failed/invalid pass marks advice incomplete; the interface explains that outcome. Scripted tests covered 17 returned controls and the 20-control bound, the focused UI check and Windows web Clippy passed. Extra passes can increase model latency; no live model or employer page was used for this change, and navigation advice does not click or authorize controls.

- Follow-up control advice now supplies only the remaining controls in its prompt, retaining their original indices instead of repeating the full list alongside them. Four focused scripted tests and the fictional resource-gated `qwen3.5:9b` test passed; the unscripted case again selected six navigation controls and omitted the submission control. This is not real-site qualification.

- In the same fictional seven-control scenario, an unscripted `qwen3.5:9b` run selected all six navigation controls and omitted the submission control. Its first pass reached the limit and the bounded follow-up completed without a reported error. This is one scenario, not evidence of general form-navigation accuracy.

- In one resource-gated fictional control test, `qwen3.5:9b` selected the remaining navigation control and omitted the submission control on the follow-up pass. The test injected the first five selections to force that pass; it did not measure the model's first-pass selection or real-site behavior. The model unloaded afterward. One concurrent system-memory snapshot showed 16,614,596 KiB available, not a measured minimum.

- If a control-ranking response reaches its five-item limit, the advisor now makes at most one extra pass over controls not already suggested. Results retain their original page indices; invalid or repeated suggestions from that pass leave the earlier advice intact and flag the additional check as incomplete. The existing cap warning remains because the extra pass can also miss controls. Scripted model tests and the UI fixture passed; no live model or employer page was used.

- The interface now warns when either local-model control-ranking pass returns its five-item limit, since additional relevant controls may have been omitted. The warning also appears if a submission-labeled item is removed afterward. Scripted model and UI tests passed; these do not establish navigation accuracy or exercise a live model. No application was submitted.

- New AI duplicate candidates now include a deterministic comparison of the saved title, employer, location and requisition text at assessment time, ignoring case and whitespace and reporting missing fields as unavailable. The comparison is evidence for review only: wording can differ for the same role, matching text does not establish identity, and the saved listings may later change. Existing duplicate records are not rewritten. The fictional duplicate-search suite (11 passed, 8 ignored) and Windows web Clippy passed; no application was submitted.
- In a fictional three-candidate comparison without requisition IDs, `qwen3.5:9b` flagged the similar Berlin research role and omitted a different role and Munich role, but incorrectly described the different titles as identical. The duplicate-search prompt now asks the model to distinguish similar wording from identical wording, and newly saved AI duplicate reasons explicitly warn that explanations can be inaccurate. On one repeat the same candidates were selected, but the explanation still contained contradictory wording about the titles; the prompt change does not solve this model limitation. Users must compare both complete listings. The live regression, duplicate-search tests (11 passed, 8 ignored) and Windows web Clippy passed; the test-owned model was unloaded. No application was submitted.

The context tests passed (7 passed, 8 ignored) and Windows web Clippy passed. No live-model comparison was run, so any effect on mapping quality remains unmeasured.

- When no eligible entry-stage experience exists for a control type, form mapping can now include reviewed review-stage observations for that control as advisory fallback. Entry-stage observations remain preferred, and unsupported, disabled or undated records are excluded. Selection and context tests plus Windows web Clippy passed. This verifies wiring, not model accuracy.

This does not prove that the local model used the hints correctly.

- The form-mapping model wrapper now independently excludes disabled, unreviewed, unsupported-version and undated experiences before applying its ten-item limit. Fictional context tests passed (5 passed, 7 ignored), as did Windows web Clippy. No local model or application was used in this check.

If those optional lessons would exceed the existing 8,000-byte request limit, it sends the current-page prompt without lessons and does not report the lessons as used. Focused experience and suggestion tests and Windows web Clippy passed. This does not measure whether lessons improve model accuracy; live model and employer-site tests were not run for this change.

## PDF template editing — 2026-11-03

- PDF extraction, single-page rendering, editing and targeted transcription now accept documents up to 50 pages, with the existing 10 MiB input, 200 KB extracted text, 30-second worker and 512 MiB Windows job bounds. Automated whole-document visual review remains capped at 30 pages and reports longer reviews as incomplete. A separate public 34-page CV initially hit the old limit; after the change it extracted, rendered its first and last pages, and accepted one short direct edit whose local crop was visually checked. Fictional 50/51-page boundary tests, visual-review and transcription/drafting tests, and Windows web Clippy passed. This is not a guarantee for all long documents; large or complex edits can still time out or require manual review. No CV content was committed.

- Direct PDF edits can now extend a single text object rightward into a bounded region that was completely white in the original rendering; changes elsewhere remain subject to text, glyph and page-image checks. Fictional tests covered expansion and refusal when another object occupies the extension. In one public CV, longer heading edits initially passed extraction checks but visual inspection exposed joined words in one and nearly invisible punctuation in another. Word-gap and minimum-glyph-size checks now refuse those cases for manual review; these conservative geometric heuristics do not establish general visual fidelity. The earlier short edit still succeeded. PDF selection and font-map tests and Windows web Clippy passed. No CV content was committed.

- The affected public CV's font maps a space to a code outside its explicit width table. Replacements containing spaces now undergo the word-gap check even when the source has the same number of spaces. Fictional PDF tests passed, including a forced spacing-check failure. At that point, automatic repair for missing subset-font spacing was not implemented.

- For a subset Type 1 font with a missing space width, the editor can now use repeated, uniquely corroborated spacing from the same page and font to create a private font resource for the edited text operation. It leaves the original font resource in place and retains the existing text, glyph and rendered-page checks. On the public CV above, the previously joined heading was edited with visible spacing; a local before/after crop was inspected. Fictional tests covered resource isolation, restoration, missing evidence, conflicting gaps and repeated ambiguous boundaries; PDF tests and Windows web Clippy passed. This is one successful document and a constrained heuristic, not general font or layout support.

- A separate public professional CV was tested locally without adding its contents to Git. Extraction and first-page rendering succeeded for a ten-page digital PDF; no pages were reported as lacking selectable text. A unique section heading was shortened through the direct PDF editor, and a local before/after crop showed the replacement in the same position. The original remained unchanged. An initial attempt using a heading absent from this CV correctly requested manual review. This is one document and one short edit, not proof of general PDF or visual fidelity. No model was loaded and no application was submitted.

- The PDF stream editor also handles the two shorthand text-show operators, preserving their spacing operands. Fictional font-map checks exercised both operators directly, and PDFium selection tests passed through the full document, glyph, geometry and visual checks. Windows web Clippy passed. Real-document coverage and arbitrary font support remain unverified.

- Adjacent-object PDF edits can now attempt a longer replacement when narrower glyphs fit inside the original text areas. Existing whole-document text, glyph, geometry, and rendered-difference checks remain required. Fictional PDF selection and font-mapping tests and Windows web Clippy passed. A first fixture with two identically positioned wide text objects was refused because PDF text changed unexpectedly; a second, narrower final-object replacement passed. This is not proof of general PDF fidelity or support for every font. No real CV or application was used.

## Duplicate search — 2026-11-03

- Duplicate-search coverage now detects sampled source text by content rather than byte length. Overlapping samples with multilingual characters can be longer in bytes than the original despite omitted content. Fictional duplicate-search tests passed (12 passed, 8 ignored), including this case; Windows web Clippy passed. This improves coverage reporting, not duplicate-matching accuracy. No live model or employer site was used.

## Form review — 2026-11-03

- Required-field review now retains invalid or incorrectly typed proposed answers, including invalid dates, out-of-range text, and unavailable or blank multi-select choices. It is still a review of proposed values, not a live browser validity check. Form preview tests passed (27 passed, 42 ignored), as did Windows web Clippy; an initial test compile failed due to a missing test import and passed after correction. No browser or live model was run.

## Document context feedback — 2026-11-02

- Form-bound approvals now use a versioned, structured schema encoding rather than separator-joined field text. This distinguishes selectors and labels containing separators, and invalidates old form-bound approvals so they must be reviewed again. Form mapping tests passed (10), web form preview tests passed (26; 42 ignored), and Windows web Clippy passed. Browser and real-site behavior were not tested in this change.

- Profile mapping now reports required non-profile controls, including files, checkboxes, dates, and multi-selects, for separate review when no suggestion is present. This notice is not a claim about what the website has already filled. Fictional suggestion tests passed (48 passed, 20 ignored) and Windows web Clippy passed; no live browser or model test was run.

- Duration evidence checks also recognize hyphenated quantity-unit adjectives such as “three-year” and “3-year”, including compound number words. Focused fictional document and answer tests and Windows web Clippy passed; live model tests were not run. This remains a limited numeric guard, not a semantic proof of experience.

- The shared document and answer evidence guard now recognizes common English number words in durations and compares their units, including simple digit/word equivalents. Compound or hyphenated number phrases are compared conservatively as phrases; a mismatch downgrades a model-supported claim to uncertain. This is not semantic verification, does not cover every language or numeric expression, and equivalent compound spellings may require review. Focused fictional document and answer tests and Windows web Clippy passed; live model tests were not run.

- Before DOCX-to-PDF conversion, revision inspection now checks bounded XML parts across the package and linked note parts even when their names or extensions are nonstandard. Unresolved referenced notes prevent conversion instead of silently skipping inspection. The check remains bounded to 128 parts and 8 MiB of XML and cannot guarantee coverage of every possible non-XML Wordprocessing part. A fictional custom-named note with a deleted revision was rejected; related unit tests passed (6 passed, 2 ignored), and an existing fictional styled DOCX converted using the installed LibreOffice. One initial conversion-test invocation failed because its fixture environment variables were absent; the subsequent styled-fixture check passed. Windows web Clippy passed. Clean-host installation remains unverified.
- DOCX text review extracts referenced notes from parts identified by the main document's internal relationships, including nonstandard part names, ignoring removed references and unreferenced notes. Missing or ambiguous relationships, unsupported target encodings, duplicate IDs, special entries and nested references are unresolved. Resolved notes are appended once, not at their original reading positions; users should verify the original. This does not guarantee full DOCX fidelity. Each note category is limited to 128 references. Microsoft's [part relationship guidance](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/previous-versions/windows/desktop/opc/parts-overview) was captured with Crawl4AI (native receipt SHA-256 `8c8307689b0ad48eb22793004589f6bc27ec58e74d672d7b28ed00d7d7a7041c`). Synthetic tests cover linked nonstandard names, duplicate and external relationships, note extraction and unresolved references. No real application was submitted.
- Model-generated cover-letter openings and closings now receive a separate bounded local-model neutrality check, displayed alongside the paragraph evidence review. Invalid or unavailable checks are shown explicitly; a `neutral` result is advisory, not proof, and does not check later edits. In one resource-gated Qwen 3.5 9B run, the French greeting and closing were classified neutral and a separate fictional 25-year experience claim was marked for review. Focused draft and UI tests and Windows web Clippy passed; the model was unloaded. This small evaluation does not establish general factual accuracy.
- A fictional Qwen 3.5 9B test placed a French-language instruction beyond the former 500-byte cutoff. The body was French, but the application added an English deterministic opening and closing. Cover-letter drafting with writing instructions now requests neutral framing in the requested style during its revision pass; if either bounded single-line field is missing or invalid, both are left empty with a review notice. A later resource-gated run returned a French body, greeting and closing. Model-generated framing is not evidence-checked and must be reviewed; one example does not establish language compliance generally. Draft tests passed (18 passed, nine ignored), the live test and Windows web Clippy passed, and the model was unloaded.
- The longer-instruction budget now uses unused job-description and template capacity before reducing the space for qualifications. A fictional short-job case retained all three prioritized facts with longer instructions; a full-budget case still reported an omitted fact and refused to drop prioritized facts. The draft suite passed (17 passed, nine ignored) and Windows web Clippy passed. This remains a bounded byte-budget heuristic; model compliance and all possible inputs are not established.
- CV and cover-letter drafting now sends up to 1,500 JSON-encoded bytes of writing instructions instead of 500. It stays within the existing prompt budget by reducing the qualification budget when longer instructions are supplied; omitted qualifications remain reported, and the draft refuses to omit user-prioritized qualifications. Fictional prompt tests verified that instructions beyond the old limit reach both drafting passes and checked the tradeoff. A resource-gated Qwen 3.5 9B run produced a fictional draft with longer instructions; it did not assess whether the model followed them semantically. The draft suite passed (17 passed, eight ignored before the additional live test), the selected live test passed, and Windows web Clippy passed. Longer inputs can still be truncated and require review; the model was unloaded.
- Draft selection feedback now identifies whether job details, writing instructions, or template text were shortened, instead of combining them into an ambiguous warning. Fictional UI checks cover individual and combined omissions; the JavaScript syntax check passed. This does not increase the model's context or recover omitted material.

## Public application mapping — 2026-11-02

- A stable uninspected-frame warning is already treated as a review obligation and does not by itself suppress suggestions for inspected fields. The earlier variation therefore cannot be attributed to that warning alone. When a blocking inspection warning prevents mapping, the preview now explicitly says that suggestions were not attempted; the live evaluation uses that signal instead of inferring the cause from a frame warning. A subsequent read-only Qwen 3.5 9B run returned four correct fictional-profile suggestions and the same CAPTCHA, frame and custom-control warnings. Preview and worker tests and Windows web Clippy passed. No fields were entered or submitted, and the model was unloaded.
- A read-only background-worker check on the previously captured public DoorDash Greenhouse application returned eight standard fields and warnings for a CAPTCHA, an embedded frame, hidden and custom controls. One run returned no suggestions; a later run produced four suggestions for the fictional first name, last name, email and phone, all matching the supplied fictional values. The initial evaluation incorrectly required suggestions even when no suggestions were returned alongside a frame warning; a subsequent incorrect assertion assumed the warning always meant no mapping had run. The corrected evaluation accepts either outcome, validates every returned suggestion, and passed on the four-suggestion run. No fields were entered and no application was submitted. Dynamic timing and uninspected controls remain limitations; this does not establish that the application can be completed. The test-owned model was unloaded and Windows web Clippy passed.

## Form loading observations — 2026-11-02

- Browser inspection now requires two consecutive unchanged nonempty form snapshots before considering its bounded loading check settled. If the check ends with a nonempty form that has not settled, it marks the page for manual review. This adds approximately 750 ms to the prior first-stable path and still cannot detect fields that appear after the bounded check. A real local browser detected a fictional field added 800 ms after page load; two existing staged-loading browser checks also passed. Browser library tests passed (21 passed, 3 ignored) and browser Clippy passed. No external application was opened or submitted in this check.

## Local inference memory checks — 2026-11-02

- A resource-gated automatic start of the installed `ministral-3:14b` model succeeded with a 4,096-token context. Ollama reported 8.6 GB and 100% GPU residency, so this did not test automatic CPU offload. Available system memory was sampled at approximately 18.51 GiB before startup, 10.55 GiB after loading, and 18.94 GiB after the test model was unloaded. These are point-in-time readings, not peak usage measurements; no inference quality or sustained-memory behavior was tested.
- The model-start result now displays the reported GPU allocation and estimated model offload immediately after startup or reuse, without requiring a separate status check. Fictional backend and interface checks cover both values; model-start tests and Windows web Clippy passed. The numbers come from Ollama's report and do not measure total system RAM consumed by the runtime.
- Waypoint now accepts one local model reporting a nonzero GPU allocation of at most 16 GiB and at most 2 GiB difference between total and GPU allocation. The startup estimate allows for that offload, while retaining the file-size-plus-10-GiB system-memory preflight and in-flight available-memory guard. The UI labels offload as an estimate derived from Ollama's report, not total system-memory use. In one resource-gated test, Qwen 3.5 9B reported 4,643,901,929 GPU bytes and 866,186,688 offloaded bytes and completed a guarded fictional inference; the test-owned model was unloaded. Boundary and UI tests, the web suite (621 passed, 342 ignored) and Windows Clippy passed. A larger model and automatic startup into a split allocation were not tested. Allocation estimates and snapshots cannot prevent every resource spike; a rejected model can remain loaded and must be checked before retrying.
- Automatic startup now accepts installed local GGUF files up to 16 GiB for resource estimation. The earlier estimate used file size plus 20% and 0.5 GiB of GPU memory; the latest change above allows an estimated 2 GiB offload. System RAM must still meet file size plus 10 GiB at the startup snapshot. Boundary, model-start interface and Windows web Clippy checks passed. The estimates do not guarantee the actual model fits or that loading stops under pressure.
- Waypoint's resident-model checks previously expanded from 14 GiB to 16 GiB of reported fully GPU-resident allocation; the later change above adds bounded offload. Ollama's [FAQ](https://docs.ollama.com/faq#how-can-i-tell-if-my-model-was-loaded-onto-the-gpu) describes fully and partially GPU-resident `ollama ps` reports; the isolated Crawl4AI receipt is `ollama-faq-e736c3fa1eb045d1a68a5358b4ee2cba.json` outside Git (SHA-256 `930d44d485951bffc7f6c6c17c83898dff817bb0c6d5a3cc12a0e70ce963af7c`).
- Model startup now uses the same periodic available-memory guard during its load request. A local mock verified that simulated pressure closes the request and reports that the model may still be loading or resident; a resource-gated Qwen 3.5 9B startup then completed normally and the test-owned model was unloaded. Five startup tests passed (three live tests ignored in the ordinary suite), the selected live startup passed, and Windows web Clippy passed. Closing a request does not prove Ollama immediately stops loading; there is no hard RAM reservation.
- Guarded local inference now rechecks its cancellation and memory guard while writing a request as well as while waiting for the response. A fictional partial-write and timeout test confirms that the caller stops writing when the guard reports pressure; all inference library tests passed (19 passed, 2 ignored), and its Clippy check passed. Closing the request is not a guarantee that Ollama immediately stops server-side work or unloads its model, and memory checks remain snapshots rather than reservations. Startup VRAM and RAM limits were not changed.
