## Unicode location duplicate review — 2026-11-02

- A disk-backed SQLCipher fixture now also checks that an injected index write failure rolls back the Unicode recheck cursor, that reopening the database resumes at the next record, and that a prior `different` decision survives. The first test assertion incorrectly used an API that intentionally excludes `different` pairs; checking the stored decision directly passed. This is a small fictional fixture, not a large-database performance measurement.

- Duplicate candidate comparison now recognizes an explicit location suffix when Unicode lowercase matches despite case differences, such as `ZÜRICH` and `Zürich`. A new migration schedules existing jobs for a resumable recheck in pages of at most 64 records; candidates remain pending for user review and are never merged automatically. This is Unicode lowercase, not locale-specific case folding or transliteration: a Turkish dotted-I counterexample remains unmatched. Fictional migration, duplicate-hold and bounded-page tests passed, as did the encrypted store suite (100 passed, 4 ignored before the additional bounded-page test), SQLCipher store Clippy and a web profile-restart test. No large-database timing or memory benchmark was run.

## Local model identity check — 2026-11-02

- A draft task now snapshots the verified resident model identity before template processing and binds both scanned-template visual transcription and structured drafting to it. A replacement with the same model name but a different digest is rejected before either guarded phase. Fictional prebound-identity tests passed for both guards; the drafting unit group passed (16 passed, 8 ignored) and Windows lint passed. The complete visual-to-text path was not run with a live model; approximately 14.86 GiB of system RAM was available at the initial snapshot, below the conservative startup estimate for the installed 6.15 GiB model. Checks cannot exclude transient changes that revert between observations.
- Production visual requests now compare the validated resident model name and digest before and after each request; a changed identity discards the output. Multi-page PDF visual review also binds the digest across its pages. Fictional guard tests covered replacement during a request and between pages; the visual-review and document-review unit groups and Windows lint passed. These are snapshots, not a guarantee against a transient change that is reverted; other multi-request visual workflows are not yet bound across their entire task. No live model was loaded or tested for this change.
- Guarded structured-text workflows now bind the resident model name and digest on the first request, recheck both before and after each model call, and discard a response if the identity changes. Fictional tests covered a same-name digest replacement between calls and during a call. The single-threaded Windows web suite passed (605 passed, 329 ignored), and Windows lint passed. The checks are snapshots and cannot detect every transient replacement; visual inference uses a separate path and is not covered by this new per-workflow digest binding. No live model was loaded or evaluated for this change.
- Before accepting a resident model for guarded inference, Waypoint now compares its reported digest and name with a unique local GGUF entry in Ollama's model inventory and rejects explicit remote-model or remote-host markers. Startup checks the same markers before requesting a load and checks the binding again after loading. Missing, malformed or mismatched identity is refused. This is a point-in-time check; a runtime or model configuration can change after it, and it is not proof against a compromised runtime or every possible forwarding mechanism.
- Ollama's source API types at blob `1aa71342a372fc2a0902cc55f43afe91c3a55d06` distinguish remote markers in list entries from the resident-process response; the native GitHub API capture is retained at `target/ollama-api-types-research.json`. Fictional HTTP and inventory tests covered remote aliases, mismatched digests, duplicate entries and changing residency; the model-start tests passed (4 passed, 3 ignored), the capability tests passed (2), and Windows lint passed. The local installed Qwen inventory was inspected, but no model was loaded: the available-memory snapshot was near the conservative startup threshold. Real resident-model compatibility and any time-of-check race remain unverified.

## Optional local web-search connector — 2026-11-02

- The preceding connector is now disabled until at least one underlying engine is explicitly allowed for discovery in the source permission registry. Previously fast mode left engine choice to the provider; a local OpenSERP `v0.8.17` process reported Google, Yandex, Baidu and Ecosia as available. Google’s [published policy](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/essentials/spam-policies) restricts automated Google searches without express permission. Future enabled requests explicitly name registry-approved engines and reject organic results attributed to another or unidentified engine. No such engine is currently approved, so users should use an existing permitted provider. Provider configuration and truthful provenance cannot be guaranteed by this check.
- The Windows `v0.8.17` release archive matched GitHub's published SHA-256 `5c03543863c00fc16e4285380bb6b066b1d130d8624cd06285fe52f711ddc21d`. An isolated, test-owned server ran with raw HTTP, retries disabled and no proxy; its local engine inventory and empty-query validation were checked, then it was stopped. No external search query was sent. The Google policy page was captured with Crawl4AI `0.9.5+flitzzip.portable2`, HTTP 200, receipt `google-policy-300504616c59431fbc4db9b96e75a44f.json`, SHA-256 `a336bebea323dca307880cca629287c5cf7346af195baa00fb1dd8c2d014af85`. A fictional policy fixture checked approved-engine request construction, failure for no approvals, and refusal of unexpected engine results; web-search tests passed (15 passed, 2 ignored), interface check and Windows lint passed. Real provider search responses remain unverified.
- The initial connector supported querying an already-running local OpenSERP service at `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/mega/`; the permission gate above now prevents its use without approved engines. Requests use fast mode, a ten-result page and bounded sequential Waypoint batches. The provider manages its own engines and browser; Waypoint does not install it, enforce its resource use or configure its proxies. Only typed organic results become unverified candidates; omitted non-organic or untyped results and reported engine failures are surfaced. Results are not verified job offers.
- A fictional loopback HTTP fixture checked request shape, pagination, organic filtering, failure reporting and result provenance; web-search tests passed (15 passed, 2 ignored), batch tests passed (19 passed, 2 ignored), and the coverage interface check passed. No actual OpenSERP installation or live search was tested. A bounded Crawl4AI capture of [the provider documentation](https://openserp.org/docs/) returned HTTP 200 with the expected pagination marker; provider `0.9.5+flitzzip.portable2`, receipt `openserp-96c30427ac844f3b86079242c1707559.json`, SHA-256 `3f9e5ed58c50c9c39552da3e752c3ac9b90f3fee4b20326719ba4578b2de4db2`, is retained in the local Waypoint research directory. The documentation describes the self-hosted software as MIT licensed; no code was copied. No employer was contacted or application submitted.

## Partial network observation during filling — 2026-11-02

- The shared reviewed-prefill function now also requires an initial network-counter observation before it consumes the approval or writes a field. An unavailable counter is no longer treated as permission to skip subsequent observations. A fictional driver test covered the unavailable baseline, unchanged approval, and the subsequent available-counter path; the form-preview unit group passed (25 passed, 40 ignored). The production CDP driver already supplies a counter and the web route already checked it separately. This observation is partial: it does not block network requests or prove that data was not sent through another route. No employer page was opened or application submitted for this change.
- Reviewed PDF attachment now samples the same partial browser network counter around the upload action. An observed non-GET/HEAD request, or an unavailable post-action observation, is recorded for manual review and reported even if attachment fails. If the initial observation fails, Waypoint stops before issuing the file-attachment command. Uploads can legitimately cause requests; the observation does not establish purpose or delivery, block a request or cover every transport.
- Two fictional loopback browser/API fixtures triggered a POST during attachment: one retained the file, while the other cleared it and returned an attachment error. Both recorded and reported the observed request; only the loopback server received it. Windows lint passed. A failure of the network preflight itself was not exercised end to end.
- The screenshot viewer now associates page segments with their individual capture outcome: changed, incomplete, limited coverage, recorded complete or unknown. A later successful retry does not relabel segments from an earlier changed capture. These are historical event records, not proof of current page state or full content coverage. A disk-backed archive test and interface checks covered the outcome labels and inconsistent events; event history remains bounded and can be partial.
- Page capture now makes one fresh attempt if the first tile changes, recording distinct capture IDs and preserving the first attempt and its change warning. A continuously changing fictional page produced two incomplete captures and no completion event; the broader reviewed-fill fixture passed once after the change, but that does not establish the cause of its previous failures or prove that the retry was exercised there. Matching images still cannot prove the entire page remained unchanged.
- Text entry now checks already received network events between key or text-segment operations and can stop within a field when it sees a non-GET/HEAD request. A fictional loopback page using synchronous input-triggered POST retained only a prefix of the first answer, left the next field empty, and received the POST; the failing field was not counted as completed. The independent browser request and keyboard fixtures passed. Observation remains partial and late events, other targets, GET writes and other transports can escape it; this does not block a request already sent or guarantee no submission.
- The broader reviewed-fill fixture failed twice after filling at a screenshot-segment assertion: capture reported a changed page image and retained an incomplete-capture event. The cause was not established; no full screenshot-capture success is claimed for these runs.
- If the initial browser network counter cannot be sampled, the reviewed-fill route now stops before issuing field commands, records a review event when possible and keeps the session open. The interface pauses filling and asks the user to check the employer site. The interface failure path and a normal fictional POST fixture passed; an end-to-end browser disconnection at this exact preflight point has not been reproduced. The site can still have sent data independently before this check.
- The form-preview suite exposed a warning that remained after an invalid suggestion was corrected because an added profile hint changed its text. Warning refresh now recognizes that exact suffix while retaining the warning until all same-label fields are answered. The suite then passed 25 tests with 40 ignored.
- Reviewed filling now samples the partial network counter after each successfully executed and checked field. If the count rises or the counter becomes unavailable, it stops before the next field and retains the completed-field record for review. A two-field fictional loopback fixture sent a POST on the first input; that POST reached the fixture, the second field remained empty and the attempt was flagged for review. A separate normal-fill fixture and Windows lint passed. The counter can miss delayed events and other transports, and this stop occurs after the observed request: it cannot prevent a submission or guarantee that the next field is never filled after an unobserved write.
- When either network counter snapshot is unavailable or inconsistent, the fill response now reports that uncertainty, records a manual-review event and pauses automatic visual inspection. A counter classification test and partial-fill interface test cover the unavailable state; the fictional browser POST regression also passed. This still cannot detect all requests or prevent submission, and a missing observed request is not evidence that no data was sent.
- During a reviewed fill the browser counts observed HTTP requests with methods other than GET or HEAD. If the count rises, the attempt gets a manual-review warning and event, and automatic visual inspection pauses. The observation stores no request URL, headers or body; it can include unrelated analytics or preflight requests. It is a warning after filling, not a block, and does not establish a request's purpose or delivery. Missing or late events, other browser targets, GET-triggered changes and WebSocket messages can go undetected. A zero count is not evidence that nothing was sent. This does not provide a no-submission guarantee.
- A fictional loopback page sent a POST on field input; a real browser and the reviewed-fill endpoint observed it, recorded the warning and paused automatic visual review in a separate interface test. The local fixture received that POST. No employer site was contacted or application submitted. The browser and endpoint checks are bounded examples, not proof of comprehensive network observation.
- Research: [Chromium network settings](https://www.chromium.org/developers/design-documents/network-settings/) documents proxy configuration, but does not establish a universal no-send barrier. The isolated Crawl4AI provider returned HTTP 200 with a matching content marker; native receipt `chromium-proxy-5f5b69622940407bac33a9c2445a223f.json`, SHA-256 `b57f7443f650cf898ed1bbcac0dcd9c9a3b11f2b2edfd0a8b222877d1fc28730`, is retained under the local Waypoint research directory. An alternate official source returned a challenge/HTTP 503, retained as `chromium-proxy-85975c03ad61410999131f9c05f53290.json`, SHA-256 `8576abea039414c950f0bb69d86027cd57015c8fa524f97b1cd336a4795ee50d`. An initial attempt with system Python failed because Crawl4AI was not installed there; the isolated provider subsequently passed its diagnostic.

## Later-section form reinspection — 2026-11-02

- A fictional two-step local-browser test now exercises deterministic suggestions and a separate reviewed-prefill grant for each step. After a test navigation click, reusing the first step's plan was refused; the second step was reinspected, its email was filled, the sensitive work-authorization field remained empty, and a final-submit click was refused. This is one fixture without a local model or the web approval gesture; it does not establish real-site or visual-model reliability. No application was submitted.
- When earlier-section readback finds a changed or unverified answer or attachment, the review message now identifies the first affected field's position in the current field list without displaying its value or selector; later items may remain unchecked. Successful answer checks are explicitly labelled browser readback, not visual inspection. A fictional two-step browser test covered a changed answer and cleared attachment; the navigation suite passed (14 passed, 5 ignored) and Windows lint passed. This does not establish section identity, field correctness, or delivery.
- Earlier attachment fields can now be included in those session snapshots, even if no text was filled. On return to a uniquely matching form schema, the app checks up to eight attachment fields against the staged files and reports how many matched; it retains the manual-review warning. The browser file check compares names, sizes and hashes, while a separate check compares staged bytes with their recorded hashes. This is not proof of section identity or delivery. Checks stop between fields after four seconds, but an individual file read or browser call can take longer.
- A fictional loopback wizard retained an attachment, detected a modified staged file and detected a cleared file input; it also covered an attachment-only snapshot. The initial two fixture attempts used staged files under the repository target directory and failed browser file verification; a separate existing browser attachment test passed. Using the app's normal temporary staging location allowed the fixture to pass, but the precise cause of those earlier failures was not established. The navigation suite passed (14 passed, 5 ignored) and Windows lint passed. No employer site was contacted or application submitted.
- While a review session remains open, Waypoint retains up to four bounded snapshots of its previously filled sections in memory. On a later inspection with one matching form schema, it compares the previously filled values and reports matched or unverified fields; it warns if the profile changed. Matching a schema does not prove section identity or continuing correctness, so the existing manual-review obligation remains. Multiple matching snapshots are treated as ambiguous and omitted snapshots are reported. Closing the session loses these in-memory comparisons.
- The fictional background-worker wizard returned to an earlier section, detected a retained answer and then a changed answer, and exercised profile-change, duplicate-layout and four-snapshot limits. The navigation suite passed (14 passed, 5 ignored) and Windows lint passed. This used a local fixture and offline mapping, not an employer site, full web UI or local-model evaluation. No application was submitted.
- A fictional two-section browser test now runs the background navigation worker after a simulated manual transition. It verifies the prior value after returning, surfaces the new section and its AI-use notice, leaves the email and sensitive field unfilled, and records a prior-fields review obligation. The test injects a local-file-only destination checker; the production entry point still uses the public HTTPS policy. It holds the model lease to exercise the offline fallback and does not test real-site navigation, the web endpoint or a local model.
- That test exposed a missing offline suggestion for a native email field labelled “Email address” without an autocomplete hint. The fallback now recognizes that unambiguous applicant label and continues to exclude reference contacts. An initial broader label change broke four existing ambiguity expectations and was narrowed. The fictional wizard then passed, the suggestion suite passed (44 passed, 19 ignored), the navigation suite passed (14 passed, 5 ignored), and Windows lint passed. No application was submitted.
- Reviewed-link navigation and manual rescans now recheck the live form after model mapping, using the initial-inspection merge logic. If the schema changes, stale suggestions are discarded and the new form requires review; a changed destination stops the inspection. The new section's bounded page text is also checked for possible instructions against AI assistance, with truncated or unavailable text reported. These checks do not prove that a page is stable or that the instruction detector covers every wording.
- A fictional browser test covered unchanged and changed fields, a newly detected challenge and page instructions. Its first run compared the browser's normalized file URL with the fixture's unnormalized URL and failed; using the observed browser URL fixed the test. The focused navigation suite passed (14 passed, 4 ignored) and Windows lint passed. This exercised the recheck directly, not the complete web flow or an employer site; no application was submitted.

## Attempt listing comparison — 2026-11-02

- Version-2 listing-capture checks now compare extracted text and structured-source entries against the captured metadata as well as checking the listed file fingerprints. Missing manifest entries or content inconsistent with that metadata report a mismatch. Fictional omission and changed-content tests and the attempt-record group passed (27 passed, 6 ignored); Windows web Clippy passed. The metadata and manifest remain unsigned and may be changed together, so this is a consistency check, not authentication.

- New attempt listing captures now fingerprint the generated viewer and any extracted page text and structured-source files, in addition to metadata and images. Older manifests still verify their metadata and images but report a partial check in the interface. The manifest remains unsigned: a match covers only listed files and does not establish authenticity or protect against coordinated changes. Fictional original/follow-up archive, changed-file and older-format tests passed (27 attempt-record tests passed, 6 ignored); the focused interface check and Windows web Clippy passed. No existing attempt files were rewritten.

- Attempt history now compares each readable original listing snapshot with the current saved workspace listing, including its description, title, employer, location, source, URL and requisition identifier. Changed or unavailable snapshots are shown for review; a match does not establish that the live vacancy is unchanged or authenticate the plaintext record. Reads are bounded and older, missing, inconsistent or oversized snapshots can report unavailable. The original files are not rewritten.
- A fictional disk-backed test changed the saved job title and separately checked a changed description, missing description and inconsistent snapshot fingerprint; the original attempt text remained unchanged. The focused test, UI check and Windows lint passed. No employer site was contacted or application submitted.

## Application-check freshness — 2026-11-02

- The final page check now also compares a fingerprint of up to 32,000 bytes of sampled DOM text taken after navigation and before the result is assembled. A change or an unavailable text check requires manual reinspection and discards the earlier summary and contacts; a truncated sample is disclosed. The sample can change for harmless dynamic content, can include non-visible DOM text, and cannot detect changes outside its bound or before its first observation. It does not prove that a vacancy is open.
- A fictional browser fixture changed an open/closed message while leaving the URL and form schema unchanged; the text fingerprint detected the change. The existing disappearing-field worker fixture and Windows lint also passed. The new test exercised the browser checks directly rather than an entire employer-site flow; no application was submitted.
- Before returning an application-page check, the browser now rechecks the current URL, form schema and review signals against the inspected page. If these change, the earlier summary and contact inventory are discarded and the result asks for another inspection. This can flag benign dynamic changes and does not detect every same-schema text or content change, nor establish that a vacancy is open.
- A fictional browser fixture confirmed detection of added fields and a challenge at the same URL. The first run of an existing loading-page test exposed loss of its specific disappearing-field warning; the warning was restored, and both browser fixtures passed on rerun. The focused application-check suite passed (19 passed, 21 ignored) before that warning refinement, and Windows lint passed afterward. No employer page was opened or application submitted.

## Numeric preference evidence — 2026-11-02

- A numeric time minimum now remains unknown when a uniquely located, digit-leading citation omits an immediately preceding English upper-bound cue on the same line, such as citing “35 days” from “up to 35 days.” Scripted comparison and focused context checks passed. This bounded check does not cover every wording, citation shape, numeric format or condition; it does not replace semantic evaluation. Existing assessments are marked stale through the input version. No live model was evaluated for this change.
- For explicitly stated English numeric time minimums, a positive model judgment now remains unknown when its cited text lacks a single sufficient integer with the same adjacent time unit or explicitly describes an upper bound. For example, a 2026 date, 25 days, 5 weeks or up to 35 days does not establish a 30-day minimum. This conservative check does not convert units, handle all numeric formats, or prove that an amount refers to the desired benefit or conditions; model judgment and user review remain necessary. Existing assessments are invalidated through the input version. Scripted model comparisons and the fit suite passed (75 passed, 41 ignored); no live model was run for this change.
- A uniquely located listing excerpt rejected for scoring, or returned with an unknown judgment, can now appear as explicitly unscored review context. It does not become positive or negative evidence and does not alter the score; repeated or unverifiable excerpts are not attached. Scripted fit and interface checks passed (73 fit tests passed, 41 ignored), as did Windows lint. The post-change local-model retest was again refused by the resource gate with 8,564 MiB reported free against 9,605 MiB required; no shared model was resident and no other process was stopped.
- In one resource-gated `qwen3.5:9b` run, the existing fictional preference cases passed 8/8. A separate four-case test passed 3/4: the model treated unlimited leave subject to approval as proof of a 30-day minimum. A prompt-only attempt also passed 3/4 and was discarded.
- A positive model judgment on an explicitly stated numeric minimum now stays unknown when the cited text contains no numeric digit. This is a conservative English-language check: it can defer spelled-out amounts or implicit comparisons and does not verify units, numerical relevance or semantics. Existing assessments are marked stale through the input version. The focused fit suite passed (73 passed, 41 ignored) and Windows lint passed. The post-change local-model rerun was refused by the GPU resource gate (9,374 MiB free versus 9,605 MiB required); the behavior was instead verified with a scripted model response. The test-owned model was unloaded. These small fictional tests do not establish general scoring accuracy.

## PDF setup recovery — 2026-11-02

- An isolated Windows test placed an incompatible `python.exe` before a compatible Python on PATH, started the actual background setup, installed the pinned packages in a test-only profile and rendered a fictional PDF. The test passed; it does not prove clean-host installation when no Python is present. Cleanup of the test-owned `target/pdf-setup-e2e-126dda321dc140878e5071abacb015d4` directory was rejected by automatic approval review as blocked by policy, so that directory remains.

- PDF setup now chooses an automatic PATH Python in its background task, probing at most four candidates for Python 3.10+, `venv` and `ensurepip` with a two-second, 128 MiB process limit each. If none passes, setup reports that condition rather than attempting an unverified PATH candidate. Explicit, managed and bundled runtimes retain precedence. The tool-status button no longer inserts an unverified candidate into the explicit path field. Fictional selection and UI checks passed; a local probe using a non-shell incompatible executable followed by an installed Python passed. An initial test mistakenly used the Windows command shell as the incompatible executable; it failed once and created an empty file in the checkout, which was removed, and the fixture was replaced. No PDF runtime was installed for this change, and a host with no suitable Python still needs a bundled runtime or separate installation.

- If the PDF setup start response is lost, the interface can recover the active or completed-but-uncollected task and follow its result rather than launching another setup. The task ID is available only while that process retains the setup task; app restart recovery is not established. A fictional interface test covered normal and lost-start responses, and focused setup tests and Windows lint passed.
- The earlier missing-`pypdf` test used a development executable without a neighboring bundled runtime, no configured managed runtime, and four PATH Python candidates without `pypdf`. A separate test used an installed Python to create a temporary isolated PDF environment, install the pinned packages, activate it within that temporary directory and render a fictional PDF successfully. It did not modify the user's active PDF setting or establish clean-host installation without Python.

## Converted PDF content coverage — 2026-11-02

- Text comparison for converted DOCX and plain-text PDFs now retains the PDF extractor's page coverage. The conversion and template-edit results identify pages containing both selectable text and images, or with an unknown image inventory, because text inside images may be absent from the comparison. A match still does not verify the source's complete content, visual layout or ATS reading order. Existing stored results may lack coverage; extraction failures remain unavailable.
- A fictional mixed-content PDF passed the local worker check, and a separate fictional DOCX conversion returned page coverage in its saved background result. Focused conversion, interface and Windows lint checks passed. The first worker run failed because its selected Python lacked `pypdf`; the successful runs explicitly selected the existing bundled PDF runtime. Clean-host setup and generic document fidelity remain unverified.

## Split-phone model evaluation — 2026-11-02

- A second fictional evaluation seeded incorrect initial street, city and reference-email mappings to exercise the retry independently of the first model call. In its first run, `qwen3.5:9b` corrected city but flagged street for manual review; the applicant email was not mapped to the reference. After the retry prompt clarified that the component key has an explicit profile value, one rerun corrected street and city and still left the reference unmapped. A separate rerun of the earlier split-phone test also passed. These are limited, seeded observations, not a real form or a general accuracy measure. A regression checks the two-retry limit; the suggestion suite passed (43 passed, 19 ignored) and Windows web lint passed. The test-owned model was unloaded.
- A bounded second model request now reconsiders up to two rejected component mappings when an explicit autocomplete hint, recognized applicant label and available exact profile value agree. Its response must pass the original validator and agree with that exact value; duplicate, malformed, uncertain and model-flagged results remain for review. The retry receives field metadata and key names, not profile values. In one resource-gated fictional test, `qwen3.5:9b` initially chose the full phone value again, then selected the supplied national component on retry. This does not establish general accuracy, and all proposed values still require review. The test-owned model was unloaded.
- A resource-gated fictional `qwen3.5:9b` test failed: with a full phone number and explicit national number, calling code and extension, the model chose the full number for a `tel-national` field. The validator rejected that field while accepting the separately mapped calling code and extension. Two experimental prompt changes also failed to correct the national-number choice and were discarded. The test-owned model was unloaded. These observations are not a general model-quality measurement.
- When a rejected or duplicate model mapping has a conservative exact profile match, review guidance says a matching profile value is available without revealing it or filling the field. A fictional regression checks both the available and absent-value cases. Before the retry described above was added, the suggestion suite passed (41 passed, 18 ignored) and Windows web lint passed; the local-model split-phone test failed and required manual entry.

## Screenshot-capture stability — 2026-11-02

- An existing application record can now request a bounded background page recapture without restarting the form. The session is restored even if the web page closes; checks cover tracked fields, attachments, page state and saved job/profile context, and the record remains in manual review. The latest result can be recovered while the app stays running; it is not a persistent task queue. Fictional browser-backed API cases covered stable and changing pages, profile changes, a competing task and lost responses; focused UI tests and Windows lint passed. This does not establish correctness of the filled form or employer-site behavior, and no application was submitted.
- The known screenshot-capture failures now add bounded, actionable guidance to the form review while retaining the existing incomplete-capture blocker. Unknown browser errors are not copied into the UI. Focused guidance tests and Windows web lint passed; the guidance was not exercised through a live employer page.
- [Playwright's page screenshot documentation](https://playwright.dev/docs/api/class-page) documents hiding the caret. An isolated fictional-form experiment using a temporary CDP inspector stylesheet produced four identical captures while focused, detected a changed value, and restored the stylesheet. This is an exploratory result, not production behavior: the repository's browser-control rules do not currently permit introducing that CSS control surface. The first capture of the CDP CSS documentation failed its expected-marker check; a second capture succeeded (native capture SHA-256 `ab2129325fd38deddceb48494c81f45fa523e2e1653ced0c145692321647f89e`, retained outside Git). The first failure was retained as a failure.
- A fictional focused-field browser probe observed a 15-pixel reported width change after an initial screenshot and, after dimensions stabilized, image differences between captures. Pixel comparison of one pair located a 1-by-15-pixel difference at the text cursor; another pair differed at the input edge. This establishes possible capture instability, not the cause of the earlier intermittent fixture failure. The app still refuses to mark a changing capture complete; its message now mentions a blinking cursor or animation as possible causes alongside a real page change.
- The existing genuinely changing-page fixture remained incomplete as intended, and Windows web lint passed. No automatic masking or assumption that a changed screenshot is safe was added. Focused inputs and dynamic pages can still require another capture or manual review.

The UI now clarifies that an absent explanation does not establish that the candidate lacks a qualification. The result is advisory: it does not choose or fill the checkbox, and its claims and sources require user review. Sensitive or model-flagged questions remain manual, so this does not resolve the flagged checkbox group in the real Greenhouse example below. A focused UI fixture verified that requesting evidence issues no fill action; an eligibility test covered sensitive and model-flagged fields. Windows web lint passed and the test-owned model was unloaded. This single positive example does not establish accuracy for unrelated or ambiguous qualifications, nor does it test a real employer form or the full web approval path.

## Listing observation age — 2026-11-02

- A local-model closure check that says `closed: false` but also supplies an evidence excerpt is now treated as inconsistent and shown as unavailable for manual verification. This can also flag a benign excerpt about an open role; it avoids silently discarding a contradictory closure quote. Fictional inspection and closure tests passed (49 candidate tests passed, 23 ignored), as did Windows web Clippy. No live site or application was contacted in this check.
- Sequential board discovery now skips later boards from a provider that returned HTTP 429, continues with other providers, and records each skipped board and its reason. It does not automatically retry or interpret `Retry-After`; grouping by provider is conservative when a limit applies only to one board. A fictional fetcher confirmed the same-provider skip and a subsequent different-provider request. Focused discovery tests, JavaScript syntax and Windows web Clippy passed. No live source or application was contacted in this check.
- A live FreeSerp batch using two generic job phrases over two pages each returned 40 unverified candidate links, with four successful provider pages and engine availability unreported. A separate direct API capture for the microscopy phrase is retained at `target/freeserp-microscopy-api-20261102.json` (SHA-256 `4144c252d63a89cb950edbfa85d2f1368e362a81dd98614da0e5f224e2f2ef88`); some results were not job ads. One returned listing URL produced HTTP 404 in the isolated crawler, not a verified job or application form. The rejected capture is retained locally as `real-job-99efb2979e4d475384d19d4eecdafe60.json` (SHA-256 `6c59122d6edab54272d30f54b35202fd30c31f3919e67ba6ba3286427662b90a`). A 404 can mean removal or movement and does not prove vacancy closure.
- Listing inspection now reports validated HTTP 404/410 with that uncertainty, using the same sanitized error handling as application checks. A fictional browser fixture passed after its first empty-body version returned a generic browser error instead of an identifiable status; an intermediate edit attempt failed before changing the fixture. The application-check suite passed (19 passed, 22 ignored), as did Windows lint. The live result was not opened in Waypoint or used to submit anything.
- A card-initiated board refresh now reports whether that saved listing's exact URL appeared in the parsed response, was not seen, could not be checked, or changed before processing. This is a response-time observation, not a closure or application-access determination; alternate URLs can produce a non-match. Fictional tests covered exact matches, missing URLs, malformed responses and fetch failures. An isolated UI check against the public `unframe` board reported that a deliberately fictional URL was not in its 33 returned listings. No employer application was opened or submitted. Windows web lint and focused discovery tests passed.
- Older or undated cards with a recognized Greenhouse, Lever or Ashby listing URL now offer a source-board refresh. The action uses the existing bounded discovery pipeline and shows its progress; a missing listing is not classified as closed, and a board refresh may not update a manually added record. In an isolated running app, a fictional card with an injected old observation triggered one public Greenhouse board refresh (33 parsed listings, no degraded source). Focused source-URL tests passed. No application was opened or submitted.
- The job list can filter to records older than 72 hours or with unknown observation times. This combines with the existing text and review filters; it does not remove jobs or classify them as closed. The displayed age and filter use the latest job-list refresh. Focused filter and existing match-sort checks passed; in an isolated running app, toggling the filter hid and restored a fictional recent listing.
- Job cards now show how long ago the listing was last recorded, recommend a refresh after the existing 72-hour threshold, and state that availability is unverified. Missing, invalid or future timestamps remain unknown. This timestamp can represent a saved observation rather than a fresh visit to the employer page; a recent record does not establish that a position is open, and an old one does not establish closure. Age is calculated when the job list refreshes.
- Focused server boundary and interface tests passed, as did the Windows web build and lint. An isolated running app displayed the new message for a fictional manually added listing. No real job was checked or application submitted in this test.

## Attachments and custom choices — 2026-11-02

- Custom dropdown advice now returns an immediate exact match when the user's complete answer uniquely matches a visible option, matching the existing radio-group behavior and avoiding a model call in that case. The current choice snapshot is rechecked, duplicate labels are refused, and the later visual-review and explicit-action gates are unchanged. The web interface handles both immediate and asynchronous results, with bounded polling. A fictional multi-reference browser/API fixture covered exact, duplicate and stale-snapshot cases; focused choice UI and advice tests and Windows web Clippy passed. This does not fix or explain the earlier live-model abstention on the non-exact phrase `I prefer Berlin`.

- A resource-gated `qwen3.5:9b` test (installed digest `c97eb11d70b1acdc88af01eef566c1fe4f7fbe93eb1afc06871132f293ff425a`) passed one fictional multi-reference choice workflow with live vision and simulated option advice. With live advice enabled, two runs recognized the visible `Location` label but returned no option for `I prefer Berlin`, failing the test; the old result did not identify whether the proposal or the later consistency check abstained. A diagnostic rerun was refused by the GPU gate at 9,457 MiB free versus 9,605 MiB required. The test-owned model was unloaded. These observations do not establish general accuracy or prove that multi-reference markup caused the abstention.
- Choice advice now labels whether a future abstention came from the initial model response or its second consistency check, and the web interface explains the distinction for both comboboxes and radio groups. This does not change the decision or establish why the model abstained. Fictional branch tests, focused interface checks and Windows web Clippy passed; the changed result was not retested with a live model after the resource-gate refusal.

- A fictional multi-reference choice was also exercised through the web backend's reviewed opening, option selection, record and rescan routes. The existing single-reference fixture still passed after sharing the test setup; the tests use simulated visual-review responses rather than a live model or web approval gesture. Windows web Clippy passed. No employer page was opened or application submitted.

- The browser can now inspect, open, scroll and select a custom choice when `aria-controls` or `aria-owns` contains several references and exactly one resolves to a listbox in the same tree. All references must resolve uniquely; missing, duplicate or multiple listbox references remain manual-review cases. Option lookup and the final click guard recheck the same relationship. This supports a standards-defined reference-list form, but the previously inspected Greenhouse page was not shown to use it and was not interacted with. Fictional browser fixtures covered multi-reference opening, selection, scrolling, `aria-owns`, ambiguity and a changed reference; six existing custom-choice browser tests, an existing scroll test, browser unit tests, browser Clippy and the web reviewed-open fixture passed. No application was submitted.
- The [WAI-ARIA 1.3 specification](https://www.w3.org/TR/wai-aria-1.3/#aria-controls) describes `aria-controls` as referencing one or more controlled elements. Its isolated Crawl4AI `0.9.5+flitzzip.portable2` capture returned HTTP 200; native receipt `target/w3c-aria-e3f5b6d88f5d42c1853d7b4df68d338e.json`, SHA-256 `09cb7054ccf0c2e0198c4ac72b9b5d257afe74817de9f2fd4bc9208f8fe6d7ca`.

- A separate fictional browser fixture cleared an uploaded file when the choice opened. The choice worker reported the action as opened but the attachment and prior-field checks as unsuccessful. Attachment failures now also save a specific manual-review reason when detected before an action or in its status result. A browser-backed status-endpoint test confirmed the saved reason. The interface now tells the user to close the review, inspect the form again and reattach documents; reinspection can lose unsaved site changes.
- Reviewed custom-choice actions now check the current inspection's staged files and browser file controls before and after opening, scrolling or selecting. Missing, changed, unverified or over-budget attachments require review; the interface identifies an attachment check failure. Checks are limited to eight upload controls and a four-second start budget, and do not prove server receipt or prevent a later page change.
- A fictional local-browser fixture verified that a staged upload is accepted by the check and a replacement file is detected; the existing reviewed-choice workflow, two choice UI checks and Windows web lint passed. This did not test an employer form or submission.

## Greenhouse remote evidence — 2026-11-02

- Remote-title classification also leaves the status unknown when nearby words in the same sentence negate remote work, including `not a fully remote position` and `remote work is not available`. This is a bounded English-language heuristic, not complete semantic contradiction detection; other wording can still be missed and unrelated nearby negations can suppress a valid signal. Connector tests (16 passed) and Windows web lint passed.
- Greenhouse titles ending in `(Remote)` now provide a remote-work signal when the available title, location and complete normalized description contain no conflicting onsite or hybrid wording. Ambiguous, conflicting or truncated content remains unknown. This narrow heuristic can miss remote jobs or treat unrelated wording as a conflict; it does not establish geographic eligibility.

This single observation does not establish general form coverage or eligibility.
- Connector tests, the workspace remote-evidence update test, Windows web build and web lint passed. No local model was used.

- An in-process API integration test held a fictional duplicate-model call open, sent the stop endpoint, and verified that the completed match was saved while the remaining candidates were available again from the same page. This used a controlled fake model, not a live-model timing measurement.
- Stopping an all-jobs duplicate scan now signals the background worker to stop after its current model batch or individual retry, rather than waiting for the whole 12-candidate group. Completed matches are retained; unexamined candidates leave the group cursor in place for retry. Cancellation does not interrupt an in-flight model request. Focused duplicate-search tests passed (10 passed, 7 ignored), UI tests and web lint passed. The host already had a model resident, so no live model was started, stopped or used for this change.
- The all-jobs duplicate control now runs up to three groups sequentially per start, with an option to stop after the current group. It stops on incomplete results without advancing the cursor; a completed final group stops the run. A focused UI fixture covered the three-group cap, cancellation, completion and partial-result retry. The control and stop button appeared in the running app, and a no-model attempt surfaced the existing model requirement. A later attempt after starting `qwen3.5:9b` did not yield a captured completed run; a subsequent runtime snapshot showed no resident model, and the reason is unknown. This is not a live multi-group success claim.
- Duplicate review can now scan all saved jobs in bounded groups of 12, including candidates with no shared employer token or requisition. It uses primary-key pagination, a separate per-tab cursor and the existing local-model comparison and review queue; it does not declare matches automatically. Fictional tests covered cross-employer inclusion, paging, already-reviewed pages, API save and UI cursor separation (9 web tests passed, 6 ignored; one store test and the UI check passed). The new control was visible in the running app and correctly reported that no model was loaded. This is a user-started group scan, not exhaustive automatic deduplication, and it was not evaluated on real vacancies in this change.
- In one resource-gated local `qwen3.5:9b` test, the all-jobs path examined a fictional direct listing, a differently titled recruiter listing with a different employer name but explicit client attribution, and an unrelated listing. It flagged only the recruiter listing for review. This single fixture does not establish general accuracy. The model was unloaded afterward.
- Two resource-gated local `qwen3.5:9b` duplicate-search tests passed on fictional listings. In one crowded-employer fixture the selector surfaced a differently titled related role among 12 examined candidates, and the model flagged that role alone. In a second, shortened multilingual fixture it flagged a matching requisition with a different title and did not flag an unrelated requisition. These are review suggestions from two small cases, not proof of identity or duplicate coverage. The model was unloaded after each run; no applications were sent.
- PDF operations now check up to four automatically discovered `PATH` Python candidates for the packages needed by the requested operation, with a two-second, memory-limited check per candidate. Explicit, managed and bundled runtime precedence is unchanged; if no candidate passes, the first remains selected so its diagnostic is shown. A fictional Unicode conversion, extraction and page render passed with a Python lacking `pypdf` first in `PATH` and a compatible runtime second. Discovery unit tests passed (2). A package-presence check cannot prove that a package imports or works, and clean-host installation remains unverified.
- A fictional Unicode text-to-PDF test converted successfully with the installed LibreOffice, but its first extraction failed because the automatically selected Python lacked `pypdf`. The same conversion, extraction and page-render check passed with the previously bundled PDF runtime explicitly selected. Missing-package errors for extraction, preview and editing now point to the existing PDF setup control and explain the environment-variable override. This verifies one host and fixture, not automatic clean-host setup or document fidelity in general.
- New model starts now require the same reported 4,096-token context as reused models, in addition to the existing single-model and full-GPU checks. A failed post-start verification warns that a model may remain resident and asks for a fresh status check; it does not unload a potentially shared runtime. Focused startup tests passed (4 passed, 3 ignored). In a current snapshot the inventory reported 10,003 MiB free GPU memory, below its 10,232 MiB estimate for installed Gemma 4 12B QAT, so no larger-model comparison was attempted. These estimates are not reservations or quality measurements.
- A resource-gated `qwen3.5:9b` fictional form test compared mapping with no lessons, conflicting lessons, and duplicate private records plus two permitted notes. In this run all three selected the applicant email and a general contact field, without mapping the reference or manager email; both permitted notes were sent in the deduplicated case. A separate multilingual fixture mapped the applicant email and left two ambiguous fields for review with and without lessons. One test run does not demonstrate broad accuracy or improvement from lessons. Ollama had no resident model afterward; no employer site was used or application submitted.
- Form-inspection lesson selection now deduplicates according to the model-visible platform relation and note permissions before applying the per-control hint limit. This prevents distinct records with private notes or unestablished platforms from crowding out other permitted context; original records and revision checks are retained. A fictional selection test and the form-task suite passed (16 passed, 15 ignored). This has not been evaluated as an accuracy improvement with a live model.
- Form mapping now also removes duplicate model-visible lesson hints after applying platform and note-privacy rules. Different saved records can otherwise become identical when their platforms are unconfirmed or their notes are private. Distinct permitted notes and conflicting outcomes remain available within the existing budget; duplicate notes no longer consume it repeatedly. Focused inference tests passed (4 passed, 7 live-model tests ignored). This does not establish an improvement in model accuracy.
- Form-lesson selection now collapses identical saved reports instead of repeating them to fill the hint budget. Distinct platforms, context notes and conflicting reported outcomes remain eligible; notes are sent to the local model only with explicit permission. This is advisory evidence, not an automatic action or proof that a response works. Focused workspace lesson tests passed (8), as did form-inspection tests (16 passed, 15 ignored). No live application was used for this change.
- Consecutive comparable fit assessments now report changes to both bounds of the extracted-criteria range as well as the midpoint. This exposes cases such as an unchanged 50% midpoint moving from 50–50% to 0–100%. Missing or invalid historical ranges are reported as unavailable. The range still excludes unextracted criteria and model mistakes; it is not a confidence interval. The focused comparison and UI checks passed; the broader fit suite passed before the final fixture refinement (54 passed, 32 ignored).
- Web-search parsing now preserves word boundaries when a provider places tabs or line breaks in result titles and snippets. It still removes other control characters, bounds output, and treats results as unverified. A focused fictional parser case and the existing search tests passed (13 passed, 2 live-network tests ignored); no search coverage claim follows from this change.
- After the multi-segment repair, local `qwen3.5:9b` was retested on fictional browser fixtures. It again transcribed a placeholder as an entered email; live DOM checks left it unconfirmed while verifying an entered name. A separate changed-value test rejected a stale transcription, and a dense-form fixture detected its deliberately incorrect seventh answer. These are isolated cases, not general accuracy measurements. The review panel now labels each group of raw model transcriptions as unverified and warns that it can include placeholders or unrelated page content. The targeted UI test passed. The model was unloaded after testing; no employer application was submitted.
- A subsequent read-only retest identified an observation label exceeding the 200-byte limit. The limit is now 1,000 UTF-8 bytes; oversized labels still fail rather than being truncated or matched loosely. The next retest reached all four image segments but could not recheck the first: the server retained only the most recent result. It now retains up to 18 results for 10 minutes, enough for six segments and their possible halves. On another read-only retest, all four segments and their final rechecks completed; the interface still reported incomplete coverage and manual review. The model transcribed some `Select...` placeholders, illustrating that its output is not proof of filled values. No candidate data was entered or submitted. Focused visual-review tests passed (25 passed, 15 ignored); this does not establish general form accuracy or autofill capability.
- Visual-review validation now reports the observation number and whether a label is missing, empty or too long, or a value is missing or too long, without including model-produced text. The model prompt also states the existing size limits. Invalid segments still fail for manual review; the live failure below has not been reproduced with these changes. The focused visual-review tests passed (24 passed, 15 ignored).
- A read-only visual review of the same empty application form used the locally installed `qwen3.5:9b` model (reported 100% GPU). The first captured segment returned no fields, the second returned text and labels, and the third failed validation with `Vision observation has an invalid label`; the fourth was not reviewed. Waypoint marked visual review incomplete and required manual inspection. The invalid output was not retained, so its precise cause is unknown. No candidate information was entered, attached or submitted. This does not establish visual accuracy or autofill capability. The model was unloaded after the test.
- When the final form schema differs from the initial one, inspection now reports counts of added, removed and modified fields plus ordering and step-structure changes, without including field values or identifiers. The existing invalidation and manual-review gate remain in force. Focused tests cover schema changes and bounded retry behavior; web-app lint passed.
- In a second read-only inspection of the same listing, the driver instead reported a disappeared browser node and retried; that is a separate instability path, so the new schema-difference summary did not appear. Its warning now explains the retry. The cause of the disappearance is not established. No applicant data was entered or submitted.

It marked the changing form for manual review and discarded prior suggestions. No candidate information was entered, no document was attached and no application was submitted. This does not test autofill, solve the CAPTCHA or establish behavior on other listings.

## Public vacancy inspection — 2026-11-02

- The pinned research crawler was found to add an automation-masking flag and a misleading Linux browser user agent despite `enable_stealth=false`. Its isolated launch now removes that exact flag, uses an explicit research-client user agent for robots checks, and fails closed if the pinned launch flags change. Captures made with the previous configuration are not accepted as qualifying no-stealth research evidence. After correction, the CERN listing again returned HTTP 404, its careers homepage returned HTTP 200, and the IonQ URL returned HTTP 200 but failed the expected-title check. One intermediate capture with an absent user agent failed the robots parser and was retained as a failure; the corrected explicit user agent restored robots checking. Provider tests and dependency-lock verification passed. This does not establish the cause of Waypoint's navigation error.
- Individual and batch inspection feedback now shows the inspected page title when it differs from the search result title, asking the user to compare them. Different text can be harmless formatting or a genuinely different role; this warning does not decide identity or block a listing. Fictional UI tests cover both paths; the full set of 123 JavaScript checks passed.
- In a separate isolated workspace, a generic FreeSerp query found an IonQ result on the Chicago Quantum Exchange job board. An independent bounded Crawl4AI capture returned HTTP 200 with substantive content, but its page title identified a different role from the search result, so it is not accepted as evidence for the advertised vacancy. Waypoint's selected-listing inspection returned `net::ERR_INVALID_RESPONSE`, marked the item for manual review and added no job. A read-only driver probe reproduced the error using both installed Chrome 156 and the crawler's headless Chromium binary. The cause is not established; the application route was not verified. This single result does not measure general site coverage. No candidate data was entered and no application was submitted.
- As a control, the same Waypoint browser opened the CERN careers homepage after a separate bounded HTTP 200 capture. This rules out a failure of every public navigation in that run, but does not explain the IonQ-hosted page failure or verify an application form.
- A separate read-only CDP probe using the explicit `WaypointResearch/1.0` user agent received HTTP 200 for the IonQ URL. The ordinary Waypoint browser reported `net::ERR_INVALID_RESPONSE`. This shows a request-identity difference in these observations, not its cause or proof that the returned page is the requested role. Waypoint's application browser identity was not changed and the application route remains unverified.

Initially Waypoint did not add it and no persistent outcome was visible after the transient notification disappeared. On retest, the changed interface kept the HTTP 404 and manual-review message on the card. This is one unavailable page, not general freshness verification. No application was submitted.

## Isolated workspace and live search — 2026-11-02

- A process can set `WAYPOINT_WORKSPACE_DIR` to an absolute folder for an isolated workspace. It does not replace the saved workspace preference; while the override is active the settings API refuses changes to that preference, and the interface identifies the temporary workspace and disables the saved-folder controls. Invalid override paths fail startup. The focused storage and interface tests and Windows build passed; the disabled controls were also observed in the running app.
- The web-app suite passed with 515 tests and 275 ignored; all 123 JavaScript checks and web-app lint passed after the workspace and inspection changes.
- In an isolated encrypted workspace, one generic materials-science search through the web interface and FreeSerp returned nine unverified links from ten reported results. The results included search pages and unrelated material; no listing was opened and no application was submitted. This is one query and does not establish search coverage or listing freshness.

## Search engine attribution — 2026-11-02

- Search results now retain a distinct singular `engine` label even when the provider also reports an `engines` list. Labels remain unverified provider attribution and are capped at eight; the limit is reported if another distinct label cannot fit. Fictional parser tests passed; no search provider or employer was contacted.

## Browser rehearsal limits — 2026-11-02

- A local Chromium test now exercises the actual browser driver against a fictional form whose input handler sends a WebSocket frame. The loopback server received the frame and the driver's network counter increased; no external site was contacted. The initial fixture failed because its accepted socket inherited nonblocking mode; after correcting the fixture, the test and browser Clippy passed. This confirms observation after transmission for one local case, not interception or a no-submission guarantee.
- The browser's partial network counter now includes outgoing WebSocket frames as well as non-GET/HEAD requests; frame payloads are not retained. Existing fill and attachment checks stop or require review when that counter changes, and their messages identify both types of traffic. A fictional loopback browser test confirmed that an outgoing frame was counted even though request interception did not block it; a unit check confirmed its payload was not retained. Form-preview tests passed (25 passed, 41 ignored), and Windows web Clippy passed. This is detection after transmission, not prevention: frames or requests may be missed in other targets or transports, and a site can still submit independently. Real-site rehearsal remains unqualified for a no-submission guarantee.
- A fictional loopback browser test confirmed that enabling CDP `Fetch` interception after a WebSocket is established does not block messages sent on that socket. Request interception alone is therefore insufficient for a no-submission rehearsal barrier; this test does not characterize every browser transport or target.
- Guarded clicks now reject a native submit control at the actual hit point, including one nested inside a selected container, and labels associated with submit controls. A fictional browser fixture reproduced the nested-button bypass before the change and passed afterward; existing hover-cover and custom-choice checks also passed. This does not prevent page scripts or network activity from submitting by other means.
- The form-review interface now states that a site may transmit or submit information during typing or other interactions, and that review mode cannot guarantee prevention. The browser enables network observation but does not install a comprehensive request-interception barrier. A guarded click is not proof that a page cannot initiate a submission. Real employer interaction therefore remains unqualified for the user's no-submission testing requirement.

## Local choice workflow qualification — 2026-11-02

- Added an opt-in, resource-gated browser test that exercises a reviewed custom choice with the local vision model on fictional forms. Its setup completes duplicate indexing before requesting actions. The ordinary fixture-backed workflow also passes.
