A larger capture did not help. After clarifying that the model should look beside or above the control and distinguish its label from values, two runs confirmed both labels and finished the post-scroll review. These are narrow fixture observations, not evidence of general reliability. The model was unloaded afterward; no employer was contacted and no application was submitted.
- A separate resource-gated run on independent fictional controls read `Job location`, the `Berlin` option and selected value, and a later `Sydney` option. It does not cover arbitrary layouts or employer websites.
- The web-app suite passed serially and in a subsequent parallel run (515 passed, 274 ignored each). Two earlier parallel runs failed because a concurrent email-worker test held the global busy state while a listing-inspection test expected an idle worker. Those two tests now share a test-only lock; the production busy check is unchanged.

## Retaining search attribution with listings — 2026-11-01

- Selected search results now retain bounded provider-reported engine names and dates across selection recovery. New listing inspections carry that context into the first securely saved listing capture or source-only record, and the listing review displays it as unverified search attribution. The comparison uses the requested search-result URL rather than assuming a redirect is the same listing. Search queries and snippets are not included. Existing captures are not overwritten, so later search reports may not be retained; the inspection reports that outcome. A size-limit omission is reported when that capture is readable.
- Fictional tests covered selection persistence and stale-write rejection, bounded metadata, a simulated inspection save/retry through the local API, and UI rendering. All UI checks passed. The save test used a Windows workspace and source-only capture; it did not exercise an external provider or employer. No application was submitted.

## Search-result attribution — 2026-11-01

- When a provider repeats the exact same URL within one response, the search parser now retains distinct reported engine labels, fills a missing title or snippet from a later copy, and displays differing provider-reported dates separately. It keeps at most eight engine labels and four alternative dates and signals when those bounds may omit entries. The same treatment applies to bounded references to URLs seen on earlier pages. These are provider reports, not verified listing dates, and URL equality does not establish whether different URLs advertise the same position.
- Fictional parser fixtures covered duplicate counts, missing metadata, conflicting dates and bounds. A local loopback provider exercised background search and result replay with repeated attribution; focused UI and batch tests passed. No external provider or employer was contacted, and no application was submitted.

## Length-limited draft recovery — 2026-11-01

- On 2026-11-02, resource-gated reruns again produced a 51-unit answer for a 35-unit limit, even when repair prompts requested a shorter phrase or supplied a word-count target; both changes were discarded. A new ignored test wrapper makes this known failing qualification reproducible with the existing model resource gate. The overlong answer remained marked `needs_edit`, and the model was unloaded.
- A length repair now receives the prior valid draft, its measured UTF-16 length and the question. If a structurally valid answer remains too long or short after the bounded repair, the interface offers it as a draft requiring edits instead of discarding it. Its evidence check remains advisory; reviewed prefill still rejects the unedited out-of-range value. An explicit model abstention remains a failure. If the repair prompt cannot fit the context budget, the earlier draft can likewise be reviewed rather than silently lost.
- In the fictional 20–35-unit test, `qwen3.5:9b` repeatedly returned the same 51-unit sentence. Focused shortening and exact-excerpt prompt variants did not make it fit; the latter was discarded. The live length test still fails its strict fit assertion, and the final output is explicitly marked `needs_edit`. A focused reviewed-fill test rejects that overlong sentence and accepts a shorter user-edited version. These are narrow observations, not evidence of general model reliability. Minimum sampled available RAM was 17.28 GiB in the final run; the model was unloaded. No employer was contacted or application submitted.

## Minimum form lengths — 2026-11-01

- Native text and textarea minimum lengths now flow into the reviewed schema, mapping context, answer-drafting prompt and interface. Nonempty values are checked in UTF-16 code units before prefill; empty values remain exempt from the minimum, with requiredness handled separately. A changed minimum changes the schema identity. Drafts below the minimum get one bounded repair; contradictory minimum and maximum limits require manual review. Existing maximum-length checks remain in place.
- A fictional browser fixture verified native minimum extraction and changed schema identity; focused tests covered surrogate pairs, withholding a short suggestion, reviewed-fill errors, drafting repair and the UI counter. The local 9B model completed a fictional two-section wizard with a minimum length. In a separate 20–35-unit answer test it exceeded the maximum after repair, so the app withheld the answer; a prompt-only attempt did not fix it and was discarded. This remains a model limitation. Minimum sampled available RAM was 18.90 GiB for the wizard and 19.21/19.26 GiB for the answer checks; the model was unloaded. No employer was contacted or application submitted.

## Form format patterns — 2026-11-01

- Text-like input fields now carry bounded site-declared HTML patterns into the form review and local mapping context. A changed pattern changes the reviewed schema identity. Patterns over 512 bytes block automatic prefill for manual review; absent patterns preserve the previous schema hash. The interface shows the raw pattern as site-provided data, and Chromium still determines whether a value is valid. The app does not yet automatically reformat answers to satisfy a pattern, and invalid browser patterns may be ignored by Chromium.
- The workspace suite passed after correcting an initially wrong local linker path, before the final oversized-pattern handling and fixture adjustment. A fictional browser check then verified pattern changes alter the schema identity and an oversized pattern is reported. With `qwen3.5:9b`, a fictional two-section wizard with a patterned first field passed mapping, reviewed filling and visual checks; navigation was a simulated user gesture. This does not prove the model interprets arbitrary patterns. Minimum sampled available RAM in that run was 18.89 GiB; the model was unloaded. No employer was contacted or application submitted.

## Earlier-field validation details — 2026-11-01

- When a completed field later fails a native browser constraint, bounded rechecks now carry its label and fixed constraint category into control-focus and visual-focus review, and into the application record when capture succeeds. The custom-choice precheck also reports that category. Unrecognized errors remain generic; entered values and site-provided custom error text are not copied into these diagnostics. Rechecks still stop at the first failure and may leave other fields unchecked.
- A fictional local-browser form made a previously completed reference field invalid after an email field changed; the completed-field review identified the reference and custom-validation category without submitting. A focused test verified the four-second/32-field accounting and ignored unrecognized detail, and UI checks covered the visual-review message. This does not establish behavior on employer sites.

## Native field validation feedback — 2026-11-01

- When a text field retains the requested value but Chromium reports a native constraint failure, the prefill error now names the field and a fixed category such as invalid type, required format or a site-supplied custom error. The same category is available when verifying a previously filled field. The app does not copy the entered value or the site's custom validation text into this diagnostic; an uninspectable or changed field keeps the existing generic failure. Other validation mechanisms still need review.
- A local fictional browser form exercised format and email-type failures, successful corrections, and a later custom validity change to an earlier field. It confirmed that diagnostics omitted both the fictional input and custom message. A focused prefill test checked the field label and that a failed fill is not marked complete. No employer was contacted or application submitted.

## Site error details — 2026-11-01

- Invalid-field warnings now include visible messages referenced by `aria-errormessage` when the references resolve unambiguously. Hidden, duplicate, missing, oversized or over-budget references are reported as unavailable. Reads are bounded to 32 unique references, four per field and a short inspection budget; changed warning text requires a fresh inspection. Messages are page-provided and may contain user data. Other error patterns remain undetected.
- A fictional local-browser form showed a visible email error, withheld a hidden message and refused ambiguous duplicate IDs. Correcting the email cleared its warning on rescan; no submission occurred. Focused preview validation also checks that a changed error requires a fresh review. This does not establish behavior across employer sites.

## Site-reported invalid fields — 2026-11-01

- Form inspection now reports visible fields whose site-provided `aria-invalid` attribute indicates an error, including the field label. These warnings remain reviewable so other fields can still be prepared; if a warning changes, the existing review binding requires a fresh inspection. Hidden fields and explicit `false` values do not create this warning. This is not a general form-validity check: sites may show errors without this attribute, and absence of a warning does not mean a field is valid.
- A real local browser inspected a fictional form, identified two visible invalid fields, ignored hidden and explicitly valid controls, filled one fictional field and verified its warning disappeared on rescan while the other remained. A focused preview test checked that a changed warning requires a rescan. No employer was contacted or application submitted.

## Template rewrite scope — 2026-11-01

- With no writing instructions, template rewriting now supplies only the selected passage as candidate material; the job listing remains available for context. When instructions are present, other reviewed qualifications remain available because the instructions may request them. The editor reports omitted qualifications and warns when the initial or final draft cites additional sources, retaining first-pass citations even if the style pass drops them. Citations and evidence checks do not guarantee that all added claims are detected.
- In one fictional local-model pair, the previous version added an unrelated Python qualification and its style pass dropped the citation; the evidence check identified that claim as unsupported. After scoping the no-instructions case, the model left Python out; with an explicit instruction to include it, the model included and cited it. The no-instructions passage was otherwise unchanged. These are narrow cases; nonempty instructions that do not request additions still leave other qualifications available and the model may add them. Minimum sampled available RAM was 19.26 GiB; the model was unloaded. No employer was contacted or application submitted. Local logs are under `target/live_template_*-qwen-live.log`.

## Template evidence provenance — 2026-11-01

- When a template suggestion cites the selected, independently unverified passage, model judgments of supported claims are now reported as uncertain, with the original model judgment and explanation retained. The evidence check cites sources for the whole suggestion rather than individual claims, so this conservative rule also marks claims uncertain when another cited, reviewed fact might support them. Unsupported and already-uncertain judgments are unchanged; a failed check stays failed.
- In the previously failing fictional local-model example, the model again judged both the template claim and a reviewed Python fact supported. Because both sources were cited, the app marked both claims uncertain and explained why. The previously failing live check passed; a separate invented-management claim remained flagged. This does not establish the truth of either source or general model accuracy. Minimum sampled available RAM was 19.38 GiB and the model was unloaded. No employer was contacted or application submitted. Local outputs are in `target/live_*qwen-live.log`.

## Evidence-check recovery — 2026-11-01

- Answer and template evidence checks now retry once when the local model returns malformed structured output or a review that fails validation. The retry receives the fixed validation reason, and the same exact-quotation, status, explanation and numeric checks apply. Runtime failures are not retried. The interface identifies recovered checks; unrecovered checks remain visibly incomplete. A retry adds a model call and may be refused if the prompt exceeds the existing size limit.
- In a fictional live run, both template checks recovered after an invalid first response, including the previously failing résumé case. A separate invented-management claim was still flagged. Another fictional template check failed because the model treated the unverified selected passage as supported instead of uncertain; the interface explains that source provenance and the judgment is advisory. These observations do not establish general accuracy. Minimum sampled available RAM was 19.63 and 19.61 GiB in the two runs; the model was unloaded. No employer was contacted or application submitted. Logs are under `target/live_*qwen-live.log`.

## Template voice review — 2026-11-01

- Template drafting now asks both model passes to preserve the selected passage's grammatical person and tense unless the writing instructions request otherwise. The editor warns if the original and suggestion differ in whether they start with `I` or `we`. This narrow check does not detect other voice or tense changes, and the user must review the complete replacement.
- The local 9B model retained first-person past tense in one fictional cover-letter example after the prompt change. It still introduced `I` into a fictional résumé passage that omitted a subject; the new warning flagged that change. In the most recent run, the evidence check for that résumé example returned `invalid`, so its existing live test failed even though the voice warning worked. The interface already reports an incomplete evidence check. This is a known unresolved model-quality limitation, not a successful evidence review. Minimum sampled available RAM was 19.69 GiB; the model was unloaded. No employer was contacted or application submitted. Local output is in `target/live_template_*-qwen-live.log`.

## Template suggestion review — 2026-11-01

- The single-passage and batch template editors now show the local evidence check, qualification-selection limits and unavailable-check warnings already returned by the backend. They also identify unchanged suggestions. A supported judgment may rely on the selected passage, which is not independently verified; the interface says so. These judgments apply to the generated text and do not validate later edits or document layout.
- In one fictional local-model test, `qwen3.5:9b` shortened a wordy accessibility passage while retaining the screen-reader detail. It judged the revised claim supported by the user-selected passage, illustrating why that provenance warning matters. This is one case, not proof of reliable tailoring. Minimum sampled available RAM was 19.85 GiB and the model was unloaded. No employer was contacted or application submitted.

## Template evidence qualification — 2026-11-01

- The live fictional template test now checks that the evidence review completes and flags a claim based on the selected, independently unverified passage as uncertain. In the observed run the model retained the original passage unchanged; this does not establish that it improves a résumé or correctly classifies every claim. A separate fictional answer check identified the cited Python work as supported and an invented five-year management claim as unsupported.
- Both checks passed with the production-admitted `qwen3.5:9b`; minimum sampled available RAM was 19.88 GiB and the model was unloaded. Three focused template tests also passed. These are narrow model observations, not proof of document quality or reliability. No employer was contacted or application submitted. Local outputs are in `target/live_*qwen-live.log`.

## Preference evidence context — 2026-11-01

- Preference assessments now include bounded surrounding text from the verified original listing for a unique cited passage. The interface displays it alongside the exact quote when different; scoring is unchanged. Ambiguous quotes remain unknown, and older assessments do not gain context until reassessed. The context makes a citation easier to inspect but does not prove the model's interpretation.
- In fictional local-model checks, reordered job requirements produced the same 71% score and 43–100% range, an unspecified remote region remained unknown, and explicit location alternatives were evaluated as expected. After the change, the location test also verified that each displayed context came from the original listing and contained its quote. The 9B model was unloaded; minimum sampled available RAM was 19.90, 20.36 and 19.95 GiB across these runs. Forty-nine fit tests, the preference-context UI check and web-app Clippy passed. No employer was contacted or application submitted. Local logs are under `target/live_*qwen-live.log`.

## Matching repeatability after evidence recovery — 2026-11-01

- On one fictional job and candidate, the production-admitted `qwen3.5:9b` returned the same score, 82%, and the same rubric in three sequential assessments. The test now also checks that Python and accessibility are supported while SQL and cloud experience remain unknown, so identical but incorrect answers to those fixture requirements do not pass. A separate fictional case kept one year of SQL experience unknown against a five-year requirement and recognized six years of Python experience.
- The strengthened three-run test passed again, with minimum sampled available RAM of 19.51 GiB; the earlier repeatability and partial-experience run sampled at least 19.46 GiB. The model was unloaded. These are narrow fixtures, not a claim of calibrated scores, general accuracy or repeatability across jobs. Web-app Clippy passed. No employer was contacted or application submitted. Native logs are under `target/live_job_fit_repeatability_on_fictional_candidate-qwen-live.log` and `target/live_full_fit_does_not_treat_partial_experience_as_disproof-qwen-live.log`.

## Matching evidence recovery — 2026-11-01

- In a fictional fit assessment, `qwen3.5:9b` initially left a preferred Kubernetes operations criterion unknown despite an attested fact about administering production clusters. Two prompt-only revisions still missed it. The recovery pass now supplies explicit one-based fact IDs and clarifies that each criterion is judged separately in its job context. In one rerun the model selected the relevant fact and the independent qualification check accepted it. This changes neither the scoring arithmetic nor the requirement to review model judgments; explicit numbering increases prompt size and can cause the existing size limit to leave more cases unknown.
- In that rerun the relevant candidate scored 100 with the three extracted criteria supported; the unrelated candidate scored 50 with a 0–100 range and all three unknown. These heuristic scores are not hiring probabilities, and one case does not establish general accuracy or isolate which prompt change caused the result. Separate fictional tests still distinguished C from C++ and did not credit insufficient or unrelated years of experience. Minimum sampled available RAM was 19.75 GiB and 19.81 GiB in those runs; the model was unloaded. Forty-nine fit tests passed with 29 ignored, and web-app Clippy passed. No employer was contacted or application submitted. Local output is in `target/live_job_fit_*-qwen-live.log` and `target/qwen-fit-*-before-prompt.log`.

## Initial web-search checkpoint — 2026-11-01

- Starting a batch search now attempts to save its approved plan before returning the start response. If secure persistence fails, the search can continue in memory and the interface warns that restart recovery is not yet assured. The provider worker can start before the checkpoint finishes, so a crash before the response can still interrupt both operations.
- With a fictional loopback provider holding its first response, a real local backend returned a successful checkpoint, was stopped, and recovered the complete four-page plan after restart with zero pages saved. Fourteen batch tests, a UI warning test and web-app Clippy passed. No employer was contacted or application submitted; the local harness is `target/search-initial-recovery.cjs`.

## Interrupted search provider context — 2026-11-01

- Interrupted search checkpoints now retain the original provider URL, when it is at most 2,048 bytes, and show it alongside the prepared retry. The user still reviews the current provider before starting; it is not silently changed. Older checkpoints or longer provider URLs display that the original was not saved.
- A real local backend and browser were tested with an isolated workspace and a fictional loopback search provider. The backend was stopped after one result while the next page was in flight, then restarted; it recovered two phrases and the original provider, preserved typed text, and made no automatic retry. The provider saw only the two intended test requests. Minimum sampled available RAM was 23.65 GiB, although this was not continuous peak measurement. Fourteen batch tests, a UI check and web-app Clippy passed. No employer was contacted or application submitted. The local harness and screenshot are under `target/search-recovery-real-backend*`.

## Interrupted search plan recovery — 2026-11-01

- Wider-web batch checkpoints retain the approved phrase and page plan, including at the first checkpoint before a page is collected. After a restart, the interface can prepare phrases with failed or unsaved pages for the user to review and search again. It does not start a search automatically or replace text already entered in the form; rerunning repeats all requested pages for those phrases and replaces the displayed batch results. The interface warns about both.
- The checkpoint remains subject to successful encrypted workspace persistence and periodic checkpoint timing; a crash before the first checkpoint can still lose the plan, and an in-flight page remains unknown. Older checkpoints without a plan have no retry control. Fourteen batch tests, focused UI checks and web-app Clippy passed, including a zero-result checkpoint and reopen fixture. No live provider or employer was contacted in this change, and no application was submitted.

## Email duplicate recheck gate — 2026-11-01

- Email approval, rehearsal and reservation now treat an active legacy duplicate-recheck cursor as pending indexing, even when every job already has an index row. The check occurs in the same database transaction as the existing handoff gate. Acknowledging bounded coverage does not bypass pending work; an existing approval is not consumed by the refusal.
- A fictional database fixture verified refusal during the cursor recheck and successful rehearsal after it clears, using both SQLite and SQLCipher. Web-app Clippy passed. The email transport was not called and no message or application was sent. Bounded duplicate comparison still cannot establish that all duplicates were found.

## Duplicate indexing before form actions — 2026-11-01

- Reviewed form fills, document attachments and custom-choice actions now wait while any job remains queued for duplicate indexing, including migration rechecks. Existing duplicate-pair holds still apply. This is a conservative global wait: large or interrupted indexing can delay unrelated jobs. Completed bounded indexing does not guarantee every duplicate was found, and concurrent changes after the check remain possible.
- A fictional browser endpoint test verified that pending indexing rejects a fill without changing the field and that the reviewed workflow can proceed after indexing. Its first run after the guard reached an unrelated screenshot-segment assertion failure; the rerun passed, and the expanded gate test passed. The web suite passed 436 tests with 227 ignored before the final fixture expansion; web-app Clippy passed afterward. No real application was submitted.

## Engineering title duplicate review — 2026-11-01

- Duplicate candidate matching now treats the standalone title terms `engineering` and `engineer` alike, so closely worded roles can be flagged even without a requisition or description. This creates a pending review pair; it does not merge jobs or establish that the roles are the same. Migration 41 queues affected existing titles for the existing bounded background recheck and preserves prior decisions. Candidate and comparison limits still apply.
- The migration and pending holds were tested with fictional jobs in both ordinary SQLite and SQLCipher. The store suite passed 84 tests with one ignored, and web-app Clippy passed. Runtime and peak memory for a large existing database were not measured. No application was submitted.

## Earlier-field warnings through subsequent sections — 2026-11-01

- The earlier-field review warning now carries forward through later navigations and rescans in the same review session, even when no additional fields were filled on the immediately preceding page. Repeated carry-forward does not duplicate the warning. Closing and starting a separate session does not carry this particular in-memory warning; saved manual-review reasons remain separate.
- Focused navigation tests covered carry-forward and deduplication (10 passed, 3 browser tests ignored), and web-app Clippy passed. This is not an end-to-end employer-site test; no application was submitted.

## Prior fields across application navigation — 2026-11-01

- When a reviewed application link opens a new inspection, completed fields from the previous inspection now produce the same explicit review warning already used for rescans. The next page cannot itself establish that earlier fields remain correct. The status endpoint records a manual-review reason when it collects the result.
- A focused unit test covered the presence and absence of prior filled fields, and ten navigation tests and web-app Clippy passed. The reviewed-link path was not tested end to end against an employer page in this change; no application was submitted.

## Placeholder and repeated-label model checks — 2026-11-01

- In a fictional form, the production-admitted `qwen3.5:9b` transcribed a placeholder email as an entered value, failing the raw-vision test. The separate live-value check correctly left it unconfirmed. A proposed prompt instruction about lighter placeholder text did not improve that example and was discarded. The UI now explicitly explains this limitation and the live-value check.
- On another fictional form, the model distinguished repeated email labels by section; it initially omitted punctuation in one value, while a focused review matched it, and a crop lacking section context remained unobserved. These are single-case observations, not a general reliability claim. Minimum sampled free RAM was 18.83 and 18.88 GiB across the two runs; the model was unloaded. UI checks passed. The navigation panel also now correctly explains its five-per-pass limit. No employer application was used or submitted. Local evidence is in `target/qwen-placeholder-before-contrast-prompt.log` and `target/live_*qwen-live.log`.

## Portuguese form vision regression — 2026-11-01

- On a fictional Portuguese form, the `qwen3.5:9b` model correctly flagged legal eligibility and consent for review and left them untouched, but the previous full-page prompt returned no visual observations in two runs. The earlier prompt returned the fields in one run; that run also exposed an incorrect fixture expectation that an unchecked checkbox should be transcribed as empty. The fixture now expects `unchecked`, consistent with the prompt and the separately verified live checkbox state.
- After retaining the explicit label-spelling instruction but restoring the earlier unreadable-value wording, one Portuguese run passed, as did one multilingual and one changed-field run. The minimum sampled available RAM was 17.74 GiB and 18.08 GiB respectively; the model was unloaded. These few observations do not prove causality or broad reliability, and unobserved fields still require review. Focused visual tests (23 passed, 14 ignored) and web-app Clippy passed. No employer form was used or application submitted. Native local logs are under `target/portuguese-*-qwen.log` and `target/*-qwen-live.log`.

## Multilingual form vision — 2026-11-01

- On a fictional multilingual form, the production-admitted local `qwen3.5:9b` correctly mapped and filled the name and email, but first transcribed `Vorname` as `Vornname`; the comparison correctly left the field unobserved. The full-page vision prompt now explicitly asks for exact label spelling and accents. In one rerun, the model transcribed both filled fields correctly and reported the sensitive fields empty. A separate changed-field case detected the altered value, and a two-step fictional workflow again passed its mapping, filling and visual checks. The next-step click in that test simulates a user gesture; the application does not automatically navigate or submit.
- Minimum sampled available RAM was 19.18 GiB for the multilingual and changed-field run, and 19.14 GiB for the two-step run. The model was unloaded after each run. These single examples do not establish general transcription accuracy or prove the prompt caused the improvement. Focused visual-review tests (23 passed, 14 ignored) and web-app Clippy passed. No real employer form was filled or submitted. Native logs remain under `target/*-qwen-live.log` and `target/multilingual-qwen-before-label-prompt.log`.

## Web regression coverage — 2026-11-01

- CI now discovers and runs all standalone `*-ui.test.js` checks instead of a manually maintained subset. Three stale test harnesses were updated to follow current function boundaries and dependencies. All 95 checks passed locally; these are simulated UI checks, not full rendered-browser coverage.
- A single-threaded web suite initially reported 433 passed, one failed and 227 ignored: the failed screenshot-record test observed `preparation_in_progress` instead of `manual_review`. Ten subsequent isolated runs passed, and a full rerun passed 434 with 227 ignored. The cause remains unproven; the test now includes the underlying recording error if this recurs. No production fix is claimed. No employer application was submitted.

## Local date and time visual check — 2026-11-01

- An isolated CPU run of `gemma4:12b-it-q4_K_M` inspected a fictional native date-time field. It read `01-Nov-2026 01:30 PM` while the stored value was `2026-11-01T13:30`; initially the comparison reported a mismatch. The comparison now recognizes this explicit English abbreviated-month and 12-hour display only for completed native local date-time fields, and still checks the live value independently. Ambiguous numeric dates and other formats remain unnormalized and require review.
- A second run reported both a transcription match and a live-value match. Minimum sampled available RAM was 15.07 GiB during that run; the isolated model was stopped afterward. A browser fixture confirmed a changed live value is rejected, and unit tests covered invalid and ambiguous dates; web-app Clippy passed. One fictional example does not establish general visual accuracy. No employer site was used or application submitted. Local evidence is under `target/model-eval/isolated-gemma-native-datetime*`.

## Reviewed native local date and time — 2026-11-01

- Native `datetime-local` fields can now be reviewed and filled at minute precision. The value has no timezone; invalid calendar dates, timezones and seconds are rejected. The browser respects native validity constraints, checks existing values before replacement and verifies the retained value. Other unsupported special controls still require manual handling.
- A fictional browser fixture rejected invalid, wrong-type and out-of-range values and verified a valid fill. A separate reviewed-fill fixture exercised the one-use grant and refused replay. Date validation, answer-review and UI checks and web-app Clippy passed. No local vision model or employer site was tested for this field; localized visual representations can differ and require manual inspection. No application was submitted.

## Document extraction response recovery — 2026-11-01

- Completed extraction status reads can now be repeated until the 60-second expiry or another extraction starts after collection. The document interfaces retry a transient polling error at most twice; server errors are surfaced immediately. A new extraction can replace a collected result, so recovery is not guaranteed if another request starts first. Results remain in memory only, and expiration is checked on later requests.
- Focused worker tests covered repeated status reads and replacement; simulated interface checks covered transient failure, retry exhaustion and server refusal. The concurrent-review UI check and web-app Clippy passed. No live model, employer page or application submission was involved.

## Document extraction handoff — 2026-11-01

- A completed document extraction can no longer be overwritten by a new request before collection. One result is retained until it is collected or becomes eligible for expiry after 60 seconds; expiry is checked on the next extraction request or poll, not by a background timer. A conflicting request receives an explicit message. A rejected request also no longer invalidates a prior document review in the same interface.
- Focused worker tests covered retention, expiry and starting another extraction after collection. A simulated interface test covered a conflicting request while the first review was pending; document-selection checks and web-app Clippy passed. No live model or employer site was involved, and no application was sent.

## Partial navigation advice — 2026-11-01

- If the supplementary navigation pass fails, the app retains validated first-pass suggestions and explicitly warns that navigation advice is incomplete. A failed first pass still fails the request. Results are still checked against the live page state before use; advice does not click controls.
- A scripted second-pass failure and the UI warning were tested; a browser fixture also verified the partial-result flag and rejection after a page change. Web-app Clippy passed. Before this change, qwen3.5:9b passed two fictional overlay and multilingual submission-label checks on the preceding commit, with minimum sampled available RAM of 18.9 GiB. The new failure path was not induced with a live model. No application was sent.

## Navigation advice behind overlays — 2026-11-01

- When an inspected page reports an obscured field, control advice now always performs the separate navigation pass, even if the first pass returned five choices. Each pass remains limited to five model-selected controls; duplicates are removed, yielding at most ten suggestions. Advice remains read-only and must be reviewed. The second model call adds latency and its failure still surfaces as an error rather than partial advice.
- A scripted regression covered a full first pass, duplicate removal and an additional section control; the UI check displayed a sixth suggestion. Focused control-ranking tests and web-app Clippy passed. This change was not re-evaluated with a live model or employer page, and no application was sent.

## Local control advice refinement — 2026-11-01

- Navigation advice now asks the local model to exclude controls that edit field values or upload documents. On one read-only inspection of a public Workable application, the resident qwen3.5:9b initially suggested a phone-country selector alongside cookie options; after the prompt change it suggested an education control and four cookie options, without the phone selector or submission control. These are individual observations, not a general accuracy guarantee. Advice is displayed for user review and does not click controls.
- The same 9B model passed two fictional checks covering combined review-and-submit controls, including German and Spanish labels. Two deterministic control-ranking tests, the control-advice UI checks, web-app Clippy, and formatting checks passed. The public-page run clicked only controls in Waypoint, not on the employer site; it did not fill or submit an application. Minimum sampled available system RAM was 18.27 GiB. The app's GPU admission check declined the installed 12B model with 10,422 MiB free against its 11,229 MiB estimate; the 9B model was unloaded after testing.

# Implementation checkpoint

## Overlay control advice — 2026-11-01

- The local control adviser can now suggest visible overlay controls alongside section navigation, without clicking or choosing consent preferences. On a public Workable page, an isolated CPU run of `gemma4:12b-it-q4_K_M` identified the cookie settings, accept and decline controls and did not suggest the submit control. In a fictional mixed-action case, two single-pass prompt variants selected the cookie option but omitted a review-navigation control. When the app detects an obscured field it now makes one additional bounded navigation pass; a rerun selected both useful controls and neither of the two submission controls. The final two-pass Workable check again identified the cookie controls without suggesting submit. It took 47.8 seconds after preload with at least 15.49 GiB available RAM; the model was unloaded. These small checks do not establish general model reliability, and the user must decide and act in the visible browser. A CAPTCHA remained unresolved; nothing was clicked, filled or submitted. Native logs are under `target/model-eval/isolated-gemma-workable-overlay*` and `target/model-eval/isolated-gemma-overlay-mixed-actions*`.
- The revised control panel was also rendered with mocked backend responses at 1440×900 and 390×844; its navigation/overlay section opened without browser errors or horizontal overflow. Screenshots remain under `target/reopen-visible-*`.

## Real-backend visible handoff — 2026-11-01

- An isolated fictional workspace exercised the actual web backend and a public Workable application page: a headless inspection was opened, the rendered handoff action closed it, and a visible inspection of the same listing opened and was then closed. The backend reported an active visible session, and the recorded form API requests contained no fill or submit operation. No candidate data was entered. The application and its browser processes were stopped after the check; available RAM was 25.01 GiB afterward. Peak or minimum free memory was not measured. The page still showed a cookie overlay and CAPTCHA, so this does not establish that the application can be completed. Screenshots and the local harness are retained under `target/visible-handoff-real-backend-*` and `target/visible-handoff-backend-check.cjs`.

## Rendered visible-browser handoff — 2026-11-01

- A local rendered fixture exercised the headless-to-visible handoff at 1440×900 and 390×844. Mocked API responses showed the expected close request followed by a visible inspection request, with no page errors or horizontal overflow. Button spacing was adjusted after visual inspection. This did not launch the real application backend or an employer browser, so it does not validate actual session reopening. Screenshots and the local harness are retained under `target/reopen-visible-*`.

## Visible-browser handoff — 2026-11-01

- When a headless form needs manual review, the panel now offers an explicit action to close that session and start a visible inspection of the original listing. It warns that unsaved site changes and panel answers may be lost, blocks the action during filling or visual review and after filling, and uses the existing pending-inspection recovery path if reopening fails. Simulated interface tests covered success, close failure, start failure and the fill guards. This has not been exercised in a rendered browser or against a live employer; it does not interact with or submit the form.

## Workable page and obscured-field guidance — 2026-11-01

- An eight-second settled Crawl4AI capture of a public Workable application returned substantive page content (native capture SHA-256 `7f19caad51b1611d6a1fcc9b709677111ac018496eed5fe4fd99bc324b76d3dc`). An isolated CPU run of `gemma4:12b-it-q4_K_M` inspected the live form with fictional profile values and suggested a phone number and LinkedIn URL; a salary question, CAPTCHA, required hidden control and custom choices remained for review. A viewport capture (SHA-256 `526eb4a3636f83f8bfda8283775bde2bdf6635c8a3fd754754c0ff4c9b425366`) showed a cookie overlay obscuring the name and email fields. The initial screenshot test used a path relative to the wrong working directory and failed; an absolute-path retry succeeded.
- Form inspection now explains when controls may be obscured, clipped or transparent and directs the user to inspect overlays in the visible browser. Fictional browser fixtures and a read-only Workable recheck passed; the latter reported 11 fields, one incomplete custom-choice inventory, the new guidance and a CAPTCHA wall. The local-model run took 45.7 seconds after preload with at least 13.82 GiB RAM available; the model was unloaded. These observations do not establish successful filling or general model reliability. No values were entered and no application was submitted. Evidence remains under `target/research/` and `target/model-eval/isolated-gemma-workable-preview-20261101`.

## Application-check destination consistency — 2026-11-01

- A fictional cross-origin redirect to a page containing both a form and embedded content exposed a path that reported fields without first checking the redirect. The application checker now validates the initial destination on every path and checks that the page address remains stable through evidence collection and local-model inspection. The failing redirect fixture passed after the fix, as did local changed-page, embedded-form and custom-control fixtures, 14 non-ignored application-check tests and web Clippy. These checks cannot detect every same-address content change or a navigation away and back between checks. No employer application was submitted.

## Reviewed date and time controls — 2026-11-01

- The form panel now displays typed date, month and time suggestions using their actual serialized values and native input types. A simulated panel test exercised both a manually entered time and typed suggestions. A fictional browser test passed the reviewed time through answer preparation and a one-use grant, verified the retained value, and rejected grant replay; it did not submit an application. Current model profile mapping does not suggest these date/time values automatically.

## Native time visual review — 2026-11-01

- A fictional reviewed-fill browser fixture now verifies that a time answer is prepared through the review path, filled under a one-use grant, and cannot replay that grant. In an isolated CPU run, `gemma4:12b-it-q4_K_M` transcribed the displayed time as `01:30 PM` while the native value was `13:30`, initially producing a mismatch. The comparison now reconciles unambiguous 12-hour displays only for completed native time fields, then still verifies the live DOM value. A rerun produced a transcription match and live-value match; available RAM stayed at or above 16.47 GiB. A separate fictional test rejected ambiguous labels, left text fields unchanged, and refused confirmation after the live time changed. These observations do not establish general model accuracy. Evidence is retained under `target/model-eval/isolated-gemma-native-time-20261101` and `target/model-eval/isolated-gemma-native-time-normalized-20261101`.
- Hosted CI for `236726e` passed browser end-to-end and agent-tool checks but failed Clippy on a single-element loop in the new fixture; that loop was removed. Its supply-chain audit also reported three vulnerabilities and five denied warnings. The local browser and web Clippy checks passed after the fixture change; hosted results for the subsequent commit remain to be checked.

## Reviewed native time input — 2026-11-01

- Reviewed form answers now support native time fields at minute precision (`HH:MM`). The browser checks the control type, site limits and validity, then verifies the retained value. A fictional browser fixture rejected invalid and out-of-range values and filled `13:30` without submission; the review API rejects malformed times. Other special native controls still need manual handling. Seconds and fractional seconds are not supported, and visual transcription may differ from the stored 24-hour value.

## Native special form controls — 2026-11-01

- Native time, week, datetime-local, range and color inputs are now identified for manual review instead of being eligible for text mapping. Their labels and required status are reported, while ordinary fields remain reviewable. Local browser and preview fixtures and Clippy passed without entering values or submitting. These control types are not yet automatically filled.

## Web test suite and model-status fixture — 2026-11-01

- After the shared-URL migration, a full web test run reported 429 passed, 216 ignored and one intermittent model-capability failure; the failed test passed alone. Its mock server could close a connection after reading only part of a split HTTP request. The fixture now consumes the declared body and checks the request path; a subsequent full run passed 430 tests with 216 ignored, and Clippy passed. This fixes a plausible fixture race but does not prove it caused the observed failure or establish live-model reliability.

## Shared application URL duplicate review — 2026-11-01

- Duplicate discovery now considers exact nonempty saved application URLs even when employers or titles differ or an employer is missing. A match creates a review pair, never an automatic merge; generic URLs can refer to different roles. Migration 40 queues existing shared-URL records for bounded rechecking, and a URL change invalidates the changed record's duplicate index. Searches remain capped at 200 candidates and report bounded coverage when the cap is reached. Store tests and the SQLCipher migration fixture passed; this is not a guarantee that all duplicates will be found.

## Listing inspection address checks — 2026-11-01

- Listing inspection now rechecks the page address after structured-data and text extraction, model inference, capture and contact collection. An address change stops the inspection rather than saving the result against the earlier URL. A local browser fixture covers navigation to another page. Same-address content changes and navigation away and back between checks remain possible.

## Listing capture consistency — 2026-11-01

- Listing captures now check the page address and dimensions between tiles and compare a fresh first tile before calling the screenshot set complete. If the capture changes, the partial screenshots remain available, but page text is not attributed to that capture. A local wide-page fixture verifies stable capture, address mismatch and dimension mismatch. These checks cannot detect every change in another tile, hidden content or nested scrolling areas.

## Read-only Ashby application preview — 2026-11-01

- The public Ashby board API returned 74 listings (raw response SHA-256 `5cd3b481cf94f61fbda8d83570dc4977726fc676c11f5cf47352ce4dc677fafb`). The selected Engineering Manager application page initially produced an empty crawler extraction; a new optional, bounded post-load wait produced substantive content after five seconds (native capture SHA-256 `7c0af163a784b3926123ca13ddfd54181d3b2bcdb952a6f9db62ff9997f7a2ca`). The unsuccessful initial capture is retained separately. These counts and the posting are point-in-time observations.
- Waypoint's browser detected nine fields, one incomplete custom-choice inventory and a CAPTCHA wall. An isolated CPU run of `gemma4:12b-it-q4_K_M` with a fictional profile suggested name, email and LinkedIn and flagged four other detected text fields for manual review, including three open-ended experience questions. The page contains other questions not offered as ordinary detected fields; this is incomplete form coverage. The preview test took 51.81 seconds after preload, with at least 16.05 GiB available RAM. No values were entered, no choices clicked, no files uploaded and no application submitted. Native evidence is retained under `target/research/` and `target/model-eval/isolated-gemma-ashby-preview-20261101`.

## Agent tools and Rust coverage — 2026-11-01

- Added repository-local agent skills, an isolated source-bound CocoIndex adapter,
  and an isolated, pinned Crawl4AI source-build and capture workflow. The crawler
  installed in a Waypoint-only cache, passed its local doctor and 19 transferred
  package-contract checks, and captured a public documentation page with robots
  checking and a native receipt. The CocoIndex adapter indexed and searched a
  one-file fictional repository using a read-only existing CLI and a hash-checked
  model; an earlier attempt using the default symlinked model cache failed on

  with 42,118 hit lines of 71,103 instrumented lines across 124 source records.

  The new OIDC Codecov workflow and README badge have not yet been verified on
  GitHub or Codecov; a local report is not evidence of a hosted upload.

## Local match repeatability — 2026-11-01

The qualification test took 221.66 seconds after preload; available RAM stayed at or above 13.8 GiB during the test. This measures repeatability on one case, not accuracy, calibration, general reliability or production GPU behavior. The test harness permits the isolated CPU runtime only when explicitly configured; production model eligibility is unchanged. Native output is under `target/model-eval/isolated-gemma-fit-repeatability-20261101`. Lint passed.

## Two-step fictional application with Gemma — 2026-11-01

- The local-model wizard test now supports an explicitly isolated CPU benchmark runtime and verifies that both model-assisted mapping requests complete without the error fallback. On a fictional two-step form (fixture SHA-256 `6add62b14f2b6694ca3bf83c9567658421ebe85ed7d8597118f0e99e3eb4091b`), `gemma4:12b-it-q4_K_M` and the existing mapping pipeline prepared reviewed values, the browser filled and verified name and email, the work-authorization field remained empty, and vision checks read the filled values. Native output is under `target/model-eval/isolated-gemma-wizard-model-required-20261101`.

## Focused native-choice visual review — 2026-11-01

- On a fictional browser datalist (fixture SHA-256 `349daa309d0eb70c02f376b9b5dbe20c09a0206e88330d259222ae29138c9c65`), `gemma4:12b-it-q4_K_M` first read the selected value but appended an empty section separator to its label, leaving the match unobserved. The focused prompt now instructs the model to use a separator only between visible headings and field labels. On rerun, the selected value was visually matched and checked against the live control; the blank changing field was not confirmed.
- The isolated CPU test took 17.82 seconds after preload, with at least 17.66 GiB RAM available during the run. A separate regression run passed the full-page, focused-heading and control-only section checks in 54.36 seconds, with at least 17.09 GiB available; RAM returned to 27.0 GiB after cleanup. Focused unit tests and lint passed. These fictional cases do not establish general model reliability. ## Focused visual-review prompt — 2026-11-01

- A fictional sectioned form exposed prompt contamination: when the requested qualified label was included in the focused prompt, `gemma4:12b-it-q4_K_M` returned that label even for an image containing only the control and no visible label or heading. The focused prompt now asks for visible fields without supplying the requested label; matching to the expected label happens afterward. A mock-service test checks that the requested label is absent from the prompt.
- In an isolated CPU rerun of that fixture, the model matched the target when its section headings were visible and the control-only crop remained unobserved. The three-observation live test took 51.43 seconds after preload, with at least 17.09 GiB available RAM and 27.2 GiB after cleanup. Focused visual-review tests and lint passed. This is one fictional case and does not guarantee that a model will not invent labels; visual observations remain advisory and live checks remain required. Native output is under `target/model-eval/isolated-gemma-section-unbiased-20261101`.

## Repeated visual labels — 2026-11-01

- The previous section-prefix matching change also made distinct, fully qualified labels with the same final field name unobserved. The matcher now accepts exact, unique qualified labels while still refusing an unqualified label when its leaf could refer to multiple fields. Focused ambiguity tests and lint passed.
- In an isolated CPU run, `gemma4:12b-it-q4_K_M` correctly distinguished both email values and their section headings in the full-page view of a fictional local browser form (fixture SHA-256 `ecd6533ec14df16410ad152cef70cd09d854abbd006fefbd5f4490da263920f4`). Captured output is under `target/model-eval/isolated-gemma-section-form-20261101`.

## Dense form visual comparison — 2026-11-01

## PDF page-boundary review — 2026-11-01

- The local PDF worker now counts selectable character boxes extending more than 0.5 PDF points beyond the intersection of the media and crop boxes. A nonzero or unavailable count keeps the document for manual review, and the interface explains the warning. Older reports without this observation also need review. This is an advisory geometry check; it does not detect every form of clipping or assess nonselectable content.
- A fictional three-page fixture exercised an unclipped page, text beyond a page edge, and text outside a crop box. The local PDF worker, focused review tests, interface checks and lint passed. No vision model or real application was used in this check.

## Styled DOCX conversion fixture — 2026-11-01

- A fictional styled DOCX was edited across text runs and converted with local LibreOffice. The one-page PDF's extracted text contained the replacement and omitted the original; a manually inspected render retained the visible mixed text styles. The fixture test now checks PDF text extraction as well as DOCX text. The source SHA-256 was `8edfc3c730cd4852d421a074ed408cf8f288d368db962833ef5d42944bc92d58` and the manually inspected PDF SHA-256 was `f80ff80ae414ddc527a4493e210144eefc0e26b75a63ecdec4fdf368d1f9c9b9`. This is one synthetic template, not a local-model visual review or evidence of general template fidelity.

## Text-to-PDF fixture — 2026-11-01

- A Windows local test converted fictional UTF-8 text with LibreOffice, then extracted its accented names and rendered its single PDF page with the configured PDF worker. The test now keeps its result in memory instead of overwriting a fixed output file. The run passed in 16.31 seconds on this host; it is not a visual-quality assessment or a clean-host installation test.

## Document visual-review completeness — 2026-11-01

- Attachment follow-up now treats a visual report as incomplete when its overall completion flag, inspected-page count, or error state conflicts with the page findings. Seven focused document-visual tests passed. On Windows, the PDF worker (pypdf 6.10.0, pypdfium2 5.14.0, Pillow 12.3.0) rendered a fictional two-page PDF and flagged its intentionally overlapping text for follow-up, including when a simulated model pass failed. The vision-model test was not run; these fixtures do not establish general visual quality or model accuracy.

## Incomplete duplicate decisions — 2026-11-01

- A duplicate pair marked as the same without a valid preferred listing, or with an unrecognized status, holds both jobs and is presented as pending with an explanation. A new explicit decision is accepted only at the current revision; stale and replayed decisions are refused. Store tests covered these cases and normal same/different decisions; workspace tests and existing mocked duplicate-review UI checks passed. A rendered fictional UI fixture showed the explanation, a simulated stale-decision error, and a refreshed decision using the new revision at desktop size; the mobile view had no horizontal overflow. That fixture mocked the API. A separate endpoint test used a temporary plaintext fictional workspace and verified the state response, stale refusal, accepted decision, replay refusal and final holds against SQLite. This does not guarantee detection of every duplicate.

The endpoint test adds a direct test-only dependency on the already locked `rusqlite` package; the lockfile change adds only that dependency edge. On 2026-11-01 the required `cargo audit --deny warnings --ignore RUSTSEC-2026-0192` reported three `zip` advisories (RUSTSEC-2026-0367, -0368 and -0369) and five denied warnings; `cargo deny check licenses bans sources` also failed its license policy. These package versions were already in the lockfile before the test dependency was added. No remediation or clean CI claim is made here.

## Changing form during read-only inspection — 2026-11-01

- A Greenhouse page intermittently replaced a DOM node during the initial read, causing inspection to fail. Initial reads and later current-page rescans now retry that specific stale-node error once without reloading and add a blocking manual-review warning; a second failure still surfaces. A disappearing custom choice also leaves its inventory incomplete. Other protocol failures are not converted to success.
- A unit test covered the retry, retained CAPTCHA wall and failure paths; fictional changing-control and form-preview browser fixtures passed. A subsequent read-only visit to the same public page passed; a separate visit reported 10 detected fields and 14 collapsed custom choices with an incomplete inventory. The live visit does not prove the retry was exercised, and CAPTCHA and custom controls still require manual review. No values were entered or submitted.
