## Search-constraint review cues — 2026-11-01

- Jobs with unresolved search constraints show the specific reason on the card and a review cue in the selected-job panel, with details under the match explanation. A redundant card badge was removed after rendered review. Mocked JavaScript checks cover the selected-panel cue. A fictional, intercepted-API browser fixture rendered the interface at 1440×900 and 390×844; the cue appeared, keyboard activation opened the explanation and selected the job, and no horizontal overflow or browser errors were observed. This was not a live backend or employer-site test.

## Local model capability identity — 2026-11-01

- The displayed vision-capability result now requires the same valid resident-model digest before and after the capability lookup, in addition to the existing name and resource checks. Missing or changed digests leave capability unknown. Fictional local-service tests passed for stable, changed and missing digests; this remains a snapshot and does not reserve the model or prove a capability lookup describes the resident weights.

## Salary currency comparison — 2026-11-01

- Salary constraints compare three-letter currency codes without case or surrounding-space differences. Missing or malformed codes remain unknown; basis, period and compensation category still have to match. The search suite and 46 workspace tests passed; network tests were not run.

## Listing freshness — 2026-11-01

- Future observations, an invalid current time, and negative freshness windows now report unknown instead of fresh or stale. Boundary and clock-skew cases passed in the search tests; the workspace suite also passed (46 tests, with two network tests ignored across its targets).

## Blank search constraints — 2026-11-01

- Empty language and location entries no longer exclude listings. Language codes are trimmed before comparison, and an employer language list containing only blank entries remains unknown when a language is required. All 19 search-crate tests passed.

## Sponsorship constraint — 2026-11-01

- A candidate who does not require sponsorship no longer excludes employers that offer it. When sponsorship is required, an offer passes, a stated absence fails, and missing information remains unknown. The search crate's 18 tests passed, including all combinations of the candidate setting and employer status.

## Bounded visual cross-check — 2026-11-01

- The live-value checks following a visual review stop starting new checks after four seconds and use the browser's one-second per-command review timeout. Remaining visual matches are reported as unconfirmed. A fictional driver test covered an exhausted budget and an earlier verification failure. Individual browser operations can contain multiple commands, so four seconds is not a hard wall-clock deadline. No local model or employer site was used.
- The URL, dimensions and screenshot recaptures around that check now also use the short per-command timeout. The visual-review unit selection passed 22 tests (10 ignored). The local browser endpoint fixture intermittently failed its two-segment assertion: a retained synthetic event log showed Chromium's measured width changing from 1243 to 1258 pixels during capture, followed by a first-tile mismatch on retry. Five subsequent diagnostic runs passed before another failure. The trigger for the mismatch is not established, and capture correctly reported uncertainty rather than a complete record. These checks do not establish a hard total timeout or live-site behavior.
- A later retained failing fixture showed that the first two changed tile pairs differed by three pixels each near a focused control; two other pairs differed by six and eight pixels. Hiding the fixture's text caret did not eliminate the failure (one initial pass, two further passes, then failure), so the cause remains unresolved. The caret experiment was reverted and production image comparison remains strict.
- The final tile recheck now records whether the address, dimensions or first tile changed. A dimensions change at that stage can use the existing one-time fresh-capture retry; an address or content change still stops the capture. A classification test and a local changing-page fixture passed. This does not resolve the intermittent first-tile mismatch or establish that retries succeed on real pages.

## Focused visual review of choices — 2026-11-01

- Focused visual review now asks the local model to report checkbox, radio and select states as well as entered text, and explicitly excludes placeholders. A fictional local service test verified the request and comparison path. The modified prompt has not been evaluated with a real model, and visual transcription remains advisory and subject to live-value cross-checks and manual review.

## Text-only datalist options — 2026-11-01

- Native datalist options without a value attribute can be reviewed and selected when their value is a single unambiguous text node. The driver checks the live option value and rechecks it after input events; ambiguous whitespace or markup still requires manual review. The existing datalist review warning remains because retained text does not prove a site's internal selection state.
- A fictional local-browser fixture exercised text-only choices, whitespace ambiguity and a choice that changes during input. A separate reviewed-prefill fixture exercised a text-only option through a one-use grant and verified its retained value. These fixtures and the browser unit suite passed; no real employer site or local model was tested.

## Native datalist choices — 2026-10-31

- Standard text inputs backed by an unambiguous, bounded HTML datalist can now be offered as reviewed choices. Waypoint selects only an explicitly listed value, sends input and change events, and rechecks the value and options. Duplicate IDs or values, disabled choices, custom ARIA comboboxes, and changed or invalid controls remain manual. A manual-review warning remains because a site's internal selection state may not be visible from the input value.
- Fictional local fixtures verified selection, page event handling, rejection of invalid or changed lists, a shadow-root datalist, and reviewed web-app prefill. The local browser suite passed 47 tests before the final shadow fixture extension, which passed separately; the SQLCipher-enabled web suite passed 301. No live site or local model was tested.

## Mixed embedded scopes — 2026-10-31

- The existing four-scope inspection budget can now be used along one nested path instead of stopping at three. Frame and open-shadow selectors retain every ancestor identity and visibility check. If another relevant scope cannot be inspected within the limit, the form and application checker report that gap for manual review.
- Fictional local-browser fixtures covered shadow-to-frame-to-shadow and a four-scope shadow/frame chain, including hidden-ancestor and replaced-frame refusal. A fifth-scope fixture reported incomplete inspection. The reviewed web-app prefill path exercised the mixed forms. The local browser suite passed 46 tests and the SQLCipher-enabled web suite passed 301; live employers and local-model interpretation were not tested.

## Open web-component form controls — 2026-10-31

- The browser can inspect bounded open shadow roots and bind field selectors to each host and root. It can fill and verify supported native controls there; closed roots with apparent form controls remain manual review. Web-component content is flagged for review, and visual page captures are not described as complete when such content is present. Decorative open roots without form controls or nested scopes do not consume the four-scope inspection budget.
- Fictional local-browser fixtures exercised text, dropdown, checkbox, radio and PDF attachment controls, nested open roots, host hiding and replacement, and a closed root. A reviewed web-app prefill test covered the open-root field. The 43-test browser suite passed before the attachment fixture extension; that extension passed separately. The 301-test SQLCipher-enabled web suite passed. Mixed frame-and-shadow scopes, real employer sites and local-model accuracy remain unverified.

## Process-isolated embedded form detection — 2026-10-31

- Waypoint can temporarily attach a separate browser protocol session to an isolated frame target identified in the controlled page's DOM. It checks only whether the target document appears to contain form controls, binds the observation to the frame and loader, and detaches. It does not return or fill the isolated fields. Unavailable targets and the four-target inspection limit are explicitly reported for manual review; CAPTCHA and other blocking walls remain blocking.
- A fictional sandboxed frame, both application-review entry points, and a five-frame budget fixture were exercised locally. The local browser suite passed 40 tests before the additional budget fixture; that fixture passed separately. The SQLCipher-enabled web suite passed 300 tests. No live employer site, local model, or cross-origin autofill was tested.

## Detectable inaccessible embedded forms — 2026-10-30

- When the browser protocol exposes a visible frame document that the embedding page cannot access, Waypoint can flag apparent form controls without offering those fields for autofill. Application inspection keeps that page for manual review rather than automatically following an alternate link. Listing extraction retains the frame warnings while CAPTCHA and other blocking walls still stop extraction.
- Fictional local tests exercised separate origins on the same host, the application-check and form-inspection paths, and a sandboxed process-isolated frame. Process-isolated frames whose DOM is absent from the parent protocol session remain uninspected and may still be indistinguishable from decorative content; no cross-origin filling was added and no live site was tested.

## Preference warnings on job match badges — 2026-10-30

- Job-list estimates now indicate a possible preference conflict or that preferences need review when the preference audit, comparison, limits, or unknown judgments leave a gap. This is a cue to inspect the assessment, not proof that the model found every preference. Current saved assessments have this cue recomputed on read; the batch response carries only the status, not the user's instruction text.
- Tests cover missing and unknown criteria, conflicting judgments, audit limits, saved assessment handling, the batch endpoint and badge wording. These use fictional data and do not measure local-model accuracy.

## Recovering missed job preferences in fit assessments — 2026-10-30

- When job-search instructions are present, an additional bounded model pass checks for up to three omitted job-selection preferences. Valid exact excerpts enter the rubric as unknown, and only the existing separate listing comparison can establish a supported or contradicted judgment. Invalid or unavailable results leave the original rubric intact; an assessment with instructions but no extracted preferences carries an explicit review warning.
- The fit input version changed so assessments generated by the earlier workflow are reported stale. Scripted tests cover omitted preferences, invented and duplicate excerpts, a full fictional comparison, and the interface status. The resident local model was not evaluated because the GPU was already occupied; the new pass can still miss or misinterpret instructions.

## Compact model references for embedded fields — 2026-10-30

- The form-mapping and option-selection prompts use short collision-checked identifiers for embedded fields. Model responses are translated back to their original frame-bound selectors before validation and filling; guessed internal paths are not accepted as supplied identifiers. Manual-review selections use the same translation.
- Scripted inference tests cover translation, collisions, rejected guessed paths and dropdown options. A scripted response was also exercised against a real local browser fixture. No actual local model was loaded: the host GPU already had about 14 GiB of dedicated memory in use. Model accuracy or improvement from this prompt change has not been measured.

## Checking other answers after visual navigation — 2026-10-30

- Visual focus rechecks the selected answer first, then other completed fields. It reports verified, failed and unchecked counts, stops after a failure, and records a manual-review reason if any field fails or is left unchecked. The pass checks at most 32 fields and stops starting new checks after four seconds; individual browser protocol calls have a one-second timeout during the pass.
- A fictional local page changed a different filled field during scrolling; an HTTP/browser test confirmed it was identified while the selected field remained intact. A unit test covers the 32-field cap and early-failure accounting. This is a point-in-time check, not a guarantee against later site changes.

## Checking answers after visual navigation — 2026-10-30

- After scrolling to a completed field, Waypoint rechecks its live value against the reviewed answer. An unavailable or changed value creates a manual-review reason and is reported alongside the screenshot; this does not guarantee the site will keep the value later. The shared verification path covers supported text, date and native choice fields.
- A fictional browser and HTTP fixture verified both an unchanged answer and a page that clears the answer on scroll. The web UI checks include the changed-value message. No live employer page or vision model was used.

## Bringing filled fields into view — 2026-10-30

- After a successful prefill, the review panel offers a list of fields filled in that session. The selected field can be scrolled into view and captured without Waypoint typing into it; the site may react to scrolling; the server checks the current form schema and review walls before scrolling and rejects fields outside the completed set. A fresh visual review can then be run, and captures are saved to the attempt record when available.
- Outer-page capture coverage is no longer marked complete when an embedded-frame review warning is present. An actual local-browser fixture verified scrolling inside a frame, and an HTTP integration test checked the completed-field restriction and retained answer. The UI handler's success and error states were tested. The rendered interface at multiple widths and local-model interpretation of this capture have not been verified.

## Nested embedded forms — 2026-10-30

- The form reader can inspect accessible visible frames up to three levels deep, with at most four embedded documents per inspection. Field selectors carry each ancestor frame and document identity; every level is checked again before a field action. Uninspected, inaccessible and deeper frames remain a manual-review concern.
- Fictional local-browser tests verified a two-level field, refusal when the ancestor is hidden, and rejection after either the inner or outer document is replaced. A reviewed web-app prefill test also filled the nested field. Three-level behavior, live employer sites and visual-model accuracy have not been verified.

## Embedded controls and reviewed prefill — 2026-10-30

- Hidden or inaccessible frames no longer consume the four-document inspection allowance. Frame discovery stores identifiers instead of copying every embedded tree, and a time budget bounds inspection. Dropdown labels no longer absorb the text of their options when the label wraps the control.
- A fictional local browser fixture fills and verifies text, date, dropdown, checkbox, radio and file controls in a visible frame preceded by five hidden frames. A second local test exercises the web app's reviewed prefill grant and verifies the embedded value. The frame still requires manual review; this does not establish live-site or model-vision coverage.

## Embedded form fields — 2026-10-30

- Accessible visible first-level frames can now contribute fields to the reviewed form. Frame-scoped selectors bind the embedding element and its document identity, and are refused after the document changes or the frame becomes inaccessible or hidden. Up to four frame documents are inspected and the existing review warning remains. Inaccessible and nested frames still require manual handling. The protocol limits individual messages to 16 MiB; oversized DOM responses fail rather than being treated as complete.
- A fictional local-browser fixture checks same-origin text fill and retention, isolation, document replacement and hidden-frame refusal. This is not evidence of live-site coverage or visual-model inspection.

## Preserve an embedded application page — 2026-10-30

- Form inspection and application checking now stay on the current page when it has both main-page fields and an uninspected visible frame, instead of following a separate application-looking link automatically. Form inspection reports that the page should be reviewed before navigating. This is a structural safeguard, not proof the current page is an application; unrelated fields and decorative frames can require extra review. A page with no detected main fields can still follow a single permitted link.
- Two fictional local-browser tests checked both inspection paths, and a fake-driver test checked the no-main-fields case. The web suite passed (292 passed, 121 ignored); web Clippy and formatting passed. Embedded contents are still not read or filled, and no live employer site or local model was exercised. No application was sent.

## Embedded-frame review — 2026-10-30

- Form inspection now flags a rendered, nonzero embedded frame as potentially containing uninspected application content. Hidden and zero-sized frames do not trigger this cue in the tested fixture. The cue is a manual-review obligation; separately visible main-page fields can still be suggested and filled after user review, while a changed set of review walls requires reinspection. Listing inspection can retain a listing with this cue and records an extraction warning. CAPTCHA and other blocking walls remain blocking.
- A fictional local-browser fixture verified the visible-frame warning, a main-page field fill and the disappearance of the warning after the visible frame was hidden. The full local browser fixture suite passed (35 tests), and the web suite passed (291 passed, 119 ignored); web and browser Clippy and formatting passed. No iframe contents were read or filled, and decorative visible frames can produce a review warning. No live employer site or local model was exercised; no application was sent.

## Listing redirect provenance — 2026-10-30

- New listing captures retain both the URL requested for inspection and the final captured URL after an allowed same-origin redirect. Application `original-job.json` records the requested URLs from screenshot capture and source-only metadata separately, because those records may come from different captures. Older captures without that field report it as unknown; no historical URL is invented. The saved job still uses the reached URL, and cross-origin redirects still require manual review.
- A fictional loopback redirect and real local browser verified the two capture URLs; a separate archive fixture checked that two independently saved source records keep their requested URLs distinct. The web suite passed (290 passed, 119 ignored), web Clippy and formatting passed with one Cargo build job. No live job board or employer site was exercised, and no application was sent.

## Repeated search-result provenance — 2026-10-30

- A batch search now retains up to ten references per page for exact URLs seen on earlier pages, including the later provider-reported title, engine labels and publication text. The page reports the total repeated count and omitted-reference count, and the web interface exposes the retained references without adding them as new candidate entries. These references are bounded and unverified; this is exact-URL provenance, not detection of similar vacancies across different URLs or proof of job identity.
- A fictional local provider test checked that repeated URLs were not added as candidates and that later engine attribution and omitted counts survived. The web suite passed (290 passed, 118 ignored); web Clippy, formatting and search UI checks passed. A Playwright component fixture at 1200×900 and 375×850 checked expansion, keyboard closure and a long URL without horizontal overflow; the Browser plugin was unavailable. No live provider, local model or employer site was exercised for this change, and no application was sent.

## Cancelled batch-search worker — 2026-10-30

- When batch search is cancelled while a completed page occupies the one-slot result channel, the worker now stops waiting to send its completion message. The buffered page remains available to a later poll, which reports cancellation and unattempted pages after the worker disconnects. The task itself remains available until the client polls it; this change does not add automatic job import or expand provider coverage.
- A bounded-channel test verified worker exit, buffered-page recovery and coverage accounting; all 10 batch-search tests passed. The web suite passed (289 passed, 118 ignored), web Clippy and formatting passed with one Cargo build job. No live search provider or local model was used for this change; GPU usage was approximately 14.8 GiB before the work, so no additional model was loaded. No application was sent.

## Protect active form-review markers — 2026-10-30

- Review endpoints now reject clearing the pending inspection/session marker while its worker, browser session or navigation worker is active, and reject clearing all review reasons during that active work. Other individual reasons remain independently resolvable. Once the task or session is no longer active, a retained marker can be reviewed and cleared normally. The navigation worker also protects its own pending marker while it holds the browser session.
- A fictional local-browser handler test verified that active markers and bulk clearing are refused, another reason can be resolved, and normal close clears its marker. The reviewed prefill fixture also passed with a real pending marker present. The web suite passed (288 passed, 118 ignored), web Clippy and formatting passed. This does not test a live employer site or a forced process crash; no application was sent.

## Review session interruption marker — 2026-10-30

- The inspection review marker now stays in the database while its browser session is open. A normal session close clears its exact revision; expiry or profile changes first save an interruption reason and then clear the marker. If the process or a navigation worker loses the session, the marker remains for manual review rather than implying the browser can be resumed. Old markers with the earlier inspection-only wording are not automatically rewritten.
- A fictional local-browser test exercised the real close handler and checked that an unrelated review reason remained. It also checked that an unclosed marker survived reopening the workspace; it did not forcibly crash a running process. The web suite passed (288 passed, 118 ignored); web Clippy and formatting passed. No employer application was sent.

## Interrupted initial form inspection — 2026-10-30

- Starting a form inspection saves a task-specific pending review reason before launching the worker. It remains visible after a workspace reopen if the process stops during inspection. Once a failure has a durable review reason, the matching pending reason is cleared by revision; unrelated or newer reasons are preserved. A failed worker start attempts to clear its own marker and reports a cleanup failure if that fails. The later session change above keeps the marker through a successful inspection until the session is closed or interrupted; the browser session itself remains in memory.
- A fictional workspace-reopen test checked persistence and revision-specific clearing; the existing disconnected-worker handler test now also checks replacement of the pending reason with a failure reason. The web suite passed (288 passed, 117 ignored), web Clippy and formatting passed with one Cargo build job. A separate worker test opened a fictional local form in a headless browser without filling it. No live employer form or forced process crash was tested, and no application was sent.

## Browser fixture and review assertions — 2026-10-30

- GitHub's browser job on `0132200` failed three local-fixture tests. Two assertions predated the current treatment of unavailable controls: the revised tests require the review warning and verify that disabled rich-text fields cannot be filled. The 404 fixture sometimes accepted an unrelated or empty browser connection and closed before the intended request; it now waits for the expected request and asserts an actual HTTP 404. A full local Edge run passed all 34 browser tests before a subsequent bounded request-reading improvement; the 404 test passed again after that improvement, and browser Clippy and formatting passed. This is local fixture coverage, not live employer-site qualification. No application was sent to an employer.
- The GitHub build-and-test job passed on `0132200`; the browser and existing supply-chain jobs failed. The supply-chain failure was not investigated in this round. The revised browser fixture has not yet been verified on GitHub.

## Web UI checks in CI — 2026-10-30

- CI now stops immediately when each of the first six web review checks fails, instead of allowing a later Node command to overwrite the failure exit code. It also runs the fit split and dimension checks. Enabling proper gating exposed a stale export-recovery fixture that did not include the selected-facts rendering helper; the fixture now loads the actual helper. All nine affected Node checks passed locally. This does not prove the hosted workflow or unrelated checks pass.

## Windows package copy verification — 2026-10-30

- The per-user installer includes hidden files when copying and verifies the installed executable, OpenSSL DLL and inventoried Python and LibreOffice files before creating a shortcut or reporting success. The additional hash pass increases installation time. A fictional Windows installer fixture reproduced the previous hidden-file omission and passed after the change; it also simulated post-copy dependency corruption and confirmed that installation fails instead of reporting success. A complete package on a clean Windows host was not exercised.

## Fit split in the web interface — 2026-10-30

- A local Playwright fixture served the actual web page, JavaScript and CSS with fictional mocked API responses. It loaded a saved `criteria-v2` assessment, opened its fit review, split the grouped C/C++ criterion, changed one judgment and verified the temporary 75% result while both displayed original AI badges stayed at 100%. The original judgment was disabled while split, and keyboard Enter closed the split and restored the 100% temporary result. Checks ran at 1440×900 and 375×900 with no observed page or console errors or narrow horizontal overflow. The first mock omitted `scoring_version`, so the panel correctly did not render rubric controls; adding the field used by actual assessments fixed the fixture.
- The Browser plugin was unavailable and the fixture used an existing local Playwright/Edge installation. This was a mocked frontend integration, not a live backend, local-model or third-party-site test; the temporary correction is not persisted. No application was sent.

## Temporary split of a fit criterion — 2026-10-30

- In the fit review panel, a user can temporarily replace one listing criterion with two non-overlapping exact excerpts from that criterion and choose a separate judgment for each. Both parts inherit the original importance, so splitting changes their combined weight; the revised estimate is a user-exploration scenario, not a saved correction and does not update the job badge. Parts start unknown, and the original judgment is disabled until the split is closed. Splits that are missing, overlap, or are not present in the original excerpt cannot be calculated. The existing 12-criterion limit also applies to the temporary result.
- Node checks covered the score and split controls. A local Playwright fixture using the actual component functions and CSS exercised invalid input, a valid C/C++ split, a changed judgment, and restoring the original at 1200×900 and 375×850; no page or console errors were observed and the narrow viewport had no horizontal overflow. The first fixture attempt omitted its UTF-8 declaration and failed on text matching; it passed after that fixture was corrected. This did not exercise the full application, persistence, keyboard-only navigation, or a live model. No application was sent.

## Possible grouped fit requirements — 2026-10-30

- The fit review now flags listing excerpts containing English `and` or `or`, a comma, or an ampersand as possibly grouped requirements. The existing concerns display shows the excerpt and asks for review. The cue does not split criteria or change a score: it can flag a single combined concept and miss groupings expressed in other languages or without those markers. Older assessments become stale through the changed input digest. It is not a substitute for semantic assessment.
- The web suite passed (287 passed, 117 ignored), as did web Clippy and formatting with one Cargo build job. A fictional test checked the cue, a word-boundary counterexample and unchanged scoring arithmetic. No live-model or browser-UI test was run for this change; the display uses the existing concerns path. No application was sent.

## Atomic fit criteria probes — 2026-10-30

- Four fictional listing-only probes with resident `gemma3:4b` extracted a nursing license and experience duration independently, but grouped German with English and C with C++. Adding explicit instructions and examples to split independent qualifications produced the same grouped output on those four inputs. A separate four-item review returned IDs 2 and 4 (the individual duration and license) rather than IDs 1 and 3 (the grouped languages and programming languages). These are small, single-run probes; grouped wording can sometimes describe one combined requirement, and this does not establish general model accuracy. No automatic splitting, score change, or model-review feature was added on this evidence. Fictional prompt and response receipts are under `target/model-eval/`.
- Each request was sequential, used the already GPU-resident model with a 4,096-token context, and passed a fresh system-memory and dedicated-GPU snapshot preflight. Snapshots are not peak measurements. The model was left to expire normally; no application was sent.

## Split fit extraction probe — 2026-10-30

- Three isolated fictional prompts explored separating criterion extraction from fit judgment using the resident `gemma3:4b` model (digest `c30276f7dffec93a536d283e15088990454b8d21cf180a3d6eea4959e4147291`, 4,096 context). Listing-only extraction returned the four named technical requirements separately; preference-only extraction returned the two explicit preferences. A combined listing-and-preference request grouped three skills into one criterion and omitted the cloud preference. These were single responses on one case, not repeatability or accuracy measurements, and did not exercise evidence matching, scoring, or the production workflow. No fallback was added because incomplete or grouped criteria can distort the score. Raw fictional prompts and responses are retained under `target/model-eval/`.
- Each probe used the already resident model and a resource preflight. A later snapshot showed 25.10 GiB free system memory and 10.10 GiB maximum dedicated adapter usage; peak usage was not measured. The model was left to its normal expiry. No application was sent.

## Small-model fit repeatability check — 2026-10-30

- In the existing three-run fictional repeatability test, local `gemma3:4b` (digest `c30276f7dffec93a536d283e15088990454b8d21cf180a3d6eea4959e4147291`, 4,096 context) returned no valid assessments after the bounded repair. Its responses included a Python fact as supposed SQL evidence, candidate facts as listing criteria, and non-null fact references for unknown judgments. Validation withheld every score. An experimental prompt that numbered facts and emphasized source rules also produced zero valid assessments in three runs and was discarded; this single case does not establish general model performance or test the overlap-specific repair.

Snapshots showed 28.11 GiB free system memory and 6.19 GiB maximum adapter usage before loading, 25.52 GiB and 10.10 GiB before the first test, and 25.49 GiB and 10.14 GiB after the second. These are snapshots, not peaks; the model was left to its normal expiry. No application was sent. Raw local test output was retained under `target/model-eval/`.

## Fit overlap repair feedback — 2026-10-30

- When a model-selected rubric contains overlapping excerpts, the single bounded repair request now identifies the conflicting excerpts as JSON strings, limited to 120 characters each. The input digest version changed so earlier assessments become stale. A controlled fixture confirms the repair request and a valid second response; it does not show whether a local model can repair the rubric or whether the resulting score is accurate.
- The web suite passed (286 passed, 117 ignored), as did web Clippy and formatting, using one Cargo build job. No model was resident, and the host's basic GPU query did not provide a reliable free-VRAM reading, so no live-model run was started. Peak resource use was not measured.

## Overlapping fit evidence — 2026-10-30

- Fit validation rejects two excerpts from the same source when every possible occurrence overlaps, including partial overlaps where neither excerpt contains the other. Separate occurrences remain eligible. Existing assessments become stale because the input digest version changed. This prevents one identifiable form of double counting, but can also reject distinct criteria phrased in overlapping passages; a revised rubric may be needed. Separate or semantically equivalent passages can still describe the same consideration, and model judgments remain unverified.
- With the Windows SQLCipher build helper and one Cargo build job, the fit-task tests passed (37 passed, 21 ignored). The full web suite initially exposed a stale draft fixture expecting three model calls despite the bounded evidence-check repair; after correcting that expectation, it passed (285 passed, 117 ignored). Web Clippy and formatting passed. No local model was loaded for this change, so it does not establish model accuracy or live repeatability.

## DOCX insertion formatting — 2026-10-29

- Exact DOCX edits that insert text without deleting the matched passage now preserve the original runs instead of rewriting the whole match. An insertion at the beginning of the match uses the following run's style; otherwise it uses the preceding run's style. The chosen style may not match the user's intent and still requires visual review. Unicode and unique-match checks remain in force.
- A fictional DOCX with bold, italic and underlined runs was edited, converted using installed LibreOffice, rendered and visually inspected. The inserted phrase appeared in the adjacent bold run while the original italic and underlined text remained styled. Focused tests also exercised insertions at the beginning, inside and end of a passage. This single fixture does not establish fidelity across real templates, pagination or arbitrary edits. The local vision model was not run for this fixture.
- The web suite passed (201 passed, 67 ignored), the styled conversion fixture was run separately, and web Clippy and formatting passed. Peak resources were not measured. No application was sent.

## Preserve unchanged DOCX run formatting — 2026-10-29

- For a unique exact DOCX replacement with a nonempty changed span, unchanged Unicode prefix and suffix text remain in their existing runs, so their styles are preserved. The changed span uses the first changed run's style. Pure insertions retain the earlier first-run behavior because the intended insertion style is ambiguous; a no-op returns the original bytes after uniqueness validation.
- A fictional three-run DOCX (bold prefix, italic changed word, underlined suffix) was edited by the application, converted using the installed LibreOffice and rendered. The resulting single page was visually inspected and showed the intended text and styles. This does not establish fidelity for complex real résumés, pagination or arbitrary templates.
- On the 100-DPI render, resident qwen3.5:9b transcribed the accented text and identified bold and underline but missed italics. On the 200-DPI render it identified all three styles but dropped the accent. These are two observations of one fictional page, not a benchmark; model visual inspection remains advisory. Peak resources were not measured.
- The web suite passed (201 passed, 66 ignored), the styled conversion fixture was run separately, and web Clippy and formatting passed. No application was sent. The CocoIndex shared runtime remains unusable for this checkout without an isolated binding; no shared settings or registration were changed.

## DOCX inline boundaries — 2026-10-29

- Exact DOCX replacement now treats inline images, hyperlinks, Word fields, references and several other non-text structures as boundaries. It still supports text split across ordinary Word runs and edits wholly inside a hyperlink. A selected passage that crosses one of these boundaries now requires manual editing rather than matching text that was only concatenated by the parser.
- Fictional XML fixtures verified the boundary behavior and preservation of the hyperlink and drawing elements. The web suite passed (200 passed, 65 ignored), as did web Clippy and formatting. This checkpoint did not render or visually inspect a real résumé and does not establish layout fidelity across DOCX files.

## Review selection for repeated labels — 2026-10-29

- Form inspection responses now identify selectors with identical labels. Suggestions for these controls remain available, but the interface leaves them unchecked by default and asks the user to select or edit each after reviewing it. This is a presentation safeguard, not proof that a selected mapping is correct; exact-label comparison can miss differently worded or differently cased ambiguities.
- A fictional local browser form confirmed that two same-label email controls can both receive suggestions and are identified as repeated. A focused UI test confirmed that those selectors are not preselected. The web suite passed (199 passed, 65 ignored), as did web Clippy and formatting. The browser fixture did not submit an application; peak resources were not measured.

## Repeated form labels — 2026-10-29

- Form review now distinguishes repeated identical labels by their order in the inspected schema, both in the field list and in required-field coverage messages. The site's original label and schema remain unchanged for mapping, approval and browser verification. Numbering is a review aid, not proof that visually similar fields have the same meaning.
- A fictional local browser form with two required Email controls produced separate numbered fields and warnings without submitting. A focused test verified that filling one mapping clears only its corresponding warning. The web suite passed (199 passed, 64 ignored before adding the separately run browser fixture), followed by web Clippy, formatting and the focused test. Peak resource use was not measured.

## File-input format checks — 2026-10-29

- Before attaching a local file, the browser driver checks the file control's declared `accept` hint against its extension and a small set of common MIME types. Explicitly incompatible or unresolvable types require manual review; the driver checks the hint again after attachment and during later file verification. An HTML `accept` hint is not proof of server acceptance, and the fixed MIME mapping can leave other formats for manual review.
- A real headless browser on a fictional local form rejected a PDF from a Word-only input before its change event, accepted it on a PDF input, and reported a control that changed its accepted format after selection. No application was submitted. Browser and web tests passed (6 and 198 respectively, 91 ignored across the suites), along with scoped Clippy and formatting. Peak resource use was not measured.

## Disputed fit qualification tie-breaker — 2026-10-29

- When an initial contradiction cites a fact without an English negation and the independent qualification comparison instead finds support, a bounded additional blind comparison may resolve it as supported only if that comparison also finds support. At most six disputed items and 6,000 payload bytes are considered; malformed, unavailable or non-supporting results remain unknown. The UI reports the reconciled count and still says model agreement is not proof. The assessment digest version was changed to mark older assessments stale.
- In one fictional qwen3.5:9b case, the initial model output incorrectly labelled a six-year Python fact contradicted while its explanation described it as supporting a five-year requirement. Both subsequent comparisons returned supported; the final result kept partial SQL experience unknown and scored 75% with a 50–100% range. This one example does not establish general accuracy or repeatability; the same model can agree with itself incorrectly. The extra request occurs only for this bounded disagreement and peak resources were not measured.
- The web suite passed (198 passed, 64 ignored), web Clippy, formatting and fit-badge UI checks passed; the focused tie-breaker test was rerun after a lint-only edit. The live test used one already GPU-resident local model after a free-memory preflight. No application was sent.
- Automatic acquisition of LibreOffice when absent remains unimplemented. The required isolated Crawl4AI provider was unavailable in the current tool inventory, PATH and default Python environment; no unverified download source was selected and no dependent page research was performed.

## Converter discovery for packaging — 2026-10-29

- The Windows package builder can now discover a complete local LibreOffice distribution from `WAYPOINT_SOFFICE` or a `program` directory on `PATH`, before checking standard installation locations. It still requires the converter executable and a distribution license, and bundling retains the existing file, size and hash checks. Missing or invalid configured candidates fall through to other locations.
- Fictional small-distribution tests covered configured and PATH discovery, fallback, bundling and the license requirement. On this host discovery selected the existing `C:\Program Files\LibreOffice`; no full distribution was copied or qualified in this checkpoint. Automatic acquisition on a machine without a converter, clean-host installation and bundled conversion remain unverified or unimplemented. No external download was performed.

## Employer initials in duplicate discovery — 2026-10-29

- Duplicate candidate indexing now includes a bounded ASCII employer-initial key, so an abbreviated employer and its expanded name can become a review pair even without a shared employer word. Existing title, description or requisition evidence is still required; matching initials can belong to unrelated employers, so pairs remain pending review and jobs are never merged automatically. The existing token and candidate limits still apply, and non-ASCII initials, employer aliases and unseen candidates can be missed.
- Schema migration 26 marks existing employer indexes for the existing bounded reindex workflow; existing jobs and prior duplicate decisions remain intact. The migration and reindex were tested on an in-memory fictional database, including both import orders and a distinct role. Large-workspace migration and peak resource consumption were not measured.
- Store, workspace and web tests passed (51, 40 and 197 respectively; 65 ignored across these suites), as did scoped Clippy and formatting. No external search or application was sent.

## Batch search coverage summary — 2026-10-29

- Batch web-search completion now reports requested, attempted, successful, failed and unattempted provider pages, plus counts of pages with truncated result processing, unresponsive engines, or unknown engine availability. The interface calls pages attempted rather than completed and shows the counts after normal completion, cancellation, interruption, or recovery. These are provider-page counts, not estimates of all jobs or websites searched; candidate links remain unverified.
- A fictional loopback provider returned one empty successful page and one HTTP 503; the batch reported one success and one failure. Additional tests covered an early stop, replay and mixed coverage, and the UI summary. The web suite passed (197 passed, 64 ignored), along with web Clippy, formatting and the search-coverage UI test. No external search or application was sent.

## Capture before reviewed link navigation — 2026-10-29

- Before opening a user-reviewed application link, the navigation worker now attempts to save bounded screenshots of the current page in the attempt folder. Capture failure or an unavailable record adds a manual-review warning; a failed capture also attempts to record an incomplete event and manual-review status. The normal page capture limits still apply and screenshots do not prove hidden content was captured. A user navigating manually before requesting a rescan cannot be captured retroactively.
- A local headless-browser fixture filled a fictional field, saved two bounded page segments before opening another fictional local page, and checked their record events and files. A forced screenshot-file collision produced an incomplete-capture event. This exercised the capture helper and local browser, not the public-HTTPS navigation endpoint or a live employer. No application was submitted. The web suite passed (194 passed, 64 ignored), as did web Clippy and formatting; peak resources were not measured.

## Attachments across form inspections — 2026-10-29

- Form attachment verification is now scoped to the inspection session where the upload control was observed. A later inspection retains the staged files and records but warns that prior-section uploads cannot be verified on the current section; it does not report an absent old selector as a failed current attachment. A new inspection may also be a rescan of the same page, so this conservative warning can require additional manual review. It does not prove earlier uploads survived navigation.
- A headless-browser fixture verified that a prior-inspection attachment selector is not checked against the current page while a changed current attachment still triggers review. The web suite passed (194 passed, 64 ignored), with web Clippy and formatting. Separately, the pre-existing fictional two-stage wizard passed with the resident local model and visual checks, using a simulated user gesture to advance. Automatic wizard-button navigation and live employer-site behavior remain unverified; no application was sent. Peak resources were not measured.

## Repeated visual freshness records — 2026-10-29

- Re-polling a completed form visual review now records a new freshness event and screenshot when the recaptured image or freshness status differs from the last recorded observation. An identical retry remains deduplicated. A failed record write is not marked saved and may be retried. This does not establish that the form is correct or complete.
- A single real headless browser on a fictional local fixture filled a form, changed the same field twice, polled the same review twice, and verified two recorded events referencing distinct saved screenshot bytes. No application was submitted. The web suite passed (194 passed, 64 ignored), as did web Clippy and formatting. The browser test ran after a free-memory preflight; peak resource use was not measured.

## First-pass conflict review — 2026-10-29

- The conservative negation prerequisite for candidate-fact contradictions now also applies when validating the initial model rubric and later rubric updates. A partial positive fact without an English negation marker is counted as unknown, not as a contradiction. This marker does not prove a conflict, may miss implicit or non-English conflicts, and the model's semantic judgments still require review. The input digest version was changed so existing assessments become stale.
- In a fictional end-to-end test, the resident qwen3.5:9b model left one year of SQL unknown against a five-year requirement. It also left an explicitly stated six-year Python fact unknown against a five-year requirement. An experimental follow-up search failed to recover that fact and was removed; this false negative remains unresolved. One example is not a general accuracy measurement.
- The web suite passed (193 passed, 64 ignored), along with web Clippy and formatting. The live test used one already GPU-resident local model after a free-memory preflight; peak resource use was not measured. No application was sent.

## Explicit conflicts in additional fit evidence — 2026-10-29

- The additional search for initially unknown requirements can now propose an explicit contradiction as well as support; the separate qualification comparison must agree before the judgment contributes to the score. In this recovery path, a contradiction is considered only when the cited fact has an English negation marker. This is a conservative lexical prerequisite, not proof of a relevant conflict; other languages or implicit conflicts may remain unknown. It does not govern the first-pass rubric.
- The resident qwen3.5:9b model produced 87% on all three repeated runs of one fictional assessment. The recovery pass proposed an unrelated fact for SQL on each run, and the qualification comparison downgraded it. In a separate fictional near-miss test, both model passes incorrectly treated one year of SQL as proving insufficient total experience. After the guard, that item remained unknown, six years of Python supported the five-year requirement, and an explicit statement of never holding a nursing licence was marked contradicted. The result was 50%, range 33–67%. These cases do not establish general model accuracy or repeatability; negation can refer to unrelated context and model agreement can still be wrong.
- The previous Python-evidence live case also passed after the change. The web suite passed (192 passed, 63 ignored), as did web Clippy and formatting. Live tests used one already GPU-resident local model after a free-memory preflight; peak resources were not measured. No application was sent.

## Additional evidence for unknown requirements — 2026-10-29

- Fit assessment now asks the local model to revisit up to six initially unknown listing criteria against the selected candidate facts. Candidate facts must be referenced by valid index and then pass the existing separate qualification comparison before they affect the score. Responses with missing, duplicate, or invalid IDs or fact indices, model failures, and payloads over 6,000 bytes leave the original unknowns in place. The UI reports selection and proposal counts; the changed input digest marks older assessments stale.
- On the previously recorded fictional qwen3.5:9b case, the first pass again missed explicitly stated Python experience. The additional search proposed the existing fact, the independent comparison agreed, and the resulting rubric marked Python supported while leaving the unstated SQL and chemistry doctorate unknown. The score was 70% with a 40–100% range. One example does not demonstrate broad accuracy, repeatability or calibration, and two passes of the same model can agree incorrectly.
- The web suite passed (192 passed, 62 ignored), along with web Clippy, formatting and the fit-badge UI test. The live test used one already GPU-resident model with a 4,096-token context after a free-memory preflight. Peak resource use was not measured. No application was sent.

## Qualification judgment cross-check — 2026-10-29

- Local fit assessment now asks the resident model to independently compare cited candidate facts with listing requirements and nearby job context. It does not show the initial judgment to the second pass. Agreement is not verification of truth; disagreement, malformed or unavailable responses, and requests over the 6,000-byte payload budget downgrade affected judgments to unknown. The interface reports the check and its limitations. Existing assessments become stale through the input-digest version change.
- A fictional live test of qwen3.5:9b with a 4,096-token context retained a six-year Python fact against a five-year requirement and downgraded a one-year SQL fact against a five-year requirement. In a separate end-to-end case the model incorrectly marked Python unknown even though the candidate fact explicitly stated Python use; this cross-check only revisits positive and negative judgments and does not fix that false negative. These two cases do not establish general accuracy or repeatability.
- The web suite passed (191 passed, 62 ignored), web Clippy, formatting, and the fit-badge UI test passed. Live tests used one already GPU-resident local model after a free-memory preflight; peak resources were not measured. No applications were sent.

## Local fit repeatability and page-vision checks — 2026-10-29

- The fit rubric now warns when a preference cites job text that also contains a listing criterion. This is a possible overlap, not proof of semantic duplication; the existing score is unchanged. The fixed weighted arithmetic is repeatable for a fixed rubric, but model-selected criteria and judgments remain subjective.
- Three successive runs of the resident qwen3.5:9b model on one fictional fit case each produced 87% with a 73–100% range. The model counted remote location in both a listing criterion and a preference, illustrating possible double counting. This single case does not establish general repeatability, accuracy, or calibration.
- Application checks may request a local vision assessment of the current viewport, with a bounded exact DOM text excerpt, current URL and screenshot freshness checks, and a single-model lease. They stay advisory and require manual review. A conservative limited-language cue check downgrades unsupported application-form claims; it is not a comprehensive classifier. The model initially labeled a fictional support-contact form as an application form; the guard downgraded that same unsupported claim in a subsequent live run. One fictional application form and one contact form are not evidence of general performance, and DOM text does not prove visible evidence.
- The web suite passed (190 passed, 61 ignored); web Clippy and formatting passed. Live checks used one already GPU-resident local model with a 4096-token context after a free-memory preflight. Peak resource consumption was not measured. No employer application was submitted.
- The configured CocoIndex MCP points to another repository and its daemon failed on non-UTF-8 settings. No shared registration was changed; semantic navigation of this repository remains unavailable until an isolated current-root index is established.

## Application-check destination evidence — 2026-10-29

- A completed application-page check now includes the actual inspected page URL in its result when it is public HTTPS, remains on the original origin, and has no credentials, query or fragment. URLs with those components or cross-origin redirects are not displayed. The result still explicitly requires manual review and does not establish that an application can be submitted. The existing source URL remains the freshness key.
- Five focused application-check tests passed with one browser fixture ignored, covering URL eligibility, query and redirect exclusion, stale results and result replay. Web Clippy and formatting passed with one Cargo job. No real employer site, live browser or submission was exercised for this change; peak resource use was not measured.

## Automatic discovery of a local converter for packaging — 2026-10-29

- Windows prototype packaging now automatically selects a LibreOffice distribution in standard machine or per-user locations when its executable and license are present. `--libreoffice-dir` selects a different source; `--no-libreoffice` opts out. Bundling still inventories and checks copied files and may substantially increase package size and packaging time. If LibreOffice is absent, packaging reports that separate setup is needed; automatic download and clean-host installation are not implemented.
- Discovery selected this host's installed LibreOffice, and fictional packaging and installer fixtures passed, including a missing-license candidate. An attempt to copy the real distribution into an isolated temporary folder and run a conversion was rejected by automatic approval review with the stated reason `blocked by policy`; therefore no copied-distribution conversion or full real bundle was qualified. The installed converter was tested in the earlier plain-text PDF checkpoint, which is not proof that a bundled copy works.
- Crawl4AI was unavailable through the current tool inventory, PATH and default Python environment; no external page research or download-source claim was made. Peak packaging resources were not measured.

## Pending PDF conversion recovery — 2026-10-29

- The interface can now discover a running or finished-but-uncollected PDF conversion after a lost start response or tab refresh, and prefers the current server task over an older tab-stored ID. Status retries reuse the existing saved result instead of writing a duplicate document. Recovery is in-memory only and does not survive an application restart.
- Three focused conversion tests and the UI recovery helper test passed. A local HTTP fixture started a fictional Markdown conversion with installed LibreOffice, discovered the same task through the new pending endpoint and saved the PDF. Web Clippy, JavaScript syntax and formatting passed with one Cargo job. No rendered browser session, real document, employer site or submission was exercised; peak resource use was not measured.

## Plain-text PDF conversion — 2026-10-29

- Saved UTF-8 `.txt` and `.md` files can now be converted to PDF through the existing local background conversion workflow. Markdown is converted as plain source text, not rendered markup; the UI says so. Input is bounded to 10 MiB, invalid UTF-8, NUL and empty text are rejected, and the original saved document is retained. Conversion uses the existing isolated LibreOffice profile, 30-second limit and 768 MiB Windows worker limit.
- A fictional Unicode text fixture converted with the installed Windows LibreOffice. Text extraction preserved `München · São Paulo`, and a rendered first page was visually inspected; the resulting layout was a plain monospace page, not a professionally typeset CV. Separate background-task and local HTTP endpoint fixtures saved a PDF and retained the original Markdown. This does not verify every language, long document, clean host or missing-dependency setup; other file formats and automatic conversion remain incomplete.
- The web suite passed 186 tests with 60 live/browser tests ignored; three LibreOffice fixtures ran separately. Web Clippy, JavaScript syntax and formatting passed with one Cargo job. No model, employer site or submission was exercised; peak resources were not measured.

## Unclassified model batches — 2026-10-29

- If an active model's sensitivity review cannot be obtained for a batch, its fields now remain manual-only for that review session, including fields with exact autocomplete hints. Other successfully reviewed batches remain usable. Missing or invalid review lists still get one bounded repair; runtime failures are not retried. The session reports that classification is unavailable, rather than asserting that each field is sensitive. This change does not alter the separately available offline exact-hint mode when no model is selected.
- Tests cover an invalid repair alongside a valid second batch, an unreachable model, and propagation into the form preview. The web suite passed 185 tests with 57 live/browser tests ignored. The local-model repair and fictional Portuguese browser/prefill/vision fixtures passed separately with resident `qwen3.5:9b` after RAM/residency preflight. Web Clippy and formatting passed with one Cargo job.
- A valid model review can still miss sensitive fields or flag ordinary ones; offline hints are not a sensitivity classifier. No real employer page or submission was exercised, and peak resource use was not measured.

## Bounded form-sensitivity repair — 2026-10-29

- When a mapping response is received but its sensitivity-review list is missing or malformed, the app now makes one bounded local repair request using field descriptions without candidate profile values. Valid known flags from either response are retained. If repair fails or is invalid, the existing warning remains and exact fallback may still be offered; model inference failure itself is not retried by this mechanism.
- A fictional test deliberately removed the initial review list and used resident `qwen3.5:9b` (digest `c97eb11d70b1acdc88af01eef566c1fe4f7fbe93eb1afc06871132f293ff425a`, 4096 context) for the repair. It flagged the Portuguese work-eligibility field and prevented a misleading email autocomplete fallback. This single example does not establish general classification accuracy or eliminate false negatives.
- The web suite passed 182 tests with 57 live/browser tests ignored; the repair fixture was run separately after RAM/residency preflight. Web Clippy and formatting passed with one Cargo job. No real employer site or application submission was exercised; peak resource use was not measured.

The existing deterministic mapping restrictions remain in force. An explicit prompt distinction leaves ordinary contact fields unmapped rather than classifying them as sensitive solely because the profile is empty.

## Model-assisted form review flags — 2026-10-29

- The local mapping model can identify additional fields for manual handling, including wording not covered by the fixed sensitivity markers. Valid flagged selectors suppress both model mappings and exact-autocomplete fallback. The review session presents these fields as sensitive and refuses them in generated answers, reviewed prefill and file attachment paths; the underlying browser schema remains unchanged for freshness checks and approval hashes. A missing or invalid model review list produces a warning, and recognized flags from a partially invalid bounded list are retained.
- With resident `qwen3.5:9b` (digest `c97eb11d70b1acdc88af01eef566c1fe4f7fbe93eb1afc06871132f293ff425a`, 4096 context), a fictional Portuguese work-eligibility label not recognized by the fixed list was flagged even though its autocomplete hint misleadingly requested email; a separate email field remained mapped. A local-browser test verified a model-flagged field is rejected before a simulated prefill grant is consumed. The model can still miss sensitive wording or flag ordinary fields, and when its review list is unavailable the warning does not convert all fields to sensitive. This is not a guarantee of safety on other pages or languages.

The local-browser grant test and local-model Portuguese test passed separately, as did the prior multilingual-browser and production-mapping model fixtures. Web Clippy passed with one Cargo job. ## Multilingual sensitive-form review — 2026-10-29

- The shared browser sensitivity check now includes common German, Spanish and French labels for work permission, compensation, protected characteristics, birth dates and consent. ## Numeric quote context — 2026-10-29

- Preference comparison now also checks a bounded same-line context around a uniquely occurring citation when the citation contains a complete numeric bound. This catches a model citing only the upper or lower endpoint of a crossing interval; the outcome is unknown and requires review. The fallback does not inspect repeated citations, split-line ranges or citations without a whole bound, and the existing limits on number formats and semantic interpretation remain. Unrelated nearby numeric text may also require review. Saved assessments from the preceding scoring version become stale.
- Twelve focused fit tests passed with five live-model tests ignored, including fictional endpoint-only citations, repeated quotes, split lines, Unicode boundaries and the model-response integration. Web Clippy and formatting passed with one Cargo build job. No live model, real listing, browser session, peak-resource measurement or application submission was exercised for this change.

## Grouped numeric interval review — 2026-10-29

- The conservative preference check now recognizes comma- and dot-separated three-digit groups as candidate integers and downgrades both supported and contradicted model judgments when a cited interval straddles the preference's number. It does not resolve locale, units, decimal values, mixed separators or unrelated numbers; it only flags a potential ambiguity for manual review. Existing assessments become stale.
- The fictional local-model ambiguity fixture now includes a grouped salary case. In the initial run the model incorrectly marked the overlapping range as contradicted; after the guard covered both judgment directions, four of five cases matched expectations. The accessibility-auditing case still failed, so the live qualification test remains failing. This small test does not establish general accuracy; the guard depends on the model citing the full interval.
- Eleven focused fit tests passed with five live tests ignored. The qualification test was run separately with resident `qwen3.5:9b` after a RAM/residency preflight and failed as above. Web Clippy and formatting passed with one Cargo job; peak resource use and real employer listings were not measured. No application was submitted.

## Numeric preference interval guard — 2026-10-29

- A model-supported preference is downgraded to unknown when its single plain integer lies above the lower bound and at or below the upper bound of a plainly written two-integer interval in its cited listing excerpt. The review explanation identifies the numeric overlap. This generic conservative guard does not interpret units, signs, decimal/grouping formats, all interval expressions or semantic meaning; it cannot verify a model judgment and may require manual review even for unrelated quantities. Earlier saved fit assessments become stale.
- In the four-case fictional live-model ambiguity benchmark using resident `qwen3.5:9b` (digest `c97eb11d70b1acdc88af01eef566c1fe4f7fbe93eb1afc06871132f293ff425a`, 4096 context), the previously false 100% salary result became unknown; three cases matched expected outcomes, while the accessibility auditing case still incorrectly scored as supported. The benchmark therefore still failed at 3/4 and does not measure general accuracy. An additional counterexample prompting experiment also failed to reliably reason about the cases and was not integrated.
- Eleven focused fit tests passed with five live tests ignored, including interval boundaries and a fictional model-response integration test. Web Clippy and formatting passed with one Cargo build job. The live benchmark was run separately after checking available RAM and model residency; peak resources, other models, real listings and application submission were not measured.

## Salary minimum range boundary — 2026-10-29

- The deterministic salary constraint now reports a range that crosses the user's minimum as unknown, including when the range maximum equals the minimum. A comparable range passes only when its stated lower bound reaches the minimum; a stated maximum below the minimum fails. Missing or invalid bounds remain unknown where they do not establish the outcome. Even a stated matching range is not a guarantee of an eventual offer.
- Eleven search tests and 40 workspace tests passed, with two live network tests ignored; search Clippy and formatting checks passed with one Cargo build job. The current workspace ranking path still supplies no employer salary to this checker, and the separately generated model fit score is not corrected by this change. No live employer listing or application was exercised.

## Preference ambiguity and temporary score review — 2026-10-29

- The match detail now lets users explore changes to individual rubric judgments and recalculates the same fixed-weight midpoint and range. These changes are temporary: they do not save corrections, update the job badge, alter the stored assessment, or establish that other criteria were checked. The model output and its cited evidence remain visible.
- A new ignored live-model qualification test exercises four fictional ambiguous preference cases. On local `qwen3.5:9b` (digest `c97eb11d70b1acdc88af01eef566c1fe4f7fbe93eb1afc06871132f293ff425a`, 4096 context), only 2/4 judgments matched the expected result: the model incorrectly treated web accessibility auditing as accessible product development and the top of a salary range as a guaranteed minimum. Each erroneous single-criterion assessment displayed 100% with a 100–100% rubric range. The test failed as intended; an additional prompt attempt still scored 2/4 and was discarded. This is a small fictional benchmark, not a general accuracy estimate. The model's assessments remain unverified and users must check them.
- Ten focused fit tests passed with five live tests ignored; the new live qualification test was run separately and failed as described. JavaScript syntax and fit badge/control checks passed, including changing a temporary judgment and checking the recomputed range. No rendered browser session, real listing, application submission, or peak-resource measurement was performed.

## Listing-backed preference comparison — 2026-10-29

- Fit assessment now makes a separate local-model request for up to six extracted preference excerpts of at most 200 bytes, using at most the first 3,000 bytes of the listing. Its reported selection count, truncation and failure are visible. Every returned ID must be unique and complete, and every positive or conflicting judgment must cite an exact quote from the supplied listing excerpt. An unavailable or invalid response leaves the prior conservative assessment intact; unselected preferences remain unknown. The match detail shows cited listing text for both matches and conflicts. Older assessments become stale.
- A fictional four-preference test with locally resident `qwen3.5:9b` (digest `c97eb11d70b1acdc88af01eef566c1fe4f7fbe93eb1afc06871132f293ff425a`, 4096 context) returned the expected supported, contradicted, contradicted and unknown statuses. Three full-assessment repeats for a second fictional case each scored 87%, range 73–100%. In that case the model inferred accessible product development from an accessibility requirement even though the prompt requested explicit evidence; the cited text alone cannot validate that inference. Neither example proves general accuracy, stable results across models or runs, or that similar criteria are not double-counted. The score remains an estimate for review, and missing or truncated evidence can change it.
- Ten focused fit tests passed, plus the two separately run live fixtures. Web Clippy, JavaScript syntax and fit badge checks passed with one Cargo build job. An earlier concurrent rebuild failed because Windows had locked the running live-test executable; the sequential retry passed. Resource preflight required one fully resident model and 12 GiB free system memory; peak usage was not measured. No employer site or application submission was exercised.

## Fit evidence and repeatability checkpoint — 2026-10-29

- A preference is no longer credited or contradicted solely from a candidate fact: absent a valid exact listing citation, it becomes unknown. The current prompt does not request that citation, so preferences normally remain unknown and need manual review. The headline is derived from rubric counts instead of model-generated prose. Saved assessments with the prior input version are stale.
- A fictional repeatability fixture ran three times against locally resident `qwen3.5:9b` (digest `c97eb11d70b1acdc88af01eef566c1fe4f7fbe93eb1afc06871132f293ff425a`, 4096 context). Before the change all three runs scored 87%, but each incorrectly claimed in its summary that an unverified SQL requirement was met and used the candidate's own remote preference as evidence of the job. After the change all three scored 73%, with a 47–100% range and both unverified preferences marked unknown. This is one fictional case on one model, not a general repeatability or accuracy benchmark; criteria selection, weighting, duplicate concepts, model judgments, omitted evidence and unsupported explanations can still affect the score. A prompt requesting listing citations failed validation in a preliminary local run and is not enabled.
- Nine focused fit tests passed (three live tests ignored in that run), followed by the separately run three-repeat live fixture. Web Clippy, JavaScript syntax and fit badge checks passed with one Cargo build job. Preflight showed one model fully resident with approximately 5.13 GiB reported VRAM and at least 12 GiB free system memory before loading; peak memory was not measured. No application was submitted.

## Search-plan identifier check — 2026-10-29

- Locally generated search phrases are checked against normalized known profile identifiers, including the full name, email, address, links and phone number. Unsafe phrases are omitted with a visible count; if none remain, planning fails. The identifier list is not added separately to the model prompt, although user-provided qualifications or instructions may contain identifying text. This is not an exhaustive PII detector, and users are still asked to review phrases before searching.
