## Search planner input checkpoint — 2026-10-29

- Local search planning now reads at most 20 eligible reviewed facts through the existing bounded sealed-data path instead of loading the entire fact history. Proposed phrases still require user review before a search.
- Plans are bound to their selected facts and instructions. The status endpoint reports stale when those inputs changed, and the UI rechecks before copying a phrase into a search field. The web and Jobicy start endpoints check an unchanged copied phrase's binding again before starting a search. Editing the phrase makes it a manually authored search; it still requires user review.
- A pending-plan endpoint and a one-result in-memory replay let the UI recover an active or completed plan after a lost response or tab refresh. They do not survive an application restart and do not launch web searches automatically.
- The encrypted-workspace test passed with more than 20 fictional facts, a corrected fact, an unreviewed fact and an oversized fact; the selected 20 matched the prior full-read ordering. Three planner/context tests, planner recovery and binding UI tests, and web Clippy passed. Local handler tests rejected stale bindings for web and Jobicy starts before launching a provider. A synthetic headless Edge fixture displayed fictional recovered phrases and rejected a stale phrase at 1200 and 375 pixels without issuing a new planning request. No real local model or public provider was exercised for these changes. Peak memory was not measured, and other paths may still load larger data sets.

## Search coverage reporting checkpoint — 2026-10-29

- Search results now distinguish an omitted engine-availability report from an explicit zero and show examined versus provider-returned result counts. The denominator is one provider response, not global market coverage.
- The latest completed single-search result or failure remains available for repeat status reads, so a lost response need not discard its candidates. A pending-task endpoint lets the web UI resume a search whose start response was lost while its task remains active or unread. The tab retains its task ID across refreshes and prioritizes a newer active task. Only one terminal result is retained in memory; recovery does not survive an app restart.
- Four web-search tests, the search coverage and single-search recovery UI tests, and web Clippy passed; the existing visual-comparison UI test also passed. A local HTTP handler test recovered a fictional pending task, and a synthetic headless Edge page with mocked responses prioritized a newer task and restored its result across two reloads without another start request. A separate synthetic fixture displayed the unavailable-status wording at 1200 and 375 pixels. These checks did not test a running app or a public search provider. Hosted CI and peak resource use were not measured.

## Source-only listing capture checkpoint — 2026-10-29

- When screenshot capture fails, listing metadata can now be sealed separately using DPAPI and retained without claiming an image was captured. A later screenshot capture keeps its own metadata, while salary evidence continues to refer to the earlier retained source. The salary detail warns when the capture URL differs from, or cannot be compared with, the current job URL. Application records include the source-only metadata separately from screenshots.
- Store tests passed 46/46; a populated v24-to-v25 migration fixture also passed with SQLCipher enabled. The web suite passed 144 tests with 37 ignored, and web Clippy passed. Fictional local handler tests exercised both matching and different capture URLs, the no-screenshot result, source retention, salary retrieval and a later separately recorded screenshot. A synthetic Playwright fixture displayed the different-URL warning at 1200 and 375 pixels. A clean-host upgrade and real-site screenshot failure were not exercised; peak resource use was not measured.

## Confirmation scan boundary checkpoint — 2026-10-29

- The bounded application-reference scan now stops at a UTF-8 boundary and refuses an identifier token cut by the scan limit. This prevents a panic and avoids accepting a partial reference as a confirmation; it does not send an application.
- Five focused driver tests and `cargo clippy -p waypoint_applications --all-targets -- -D warnings` passed with one build job. The web-app suite subsequently passed 144 tests with 37 ignored, and web-app Clippy passed. No live employer response was tested; peak resource use was not measured.

## Listing metadata access checkpoint — 2026-10-29

- Existing listing-capture metadata can be read without loading or decrypting screenshot segments; repeated listing inspection uses this path. Structured source remains in the existing encrypted capture when capture succeeds. Job details can request bounded `baseSalary` evidence from one saved structured posting and display its stated fields and capture date for manual review. This is evidence from the original capture, not a salary comparison; filter wiring, employer-salary normalization and fresh-host testing remain incomplete.
- The DPAPI capture test passed, including metadata access when a fictional screenshot segment is corrupted. The web listing-inspection suite passed 8 tests with 3 local-browser tests ignored, including captured salary and ambiguous-posting cases. A local HTTP handler test passed with a fictional encrypted capture and checked returned salary evidence and timestamp. The real display function was exercised in a synthetic headless Edge page with a mocked response at 1200 and 375 pixels; it showed the evidence, date and verification caveat without horizontal overflow or console errors. This was not a running-app end-to-end test. One Cargo build job was used; peak resource use was not measured.

## Salary comparability checkpoint — 2026-10-29

- Salary thresholds now carry optional currency, gross/net basis, period and compensation category. If these are absent or disagree with a stated job amount, the comparison is explicitly unknown; a lower bound below the threshold no longer acts as an upper bound. Invalid inverted ranges are also unknown. Older settings without the new optional fields remain readable.
- `cargo test -p waypoint_search` passed 9 tests, including a legacy-settings compatibility check, and `cargo clippy -p waypoint_search --all-targets -- -D warnings` passed with one build job. The current workspace ranking path does not yet supply parsed employer salaries, and the UI does not yet collect these new comparison attributes; this change alone does not make salary filtering fully functional. Peak resource use was not measured.
- Ranking now carries unknown constraint reasons to each included result; the web detail and desktop row expose them instead of presenting them as confirmed passes. Workspace tests passed 40 with two ignored across unit and integration suites; desktop tests passed 13, the web suite passed 142 with 37 ignored, and the web lint check passed. The web interface still requests default constraints, so the new warning path was not exercised through its settings UI.

## Longer form visual comparisons — 2026-10-29

- Visual review compares up to 200 supported filled fields across captured segments, increased from 20. Further fields are explicitly reported as omitted. Each model response remains limited to 20 observations per segment, and review remains advisory; hidden content may be missed.
- With one build job and one test thread, nine visual-review tests passed (one unrelated PDF test ignored), web-app Clippy passed, and the visual comparison UI check passed. No live vision model or employer site was tested; peak resource use was not measured. No application was sent.

## Dense visual segments — 2026-10-29

- When a segment returns the maximum 20 model observations, the application is marked for manual review and the normal and recovered review flows warn that additional fields may have been missed. The saved visual result includes the limit indicator. Fewer observations also do not prove completeness.
- With one build job and one test thread, ten visual-review tests passed (one unrelated PDF test ignored), web-app Clippy passed, and the visual comparison UI check passed. No live model, real employer site or resource peaks were measured. No application was sent.

## Adaptive dense-segment review — 2026-10-29

- A visual segment reaching the 20-observation limit is rechecked sequentially in two non-overlapping halves; at most 18 captures are attempted for six segments. The job remains flagged for manual review because fields crossing the split or otherwise hidden can be missed. Saved captures identify their half and freshness recaptures use the same geometry.
- With one build job and one test thread, eleven visual-review tests passed (one unrelated PDF test ignored), web-app Clippy passed, and visual comparison and adaptive-retry UI fixture checks passed. A live model, employer site, full application startup and resource peaks were not measured. No application was sent.

## Conflicting form mappings — 2026-10-29

- When a model suggestion conflicts with an available exact-label or autocomplete suggestion, neither value is proposed for that field. Other mappings remain available; required fields are flagged for manual review. Matching values still require user review and do not establish correctness.
- With one build job and one test thread, seven suggestion tests, three non-browser form-preview tests, and two targeted conflict tests passed; four browser tests were ignored. Web-app Clippy passed before the final test-only addition. No live model, employer site or resource peaks were measured. No application was sent.

## Partial model form mappings — 2026-10-29

- Invalid, unknown or restricted individual model mappings no longer discard unrelated valid mappings. Duplicate or invalid known selectors remain unmapped, including from exact fallback; required unmapped fields remain review obligations. Model results and exact hints still need user review.
- With one build job and one test thread, eight suggestion tests and four non-browser form-preview tests passed (four browser tests ignored); web-app Clippy passed. No live model, employer site or resource peaks were measured. No application was sent.

## Form mapping explanations — 2026-10-29

- The form preview explains which fields had conflicting, invalid or duplicate model suggestions and flags unidentifiable entries without exposing profile values. Field-specific warnings clear when all fields sharing the label receive reviewed mappings.
- With one build job and one test thread, eight suggestion tests and four non-browser form-preview tests passed (four browser tests ignored); web-app Clippy passed. No live model, employer site or resource peaks were measured. No application was sent.

## Bounded mapping for longer forms — 2026-10-29

- Forms up to 200 fields can be mapped in sequential batches of at most 40 eligible fields with a 24,000-byte prompt limit. Batch results are validated against their own fields; failed batches are reported and retain exact-hint fallback. Larger forms remain available for manual review. Option interpretation is separately bounded.

Web-app Clippy passed before the final test-only addition. No live model, employer site or resource peaks were measured. No application was sent.

## Bounded option interpretation — 2026-10-29

- Non-exact dropdown and radio options are interpreted in up to five sequential batches of ten fields, capped at 12,000 bytes of field data per batch. Unresolved choices are reported for manual review; duplicate selections for one field are discarded. Exact matches remain available beyond the batch limit.
- With one build job and one test thread, twelve suggestion tests and web-app Clippy passed. No live model, employer site or peak resources were measured. No application was sent.

## Long-form reviewed fill — 2026-10-29

- Reviewed fill accepts up to 200 distinct fields, matching the mapping limit. Its request body is separately capped at 1 MiB; other normal request limits remain unchanged. Larger answers can still exceed this byte limit and need manual handling. Oversized-form guidance recognizes the current mapping-limit message.
- With one build job and one test thread, ten reviewed-path tests passed (four ignored), including a 200/201-field boundary test; the ignored local-browser prefill fixture was run separately and passed without submitting. Web-app Clippy and JavaScript syntax checks passed. No live model, employer site or resource peaks were measured.

No live model, installed-app startup, employer site or resource peaks were measured. No application was sent.

## Template edit correction tracking — 2026-10-29

- Job-linked template conversion freshness now includes the workspace correction revision and the full stored job record and description, in addition to its bounded fit context and document instructions. A correction outside the selected fit facts changes the context. Adding a new fact does not itself advance the correction revision; the fit selection remains bounded.
- With one build job and one test thread, three template-edit tests passed (four conversion fixtures ignored), and web-app Clippy passed. No live model, employer site or resource peaks were measured. No application was sent.

## Queued template suggestion freshness — 2026-10-29

- AI-suggested replacements carry their source, job and supporting-context digest into the edit queue. Before conversion, the server rejects a queued suggestion if that context or the selected job changed. Manual replacements remain available without a model binding. The digest tracks the workspace correction revision; newly added facts remain subject to bounded fit selection.

## Application check freshness — 2026-10-29

## Form inference residency checks — 2026-10-29

- Form inspection and navigation recheck the selected model's reported residency before each model request, including sequential mapping and option batches. If the check fails or the selected resident model changes, that model call is not made and existing fallback/review behavior applies. This is a snapshot and cannot prevent external runtime changes between check and generation.
- With one build job and one test thread, the residency-change fixture, twelve suggestion tests and web-app Clippy passed. No live model or peak memory measurement was made, so this is not a no-freeze guarantee. No application was sent.

## Local inference residency across workflows — 2026-10-29

- The per-request resident-model snapshot now also covers local drafting, template suggestions, document excerpts, fit and duplicate analysis, link ranking, search planning and model-assisted listing extraction. Each guarded request stops if the selected model is no longer the eligible resident model. Visual-review workers already perform their own checks.
- With one build job and one test thread, the residency-change fixture and web-app Clippy passed. No live model or peak memory was measured; the runtime can still change between the snapshot and generation, so this is not a no-freeze guarantee. No application was sent.

## Bounded answer revision repair — 2026-10-29

- Application-answer and template-passage drafting retain the required style revision and allow one repair when its structured output or qualification citations are invalid. - Drafting selects up to 18 reviewed qualifications for a CV or 20 for a cover letter from the first 500 eligible facts using word overlap with the listing. This can miss relevant facts without shared terms or beyond the limit; the interface states the limitation. The draft context digest still uses the full active fact read and is not a bounded-memory guarantee.

## Incremental draft freshness digest — 2026-10-29

- Draft freshness now hashes active claims one row at a time through the shared sealed-text read path, rather than building a full active-fact vector. The digest includes reviewed and unreviewed claims and changes after additions or corrections. SQLite may still perform internal sorting, and other data such as the document and job description is read separately; peak memory has not been measured.

## Active qualification reads — 2026-10-29

- Active-fact reads filter superseded rows in SQLite before unsealing, and single-claim lookups use an ID predicate, both through the shared sealed-text reader. This removes the previous full-history vector and repeated linear supersession scans from these paths. Active results still occupy memory and SQLite can use temporary storage; performance and peak memory were not measured.
- With one build job and one test thread, all 47 store tests passed before the final targeted-query adjustment; afterward six store work tests, the encrypted-workspace fixture and web-app Clippy passed. No live employer site or model was tested. No application was sent.

## Ranking normalization reuse — 2026-10-29

- A ranking run normalizes reviewed qualification strings once and reuses them for each listing. Existing token, phrase, duplicate and denominator behavior is preserved; job descriptions are still read one at a time. Runtime and peak memory were not measured, and the overall job result set is still accumulated.
- With one build job and one test thread, ten search tests, 40 workspace tests (two network tests ignored), and web-app Clippy passed. No live model or employer site was tested and no real application was sent.

## Dotted employer acronym duplicates — 2026-10-29

- Duplicate candidates now normalize dotted employer initials such as `I.B.M.` to `IBM`. When comparing a single compact employer token, the index also checks up to 200 older records marked as missing employer tokens. Matches remain pending review; they are not merged. Existing pairs are not retroactively rescanned by this change, and the bounded legacy scan can miss candidates.
- With one build job and one test thread, nine duplicate-detection tests and web-app Clippy passed. No live boards or peak resources were measured. No application was sent.

## Related employer requisition review — 2026-10-29

- Duplicate indexing now flags a matching requisition under moderately related employer names for user review, even if title overlap is low. The explanation includes employer, title and description overlap. Identifiers can be reused by unrelated organizations, so this is not an automatic merge; existing pairs are not retroactively rescanned and candidate scans remain bounded.
- With one build job and one test thread, ten duplicate tests and web-app Clippy passed. No live boards or peak resources were measured. No application was sent.

On 2026-11-02, duplicate candidate comparison also began considering a title
without its own explicitly separated, exact location suffix. The original
title stays intact, the comparison requires at least two remaining title terms,
and any resulting pair remains pending review. Migration 46 queues affected
older listings for bounded rechecking. A fictional migration test covered the
previously missed pair and conservative exclusions; the store suite passed
(93 passed, 2 ignored), the focused SQLCipher test and web Clippy passed. No
live-board duplicate accuracy or peak resources were measured.
The bounded neighbor ranking used for duplicate review now applies the same
location-aware title comparison. A fictional crowded-employer test verified
that a relevant title with a long location suffix remains among 12 returned
candidates; the result remains truncated and requires review. Duplicate tests
passed (40 passed, 2 ignored), as did web Clippy. No live board was checked.

Duplicate review now warns when both listings carry different requisition IDs;
the warning also explains that different sites can use different IDs. It is
computed when pairs are read, including existing pairs, and does not change the
decision or merge records. A fictional duplicate test passed with SQLCipher.
For a limited public-data check, a [board page](https://job-boards.eu.greenhouse.io/unframe/)
showed two similarly titled listings with different IDs, and an [aggregator page](https://www.landthatrole.com/job/gh-unframe-5010248101)
linked to one of them. The app manually imported three captured records into an
isolated workspace: both the cross-site pair and the different-ID pair remained
pending review, with the latter displaying the warning. Input data stayed outside
Git. This did not exercise automated web search, prove that different IDs mean
different underlying roles, or submit an application. Crawl4AI 0.9.5+flitzzip.portable2
returned HTTP 200 for the board and aggregator; extracted Markdown SHA-256 values
were `597be3de8b976f06f691e57423f2d64c5cf02427cb076d24654c0f18baa642ef`
and `9515660063333e4de7ffda3d203220ea81c04a12abd6f2deecbafcde4bc685b1`.

Not yet exercised on a live third-party site |

### Search attribution across batch pages (2026-11-01)

The batch candidate view merges provider-reported engine names and dates from retained repeated references for the same exact URL before selection or inspection. It retains at most eight engines and four alternate dates and marks incomplete coverage when pages fail, are limited, or omit repeated references. These reports remain unverified; repeated references are themselves capped per page. A selection is a snapshot: clear and reselect it to include reports arriving later. Lost-save recovery compares the attribution as well as URL and title, and older selections without the coverage flag remain readable. Focused selection tests and the web UI checks passed locally; no external provider or employer was contacted for this change.

### Rich-text overlay checks (2026-11-01)

The browser now applies its existing center-point hit test to contenteditable fields as well as native controls. A fictional local-browser fixture confirmed that a covered rich-text field and one covered on focus are left unchanged, while an unobscured rich-text field is filled and verified. The existing hidden-before-write browser fixture, web Clippy and formatting checks also passed. This is a point-in-time center hit test, not proof that every overlay or partially obscured field is detected; pointer-events-disabled overlays and later page changes can evade it. No employer site was contacted and no application was submitted.

### Final input event verification (2026-11-01)

Text filling now rechecks the field's focus and rendered visibility after the final inserted segment, before reporting success. A fictional browser fixture covered a Unicode input event that displays an overlay and another that moves focus; both return an error for review, and an already-entered partial value may remain. The existing hidden-field fixture, web Clippy and formatting passed. The check is point-in-time and cannot guarantee detection of every overlay or later change. No employer site was contacted and no application was submitted.

### Interrupted fill attempt status (2026-11-01)

An interrupted form fill now independently updates the attempt folder to manual review and saves the answer outcome; a failure in either operation is reported. If the separate outcome cannot be saved, the event log is still attempted and the status update is still attempted. Focused fictional-workspace tests exercised the normal and failed-outcome paths, and the existing local browser/API prefill fixture passed. Web Clippy and formatting passed. No employer site was contacted and no application was submitted.

### Live dynamic-form inspection (2026-11-01)

A read-only Greenhouse API request identified a current Vercel listing, and an isolated Crawl4AI capture of its public page succeeded (HTTP 200; native capture SHA-256 `3233e2fb0db4fedbb15ea17a6c41e1c11ce19d5f4989357382609c6777431c4a`, retained outside the repository). One read-only browser inspection passed, while a second failed because a DOM node disappeared during inspection. The driver now retries that specific stale-node error up to three times with bounded waits, retains a manual-review wall after recovery, and reports manual review when all retries fail; other errors are not retried. The same listing was successfully inspected once after this change. This does not establish that the intermittent failure is eliminated or that the application can be filled. Unit tests, web Clippy and formatting passed. No model was loaded, no field was entered, no control was clicked, and no application was submitted.

### Local-model preview on a live listing (2026-11-01)

With no other resident model, the production startup gate admitted `qwen3.5:9b` at 4,096 context and reported it GPU-resident. A single read-only preview of the public Vercel Greenhouse listing from the previous checkpoint used a fictional candidate. The model proposed the first name, last name, email and LinkedIn values; it flagged the required phone field for manual review. The browser reported required custom controls and CAPTCHA among the review walls. No field was written, control clicked or application submitted. This was a text-based mapping observation, not a visual-model assessment or proof that the form can be completed. A memory watchdog was active and did not report a threshold breach; the model was unloaded afterward and approximately 23.8 GiB of system memory was available. The build helper now supports `-NoCapture` for displaying test output.

### Phone mapping investigation (2026-11-01)

A prompt variant explicitly clarifying applicant phone fields was evaluated once with resource-gated `qwen3.5:9b` on the same read-only public Greenhouse page. The model again flagged the required Phone field rather than mapping it, so that prompt change was discarded. Separately, the unchanged production prompt mapped a fictional applicant phone while leaving reference, manager and unspecified contact fields unmapped in one local-model run. The contact regression fixture now includes these phone cases. This narrow result does not resolve the live omission or prove general third-party-contact accuracy. The model was unloaded after evaluation; no form values were entered or applications submitted. Web Clippy and formatting passed.

### Live mapping test integrity (2026-11-01)

A subsequent live preview returned no suggestions because a changing page blocked inference, but the previous test still passed. The read-only test now requires a completed model response and records the prompt and response for its fictional profile; it may request one fresh read-only inspection after a stale-node warning. In one such run, the raw model response explicitly put the live `Phone` field in manual review. A prompt variant explaining `autocomplete=off` did not change that response in one run and was discarded. The cause remains unproven, and no phone-mapping improvement is claimed. The model was resource-gated and unloaded afterward. No field was entered, control clicked, or application submitted. Web Clippy and formatting passed.

### Manual field review explanation (2026-11-01)

Initial and rescanned form responses now identify selectors needing model review or affected by unavailable model review. The interface explains that AI review needs attention and directs the user to the browser; other sensitive fields retain a separate manual-handling message. The same server-side restrictions still apply. A focused UI check, 19 form-preview unit tests, web Clippy and formatting passed. The new explanation has not been observed in a rendered browser. No application was submitted.

### Rendered manual-review panel (2026-11-01)

A mocked local-backend Edge check rendered the form panel at 1,200 and 390 pixels. It confirmed separate AI-review and sensitive-field explanations without horizontal overflow or page errors. The fixture used a placeholder screenshot, not an employer page. It exposed duplicate display of an identical model warning; the panel now omits that duplicate only when the corresponding field explanation is shown, retaining other warnings. All UI checks passed. The mock server's missing favicon produced a 404 and was excluded from the console check. No application was submitted.

### Local vision on a public empty field (2026-11-01)

A resource-gated qwen3.5:9b run inspected a read-only crop around the empty Phone field on the public Vercel Greenhouse page. The field's live value was empty before and after capture, and the model observed Phone with an empty value rather than the supplied fictional comparison value. The crop clipped parts of neighboring labels, so their reported labels are not established by this check; one successful observation does not qualify visual filling or general accuracy. The test used a bounded scroll and no clicks, typing, uploads or submission. The model was unloaded. The visual-review unit group (23 passed, 15 ignored), web Clippy and formatting passed; the additional exact-empty assertion was added after observing the recorded model output.

### Field capture context (2026-11-01)

Field-context captures now use up to 240 pixels of available left context within the existing 720-pixel width, while refusing a capture that cannot include the entire target. On the same public Greenhouse field used in the previous visual check, the resulting crop showed the previously clipped neighboring labels in one observation; qwen3.5:9b again reported the empty Phone field correctly in one resource-gated run. Geometry and local-browser viewport tests, web Clippy and formatting passed. Context can still be clipped by viewport edges, target size or overlays; this does not establish general visual accuracy. The model was unloaded, and no form values, clicks or submissions were made.

### Duplicate title levels (2026-11-01)

Candidate title comparison now normalizes standalone uppercase Roman levels I–V to 1–5. For example, `Nurse II` and `Nurse 2` can be flagged for review when the employer candidate checks also match; no records are merged, and different levels do not become identical. The focused fictional test also checks that C++ remains intact. Existing already-indexed records are not automatically rescanned solely by this change, and the bounded candidate scan and other identity limitations remain. The duplicate test group passed (36 passed, one ignored), as did web Clippy and formatting. No external site was contacted or application submitted.

### Existing title-level recheck (2026-11-01)

Workspace migration v42 schedules a resumable background recheck so the Roman/numeric title comparison also reaches older jobs. Each title-level scan reads at most 64 IDs and titles and compares at most one candidate job per indexing step; existing decisions are preserved. Duplicate coverage remains pending, including for email handoff, until the cursor finishes. Fictional disk-backed migration tests covered rollback and reopening, including SQLCipher, and the email handoff guard was exercised. The store suite passed (86 passed, one ignored); web Clippy and formatting passed. Large-workspace runtime and peak memory were not measured, and bounded candidate comparison can still miss duplicates. No application was submitted.

### Title-level recheck timing fixture (2026-11-01)

A single debug-build, in-memory run rechecked 10,000 fictional indexed jobs with 10 affected titles and distinct employers. It took 162 calls, 243 ms total active time, and 5.3 ms for the slowest call on this host. The fixture had no descriptions, crowded employers or production scheduling delays; the actual worker waits two seconds between progressing steps, so its elapsed time would be much longer. This is not an encrypted-disk, peak-memory, worst-case or freeze-safety measurement. The ignored fixture is retained for repeatable investigation. Web Clippy and formatting passed; no application was submitted.

### Duplicate recheck scheduling (2026-11-01)

The duplicate indexer now reports whether a step compared a job or only advanced an empty scan page. Both the background worker and idle fallback wait two seconds after comparisons, 200 ms after an empty page while more work remains, and 30 seconds when idle; lock contention and errors retain their slower retry. This avoids spending two seconds on every empty 64-record page without adding concurrent comparisons. Tests cover scan, comparison, phase transition and idle scheduling, including the SQLCipher store; 87 store tests, web Clippy and formatting passed. The new elapsed time and resource use have not been measured on a large encrypted workspace. No application was submitted.

### Encrypted title-level recheck timing (2026-11-01)

A single debug-build SQLCipher disk run of the same 10,000-fictional-job fixture, with 10 affected titles and distinct employers, took 162 calls and 1.058 seconds of active work; the slowest call took 24.2 ms. It excludes production delays, descriptions and crowded employers and does not measure peak memory, concurrent load or worst-case behavior. The test created and removed its own fictional encrypted database. Web Clippy and formatting passed. No application was submitted.

### Independent fit-audit warning (2026-11-01)

A fit badge can now show both an unknown or conflicting required criterion and an incomplete requirement audit. The latter is calculated independently from the saved assessment's coverage and audit fields, including for older saved assessments; it does not change the score or its range. Focused coverage and local API tests, all UI checks, web Clippy and formatting passed. A mocked Edge job-list fixture rendered the combined warning at 1,200 and 390 pixels without horizontal overflow or page errors. This does not validate the model's assessment or establish complete requirement extraction. No application was submitted.

### DOCX tracked-revision edits (2026-11-01)

The exact DOCX editor now excludes removed and moved-from tracked revisions from matching, consistent with document extraction. It keeps removed XML unchanged and does not join a replacement across a removed revision boundary. A fictional DOCX fixture covered removed-only text, duplicated current and removed text, and a cross-boundary match. Eight DOCX editor tests passed (four LibreOffice-dependent tests ignored); web Clippy and formatting passed. This does not establish fidelity for every tracked-change structure or template; rendered review remains necessary. No application was submitted.

### Tracked-revision PDF conversion (2026-11-01)

A fictional DOCX with a moved-from tracked revision was edited and converted through LibreOffice. The exported PDF text contained both the removed text and the replacement, despite the accepted-view DOCX extraction omitting the removed text. Conversion now refuses DOCX files with `w:del` or `w:moveFrom` markers in inspected body, header, footer, footnote or endnote parts and asks the user to resolve revisions in a document editor first. This is conservative and does not inspect every possible DOCX part or guarantee PDF fidelity. Fictional marker tests covered the inspected parts; a separate styled DOCX without removed revisions converted successfully. DOCX edit tests, web Clippy and formatting passed. No application was submitted.

### Temporary accepted-view PDF export (2026-11-01)

For narrowly supported inline removed or moved-from revisions, PDF conversion now prepares a temporary DOCX copy with those revision elements removed; the saved original and its history are unchanged. It accepts the PDF only when its extracted text matches the original DOCX's accepted-view extraction. Unsupported revision structures, unavailable PDF text extraction or a mismatch stop conversion for manual review. The fictional tracked-revision fixture that previously exposed removed text converted successfully, matched the expected text and was visually inspected as one rendered page. Unit checks covered supported inline removal and unsupported property/range markers; DOCX tests, web Clippy and formatting passed. This does not guarantee layout fidelity or support all Word revision structures; visual review remains necessary. No application was submitted.

### Revision-view export notice (2026-11-01)

Document conversion and template-edit results now carry and display whether the PDF was generated from a temporary accepted revision view. The notice explains that the original or saved DOCX retains its revision history. A fictional tracked-revision LibreOffice/PDF extraction test confirmed the flag, and a conversion recovery test confirmed it remains in the completed response. All UI checks, web Clippy and formatting passed. The notice has not been checked in a rendered browser, and the text comparison still does not prove layout fidelity. No application was submitted.

### Public CV extraction and edit check (2026-11-01)

A publicly hosted university CV was retained outside the repository for a local test (PDF SHA-256 `86a4dd5303bf096e143457eebd3b3b6d3130de2cd081b8fc0c98089bf8c9e66f`; source-page crawl receipt SHA-256 `dc5932b9b752becae4acb3989f57b0612f84a93a8f4aab8d8c5299f1624e05e5`). The 21-page, 328,121-byte PDF yielded 53,817 UTF-8 bytes, with no pages lacking selectable text in this run; its first page rendered. A local title edit was refused because the rendered page changed outside the selected text area. The original remained byte-identical, and no edited file was saved. This is one long academic CV, not a representative benchmark, and direct editing of this file remains unsupported. Fixture checks, web Clippy and formatting passed; no CV content was committed and no application was submitted.

### PDF edits with explicit Unicode mapping (2026-11-01)

The PDF stream editor now supports single-byte `charmap` fonts only when each character has an unambiguous explicit Unicode mapping. A fictional font fixture verified stream editing and rejection of an unmapped replacement character. On the previously captured public CV, a short replacement using mapped glyphs succeeded, preserved the original file and passed the existing whole-document text, glyph and visual-difference checks; the first rendered page was inspected locally. The replacement kept its original starting position rather than recentering. The earlier longer replacement remained unsupported because its letters were absent from the subset font; general PDFs, layout preservation and arbitrary replacements remain unproven. Web Clippy and formatting passed. No CV content was committed and no application was submitted.

### PDF stream regression in CI (2026-11-01)

The fictional Unicode-map PDF stream check now runs in a separate Windows CI job using Python 3.13 and the app's pinned pypdf 6.10.0 version. The same check passed locally with pypdf 6.10.0. It covers the stream editor's mapped and unmapped character cases, not the complete PDF rendering pipeline or arbitrary font encodings. Hosted execution is pending the pushed commit. No application was submitted.

### Additional public CV check (2026-11-01)

A second public PDF (428,065 bytes, 29 pages) extracted 94,502 UTF-8 bytes, with selectable text on every page. The original test initially requested a passage absent from this document, so its manual-review result did not establish an editing limitation. The test now accepts a configurable passage. A unique existing passage was shortened successfully; the original was unchanged and the existing whole-document text, glyph and visual-difference checks passed. This is one additional example, not evidence of support for arbitrary edits or layouts. The original and edited files remain local and were not committed.

The first hosted CI run for this change was rejected before jobs started because a colon in the inline pip command made the workflow invalid YAML. The command now uses a YAML block; local YAML parsing passed. Hosted validation of the correction remains pending.

### PDF passage selection feedback (2026-11-01)

PDF edits now distinguish a passage that is absent, repeated, or split across text objects, and suggest the appropriate next step. Fictional PDFs exercised all three refusals through the real PDF editing process and PDFium; the Unicode-map stream check also passed. The Windows CI PDF job now includes the selection check and the pinned PDF rendering dependencies; its new hosted run remains pending. This improves guidance only: editing across text objects is still unsupported.

### Hosted PDF checks and second CV visual review (2026-11-01)

The Windows PDF regression job for commit `bbcb169` passed, including the fictional passage-selection cases. The earlier `fe1084b` build-and-test and browser jobs passed, while its supply-chain audit failed with dependency advisories and a yanked dependency; its license-policy step did not run. A local crop of the second public CV's edited line was visually compared with the original: the shortened text appeared in the same position, with the surrounding line intact. This is one localized visual check and does not establish whole-document layout quality or generic PDF editing support. The CV and rendered crop remain outside the repository.

### Transparent heading text (2026-11-01)

Inferred section headings now exclude text inside zero-opacity descendants when collecting context for field mapping, with bounded text-node and ancestor traversal. A fictional local-browser fixture included conflicting hidden and transparent text; the expected applicant and reference labels remained distinct, and changing the visible heading changed the schema identity. The focused browser fixture, browser unit tests, Clippy and formatting passed. This does not detect every visual clipping or styling technique, and no real application or model was used.

The updated fixture was subsequently exercised once through the production suggestion path with resource-gated `qwen3.5:9b`. It suggested the applicant email and phone, and flagged the reference and manager fields for review. It also suggested the applicant email for a generic contact question; its context should still be reviewed. Minimum available system memory sampled every two seconds was 17,233 MiB; this is not a continuous peak measurement. The model was unloaded after the test. An initial orchestration attempt stopped after treating compiler stderr as an error; no model remained loaded before the corrected run. No external form was filled or submitted.

### Redirected Start Menu folder (2026-11-01)

The package installer now resolves the current user's Programs folder from Windows rather than constructing it under `APPDATA`. If that folder is unavailable, installation stops before copying and offers the existing `-NoShortcut` option. Fictional redirected and missing-folder checks and the installer fixture passed in PowerShell 7 and Windows PowerShell 5.1. This did not change Windows folder redirection or create a real shortcut; a clean-host install and default browser provisioning remain unverified.

### Browser provisioning research (2026-11-01)

Chromium's download documentation says its snapshots and Chrome for Testing builds do not auto-update; Chromium's security FAQ limits Chrome for Testing to trusted content. These distributions therefore do not resolve the need for an automatically maintained browser for arbitrary employer sites. The existing launcher prefers installed browsers over a bundled fallback, except for an explicit user choice. The package README now states the limitation. Official-page crawl receipts were retained outside Git: download-page SHA-256 `5239847a5c1dd75cf346037076b0ca421a532f3f60591ff8c3b3f4a5253fae53` and official GitHub mirror FAQ SHA-256 `a6e7b5bb1dd819f4184e2f086d940c968b0d1b31eaed6b24ffdd2d2674772ee8`. A separate googlesource FAQ crawl failed with an HTTP 503 access challenge and was retained. No browser was installed or updated; automated browser provisioning remains open.

### Multi-line form headings (2026-11-01)

The rendered-heading collector now preserves a word boundary at visible line breaks and between block-level fragments while retaining adjacent inline fragments as written. Fictional local-browser cases covered line breaks, block fragments, inline word fragments, hidden text and changed-heading schema identity. The focused browser test, Clippy and formatting passed. Other visual layouts and real employer forms remain unverified; no application was submitted.

### Boxless label descendants (2026-11-01)

Rendered label and inferred-heading extraction now retain visible text inside `display: contents` descendants, whose wrapper has no box of its own. Before the fix a fictional label was reduced from two words to one. Local-browser checks verified the full label and section context, while nested hidden text remained excluded; Clippy and formatting passed. The change does not establish visibility for all CSS layouts or when the label or heading itself lacks a box. No real form was filled or submitted.

### Boxless label and heading roots (2026-11-01)

Labels and inferred headings that themselves use `display: contents` can now provide context after bounded ancestor visibility checks; clipping in the checked path leaves the label unverified for manual review. Fictional browser tests covered a boxless label, a boxless multiline heading and a clipped label. A resource-gated `qwen3.5:9b` run on the updated heading fixture mapped the fictional applicant contact and flagged reference and manager fields for review; it also proposed the generic email question. Minimum available system memory sampled every two seconds was 17,716 MiB, and the model was unloaded. This is not a complete visibility or real-site accuracy guarantee. Clippy and formatting passed; no application was submitted.

### Larger selected match batches (2026-11-01)

The selected-job AI assessment limit is now 100, matching one visible page, and the existing worker still starts one assessment at a time. Session recovery accepts the same limit and the interface states it. A mocked test exercised a 100-job batch, one-at-a-time scheduling, recovery at job 42 and rejection of 101 selections; related batch-failure and JavaScript syntax checks passed. The page must remain open to advance the batch, and no large live-model run or resource peak was measured.

A subsequent rendered check used a fictional 100-job server and mocked assessment endpoints. At 1,200 and 390 pixels, selecting the visible jobs enabled assessment, starting and stopping a batch worked, at most one mocked assessment was active, and no horizontal overflow or page errors were observed. The first attempt timed out because the batch controls were inside closed disclosure panels; the completed check opened them first. The Browser plugin was unavailable, so locally installed Playwright and Edge were used. This did not run a model or contact an employer.

### Public CV page vision checks (2026-11-01)

The production single-page vision transcription path was tested once on the rendered first page of each of the two previously captured public CVs, using resource-gated `qwen3.5:9b`. For the 29-page CV, normalized word-multiset overlap was 299/299 reference and 299/299 transcribed words; word order was not measured in that run. For the 21-page CV, overlap was 215/215 for both and the ordered-word subsequence also contained 215 words. The model reported no uncertainty. These are rendered pages of digital PDFs, not scans, and token normalization ignores punctuation, capitalization and visual layout; they do not establish exact transcription or broader reliability. Minimum free memory sampled every two seconds was 15,931 MiB and 18,114 MiB respectively; the model was unloaded after each run. The CVs and model outputs remain outside Git. An ignored aggregate-only test is available for other explicitly supplied public fixtures; web Clippy and formatting passed.

The same first page of the 21-page CV was also rendered at 120 DPI, converted to grayscale JPEG at quality 75, and saved locally as an image-only single-page PDF (no selectable text; SHA-256 `1a5c48a0d393865655b3dabf13c41211dd6f66e31893a847d104e3f196229b0c`). One resource-gated transcription matched all 215 normalized words in order and matched the digital reference after whitespace normalization, including punctuation and capitalization. The model reported no uncertainty; minimum sampled free memory was 18,126 MiB, and the model was unloaded. This controlled derivative is not a physical scan and does not cover handwriting, rotation, noise, multi-page transcription or other documents. The aggregate test now accepts a separate reference PDF; files and transcription remain outside Git. Web Clippy and formatting passed.

### Choosing another scanned template page (2026-11-01)

After reviewing a scanned template transcription, the user can choose another nonblank scanned page and request a new draft using that page for style. The previous scanned-page transcription is replaced, not combined; the interface states that other scanned pages remain untranscribed. The request is bound to the current job, draft and template context and the selected page is rechecked against fresh PDF coverage. Fictional tests covered stale context, invalid or blank pages, the review interface and the PDF worker. A resource-gated `qwen3.5:9b` run drafted using page two of a fictional two-page image-only PDF; minimum free memory sampled every two seconds was 17,027 MiB and the model was unloaded. A rendered mock at 1,200 and 390 pixels sent the selected page without overflow or page errors; the Browser plugin was unavailable, so locally installed Playwright and Edge were used. Draft tests, web Clippy and formatting passed. Multiple scanned pages are still not combined into one draft, and no employer site was contacted.

### Combining reviewed scanned pages (2026-11-01)

The review panel now also offers to retain the user's corrected current transcription while transcribing another scanned page. Up to three distinct scanned pages can contribute to the template text in original page order; each added page requires an explicit review confirmation, and selectable text remains in order. The existing style prompt still samples at most 700 characters, so it may not use every retained passage. Earlier reviewed text is shown, but its image is not retained in this panel; the user must reopen the original PDF to inspect it again. The replace-page option remains available. Fictional tests covered order, duplicate pages, size limits and stale context; one resource-gated `qwen3.5:9b` run combined two pages of a fictional PDF and retained the reviewed first transcription in the style sample. Minimum free memory sampled every two seconds was 16,567 MiB; the model was unloaded. A rendered mock exercised both actions at 1,200 and 390 pixels without overflow or page errors, using Playwright and Edge because the Browser plugin was unavailable. Draft and UI tests, Clippy and formatting passed. This is not full multi-page document transcription, semantic verification or a guarantee of style fidelity; no application was submitted.

### Style sampling across reviewed pages (2026-11-01)

When a combined scanned template exceeds the bounded style budget, the sampler now reserves an excerpt from each of up to three scanned pages and reports their numbers. An explicitly selected style passage retains priority instead. A first fictional local-model run failed because whitespace trimmed from the combined template made a reviewed page fail verification; verification was corrected and a trailing-whitespace regression was added. The same resource-gated two-page run then passed; minimum free memory sampled every two seconds was 17,043 MiB and the model was unloaded. This provides excerpts, not full-page style or content coverage. Draft and selection tests, web Clippy and formatting passed; no application was submitted.

### Qualification suggestions from mixed model responses (2026-11-01)

Exact document quotations are retained when a model also returns non-exact suggestions; discarded suggestions are counted and shown for review. Malformed or oversized sections still fail explicitly. Three public PDF CVs were tested privately with resource-gated `qwen3.5:9b`: the first previously failed because mixed responses rejected both sections, while the revised review completed on all three. One longer CV included an oversized response, so its review was partial. These are limited examples, not proof of complete qualification extraction. The lowest sampled free system memory was 14,332 MiB, sampled every two seconds; the test model was unloaded. Source documents and excerpts remain outside Git. Focused tests, web Clippy and formatting passed.

Follow-up: responses exceeding the requested 12 suggestions are now validated instead of rejected, up to a structural ceiling of 128 entries per section. The overall suggestion cap increased from 48 to 128, with omissions still reported. On the same longer CV, a resource-gated repeat attempted eight sections with no failed sections, retained 102 exact suggestions and rejected five non-exact suggestions; no suggestions were omitted by the overall cap. The eight-section limit can still leave document text unreviewed, and exact quotations are not automatically verified qualifications. Minimum sampled free memory was 15,913 MiB; the model was unloaded.

### Additional independent web search (2026-11-01)

The wider-web interface accepts the optional Wiby JSON API using `https://wiby.me/` as the provider; the interface links to Wiby as required by its published API terms. Its index emphasizes smaller and older sites, so it should not be treated as comprehensive job coverage. A live adapter request for a generic software-job phrase returned 12 provider results, of which nine were eligible HTTPS candidates; none of the 12 titles explicitly identified a job. A more specific engineering phrase returned no results. These are unverified links, not confirmed vacancies or application forms. The production fetch and parser were exercised in an ignored live test; this was not a rendered-interface end-to-end test. No candidate information was sent. The API's terms and about pages were captured with the development crawler; fictional parser tests, search UI checks, web Clippy and formatting passed.

Follow-up: the rendered desktop interface was exercised with a generic phrase in an isolated workspace, using Playwright and Edge because the Browser plugin was unavailable. The single search displayed nine candidates and Wiby attribution; selecting and deselecting a candidate updated the counter. A two-page batch reported two successful pages. A visible checkbox alignment issue and singular counter wording were corrected and checked again. No candidate page was inspected and no application was submitted; job relevance and application-page verification remain unproven for these results.

A subsequent three-phrase search through the interface returned 24 candidates, 14 with job-related terms in their titles. One employer's live hiring page advertised multiple open roles. With `qwen3.5:9b` resident, the interface saved that page but reported an incomplete capture and missing or unverified listing details; it did not establish correct extraction of a specific role or application route. The run used a 10 GiB free-RAM watchdog; the lowest sampled free memory was 13,162 MiB, and the model was unloaded. No application was sent. The Wiby adapter now decodes basic encoded entities in result titles and snippets; unsupported entities remain unchanged rather than being guessed.

Inspecting the saved record showed the local model selected one genuine role from that multi-role page, but the record alone did not establish that every field was associated with the same vacancy. Newly model-extracted listings now explicitly warn that a page can contain multiple roles and that the selected title and details require review. This does not yet enumerate or separate all vacancies on a shared page. Candidate extraction tests and web Clippy passed.

The inspection now performs a separate, bounded local-model pass for other role titles and displays exact-text suggestions with controls to prepare a fresh search. Failure of that pass leaves the original extraction available. In two live inspections of the same multi-role page, the model suggested one other open role and two past roles despite being instructed to exclude closed positions. The interface therefore labels all suggestions as unverified and potentially historical; it does not create separate job records from them. These controls are currently shown only on the search card after inspection and are not retained across reloads. Minimum sampled free RAM was 13,894 MiB in the first completed run and 14,718 MiB in the repeat; the model was unloaded after each. Tests use fictional responses, including rejected invented titles; no application was sent.

Follow-up: role suggestions can now include a model status assessment backed by an exact quotation; unknown or nonmatching quotations do not produce a status. A separate bounded check is attempted when status is unknown. In one live repeat on that employer page, the model marked one of the two past suggestions historical and quoted a relevant passage; the remaining two suggestions had no usable status assessment. The evidence check verifies that a quotation exists on the page, not that it applies to the suggested role. The interface identifies this as a model judgment and asks for verification. Minimum sampled free RAM was 13,845 MiB; the model was unloaded. Candidate and UI tests and web Clippy passed.

Role suggestions are now retained in the first saved listing-source provenance when that metadata can be saved, and are shown again from listing evidence with the same uncertainty notice. Existing first captures are not overwritten; when a newer inspection cannot retain its suggestions, the interface reports that they will disappear from the search card. A Windows fixture exercised source-only storage and retrieval through the listing endpoints; this is not yet a rendered reload test against a live employer. Candidate tests, all 106 UI tests, and web Clippy passed.

A later rendered Windows test used a fresh isolated workspace, discovered and inspected the same public employer page with `qwen3.5:9b`, then restarted the app. All three role suggestions and the historical assessment reappeared in the selected-job evidence panel; selecting one prepared a visible search phrase without sending it. This does not verify their current status or create separate job records. A test initially looked in the wrong panel; investigation also found that typing in the jobs filter before initial state loaded could throw a JavaScript error. Initial review state now starts empty, and a repeat early-input/reopen test showed no page errors. The live inspection sampled at least 14,609 MiB free RAM, and the model was unloaded. No application was sent.

For suggested roles with an unambiguous, observed same-page fragment link, the interface can now prepare a manual job entry using that link; the user must review and add it. Links with different origins, paths, queries, or ambiguous matching labels are not offered. In a fresh isolated workspace, a live employer page exposed such a link for a second role, and the UI added it as a distinct job after explicit review. The model still suggested historical roles, so every role needs availability review. Same-URL intake with a different title or employer now stops for manual review instead of silently reusing a record or adding a requisition number; similar wording can represent either the same job or different jobs. The live run sampled at least 15,004 MiB free RAM and unloaded the model. No application was sent. Fictional intake and target-validation tests, all 108 UI tests, and web Clippy passed.

After reopening that isolated workspace, the observed role-specific control reappeared in the saved listing evidence and prepared the expected role URL and title. This repeat did not add or submit anything. The full web-backend suite passed 464 tests with 240 environment-dependent tests ignored; all 108 UI tests and web Clippy passed.

An observed role link can now start a separate inspection. For conventional page fragments the inspector requires one unambiguous observed link label, directs local extraction to that label, and rejects a different extracted title. A single bounded retry is allowed when a required title or employer fails exact-text validation. In a live test on the same employer page, the first inspection of the selected section failed because the model's employer was not an exact page excerpt; with the retry, a record with the intended title and employer was saved. The test harness then timed out waiting for a button replaced by the UI refresh, but a subsequent workspace reopen confirmed the saved record and retry warning. The record still requires review, and this does not prove its description or application route is correct. Minimum sampled free RAM was 11,987 MiB; the model was unloaded. No application was sent. Focused candidate tests, all 109 UI tests, and web Clippy passed.

The previously saved description was checked against separately captured employer-page sections. Of 62 eight-word chunks, 56 matched the selected section and none matched only the other or past-role reference sections; markup differences and shared wording make this a limited comparison, not proof of correctness. For a unique semantic DOM section matching the selected fragment, newly extracted location and description must now appear inside that section as well as the captured page. When the model returned a description outside that boundary, a bounded section-only retry produced a 180-character excerpt; all three complete eight-word chunks matched the selected reference section in one live run. The result was explicitly marked partial, and the earlier saved record was not overwritten. Minimum sampled free RAM was 14,665 MiB; the model was unloaded. A missing, ambiguous or nonsemantic section remains unbounded and requires manual review. Synthetic DOM and extraction tests and browser/web Clippy passed; no application was sent.

Reinspection of an already-saved job can now show the saved and newly extracted descriptions together without modifying either the saved job or its approvals. The comparison is temporary and is limited to 30,000 bytes of the previous description, with truncation disclosed. A Windows endpoint fixture verified that the saved text remains unchanged. A live rendered repeat on the same employer role showed the 3,347-character saved description beside the 180-character partial excerpt; the page reported no JavaScript errors. Minimum sampled free RAM was 14,575 MiB; the model was unloaded. This is a comparison only: it does not yet offer a way to apply the new description. Focused backend checks, all 110 UI tests, and web Clippy passed; no application was sent.

A reviewed reinspection can now replace the saved description after explicit confirmation. The server binds the action to the completed inspection and checks the saved job and description revisions; truncated saved descriptions cannot be replaced through this comparison. The store updates the description, invalidates the packet and grants, reopens an approved job and rechecks duplicates in one transaction. Jobs with submission in progress or already completed are refused. Fictional tests cover rollback, stale reviews, replay and confirmation; the complete replacement flow has not yet been checked in the running app.

A separate local browser fixture rendered the comparison using the application component and stylesheet. Dismissing the confirmation kept the comparison visible; accepting it invoked a simulated replacement and showed the completion notice. This fixture used fictional text and a simulated API, so it does not verify the full inspection-to-database flow in the running app.

Saved listings now offer a direct Reinspect listing action. It shows a comparison only when the returned saved identity matches the selected job. In the Windows SQLCipher app, a real saved listing exposed the control and started inspection; with no eligible model loaded and no structured job found, the app reported manual review instead of producing a comparison. The full real-page replacement remains unverified. Fictional UI tests cover identity mismatch and a refresh failure after a successful replacement. No application was submitted.

With qwen3.5:9b loaded fully in GPU memory, the saved-listing action inspected a real multi-role employer page and displayed the existing description next to a much shorter model excerpt. The replacement confirmation was cancelled, so the saved description was not changed. Minimum sampled free RAM was 13,593 MiB; the test model was unloaded. The interface now surfaces extraction warnings and explicitly warns before replacing a longer description with text less than half its length. Fictional UI tests cover the warning. This single run does not establish general extraction completeness or verify a real replacement. No application was submitted.

For a uniquely identified semantic page section, reinspection now presents the bounded DOM text alongside the model excerpt. A user can explicitly choose the observed section as the replacement only when it was not truncated; the server retrieves it from the completed inspection and applies the same stale-review and approval safeguards. The interface warns that DOM text can contain hidden or unrelated content. Fictional UI and Windows SQLCipher endpoint tests cover selection, refusal of truncated sections and replay; web Clippy passed. This new option has not yet been exercised on a live employer page.

A subsequent Windows SQLCipher run inspected the same real multi-role employer page with qwen3.5:9b fully GPU-resident. The model produced a 180-character partial excerpt; the observed section displayed the role responsibilities and qualifications and was selected through the interface in the isolated test workspace. The app reported that the section was saved. This is one page and does not establish general section correctness or repeatability; the section remains subject to user review. Minimum sampled free RAM was 14,104 MiB; the model was unloaded. No application was submitted.

Saved fit-review scenarios now report separate deterministic breakdowns for listing criteria and user preferences, using the same reviewed judgments and weights as the combined score. Excluded criteria do not contribute, and a missing or invalid source suppresses the breakdown rather than inventing one. The interface notes that these figures cover only the criteria shown; they do not measure omitted requirements or establish a calibrated probability. Older saved reviews without breakdowns remain readable. Fictional arithmetic and UI tests passed, as did Windows web Clippy.

Long fit listings now use a bounded excerpt from both the beginning and end, with an explicit omission marker, instead of only the first 8,000 characters. Coverage counts exclude the marker and remain incomplete when the middle is omitted; existing saved assessments become stale because their input selection changed. A fictional Unicode listing test checks a late requirement and incomplete coverage. This change has not yet been evaluated with a local model, and it can still miss criteria in the omitted middle. Windows fit tests and web Clippy passed.

A bounded live qwen3.5:9b test of one fictional long listing extracted the visible tail requirement and, in a repeat, classified it as required and supported by the supplied fictional fact. The model was fully GPU-resident; minimum sampled free RAM was 13,218 MiB and 13,225 MiB in the respective runs, and it was unloaded after each. This checks one artificial case, not real-world accuracy, omissions in the middle, or general repeatability.

Long-listing fit input now samples non-overlapping beginning, middle and end passages within the existing 8,000-character limit, marking both gaps and reporting the actual source characters covered. Two sequential local qwen3.5:9b runs on one fictional long listing identified the visible middle and final requirements as required and supported. In the inspected repeat, the model also treated two listed duties as preferred criteria with unknown evidence. Minimum sampled free RAM was 12,856 MiB; the model was unloaded. These runs do not establish broad accuracy, repeatability or coverage of either omitted gap.

The bounded missing-requirement audit now examines separate beginning, middle and end passages rather than only the beginning of its input. Its citations must occur within one examined passage, and omitted input remains explicitly incomplete. A fictional fixture and one live qwen3.5:9b run recovered a late requirement as unknown for further review; this does not establish full coverage or real-world accuracy. The model was fully GPU-resident, minimum sampled free RAM was 14,905 MiB, and it was unloaded. Windows web Clippy passed.

Fit coverage now identifies beginning/middle/end sampling in the saved assessment view, and reports unavailable or inconsistent input accounting rather than silently hiding it. A Windows test reopened a saved fictional long-listing assessment and verified that incomplete coverage remains visible in its returned state. The complete web-backend suite passed 474 tests (243 ignored); all 117 UI tests passed after updating their isolated helper harnesses.

On the fictional long-listing fixture, qwen3.5:9b classified an unmentioned required qualification as unknown and, in a separate assessment with an explicit contrary fact, classified it as contradicted. The test checked the cited criterion and its status; it does not establish correctness for other wordings or jobs. Minimum sampled free RAM was 12,476 MiB with the model GPU-resident; the model was unloaded.

In an isolated Windows workspace, a real saved browser-engineering listing and one fictional qualification produced no usable local-model fit assessment: the model returned a non-exact criterion excerpt after its permitted repair. Repair feedback now includes the rejected quote and its source, but a second real-page attempt still failed on another paraphrase, so this change is not claimed to fix the model limitation. The automatic-assessment status now displays up to three bounded job-specific failure reasons instead of only a count. Minimum sampled free RAM was 14,445 MiB and 14,186 MiB across the two GPU-resident runs; the model was unloaded. Fictional validation and UI tests and web Clippy passed. No application was submitted.

A general prompt example was added to discourage changing a source modifier or duplicating grouped and individual criteria. In a later isolated real-listing run with qwen3.5:9b, one role produced an assessment with a 0–100% range, while the other still failed validation because the model returned overlapping grouped and individual criteria; no score was saved for that failed role. Minimum sampled free RAM was 14,741 MiB. A follow-up using qwen3.5:9b-q8_0 was interrupted by the resource monitor at 10,031 MiB free; the model was unloaded and available RAM subsequently recovered to 21,135 MiB. That interrupted run provides no model-quality result and is not evidence the configuration is safe at other loads. No application was submitted.

On Windows, guarded resident-model requests now sample available system memory before each inference. They pause if less than one quarter of physical RAM is available, with a floor clamped between 4 and 10 GiB; invalid samples also pause. The check does not unload a shared model. A fictional boundary test and web Clippy passed. This is a point-in-time admission check, not a reservation or an in-flight watchdog, and cannot guarantee that the host will not experience memory pressure after a request starts.

Guarded Windows Ollama structured requests now recheck available RAM while waiting for a response. On a failed check or cancellation, Waypoint closes its own HTTP connection without unloading a shared model. A local TCP fixture verified that the connection closes on a simulated pressure signal; a separate live qwen3.5:9b fictional audit completed normally with the guard enabled. Minimum sampled free RAM in that run was 13,616 MiB and the model was unloaded. The fixture does not establish whether every Ollama version immediately stops GPU work after disconnect; other inference paths and model startup are not covered by this in-flight guard. Memory can fall between samples. Inference tests and Windows web Clippy passed.

A single live qwen3.5:9b test triggered simulated pressure after a fictional request entered its read loop. Waypoint returned the guard error 308 ms after the signal, and a separate short request completed in 149 ms. Minimum sampled free RAM was 14,824 MiB; the model was unloaded. This establishes client-side interruption and one subsequent response on this runtime, not proof of immediate GPU cancellation under every load or runtime version.

Windows visual-model generation now uses the same in-flight system-memory guard as guarded text requests. The connection-level simulated-pressure test passed, as did 25 non-ignored visual-review tests. A live qwen3.5:9b fictional placeholder test failed: the raw visual transcription treated placeholder text as an entered value. A separate live end-to-end check passed because the live DOM cross-check left that field unconfirmed, despite the model observing the placeholder. This does not prove the visual model is reliable or that every form exposes a verifiable live value. Minimum sampled free RAM was 13,797 MiB; the model was unloaded. No application was submitted.

Recovered visual-review summaries now distinguish a raw transcription from a live-field check and explicitly flag unconfirmed values for manual inspection. A recorded match is described as historical rather than current. Fictional UI checks cover a placeholder-like unconfirmed result, a matched live value, and missing live evidence; all 119 UI tests passed. This display change does not improve the vision model itself or guarantee that an arbitrary field can be verified.

A more explicit placeholder prompt was evaluated against one fictional screenshot in three repeated local qwen3.5:9b calls; all three still transcribed the placeholder as a value. The prompt change was reverted because it did not fix the observed failure. A separate end-to-end run with the experimental prompt passed because the live DOM check left the empty field unconfirmed. The ignored raw-vision evaluation now retains all three runs and fails if any false match occurs. Minimum sampled free RAM was 14,125 MiB; the model was unloaded. This does not prove other screenshots or models behave the same way. No application was submitted.

A missing, ambiguous or unsupported live-field mapping no longer stops checks of later fields when no browser call was made; an actual driver error still stops later calls, and the time budget remains enforced. A live fictional browser/model test placed an unfilled placeholder first: the model incorrectly transcribed it, the app left it unconfirmed, and a subsequent entered field was verified. Minimum sampled free RAM was 11,860 MiB; the model was unloaded. A deterministic test also covers stopping after a real driver error. This does not fix the model transcription error or establish correctness on arbitrary forms. No application was submitted.

Fit assessment repair now includes the rejected structured response as untrusted data, when it is at most 16 KiB, alongside the validation error and original inputs. Oversized or unavailable responses are not replayed. The same one-repair limit and validation remain in force. Fictional tests checked the handoff and size limit; this change has not yet been evaluated with a local model on the real listing that previously failed, so its effect on model accuracy is unknown.

A read-only comparison was prepared for a current public Vercel Greenhouse application to test whether a reviewed phone-related lesson changes the local model's earlier phone omission. The public page was captured by the development crawler (HTTP 200; native capture SHA-256 `a3901b7968b526f68c9f6f08fd2a40bfcd4ea6a1e61b1c6e966c0bd96a938d97`). The application startup gate refused the model: 9,475 MiB GPU memory observed free versus 9,605 MiB estimated. No model comparison or browser form inspection ran, and no application was submitted. The ignored comparison test remains for a later resource-qualified run; it checks for completed model mappings and does not type or click. A resource snapshot can change and does not reserve memory.

A subsequent read-only browser inspection of that page detected 10 fields and 14 custom-choice controls, with incomplete custom-choice inspection, CAPTCHA and other manual-review walls. The required `Phone` field appeared as a non-sensitive text control with `autocomplete=off`; the page also changed during one inspection. This observes the page structure but does not establish why the model previously omitted a phone suggestion. After the browser closed, the model startup gate again refused the comparison: 9,533 MiB GPU memory observed free versus 9,605 MiB estimated. No model was loaded, form input entered, control clicked or application submitted.

When a required or manual-review field is shown from a hidden browser session, the field notice now explains that the review must be closed and reopened with a visible browser for manual entry, and that page changes may not be retained. Visible or unspecified browser states do not show that instruction. Focused interface tests covered the message; no rendered or live-employer correction was tested. This does not change the model's classification or automatically fill a flagged field.

The visible-browser handoff now requires the server to explicitly report a hidden browser; an absent or unknown visibility value no longer claims the session is hidden. Existing navigation and handoff tests and new unknown-state cases passed. The real inspection and resumed-inspection responses both include the visibility flag. This does not verify a rendered recovery flow for older responses lacking it.

After a completed or attempted fill, the visible-browser handoff can now close and restart the session after an explicit warning that page changes and unsent panel answers may be lost. Cancelling leaves the current session open, and an in-progress fill or visual check still blocks the handoff. A focused interface fixture covered cancellation, confirmation, in-progress blocking and restart failure; no live employer form was filled.

The server now retains a sticky fill-attempt flag for the lifetime of the browser session and returns it in session status and resumed inspection results, including after an inspection resets the per-page fill lock. A fictional local-browser fill test checked the session-status response after filling; UI tests treat an absent flag conservatively. A rendered Edge test with simulated form endpoints exercised page reload, resume/rescan, cancellation, and confirmation of a visible reopen; it did not verify a real employer session. Browser sessions do not persist across application restart.

The visible-browser reopen action now asks for confirmation even before a fill attempt, because unsent panel answers or site changes may otherwise be lost. The focused interface test and a rendered Edge reload/rescan test cover cancellation in that state; the rendered test used simulated form responses. The web interface test suite passed.
