new candidates share the 600-link limit. A retry that would exceed the stored
50-page history limit is refused with a recovery explanation. Fictional local
tests cover carryover, repeated references and both limits; no external search
or employer application was performed in this check.

### Existing application values (2026-11-03)

During reviewed prefill, a value that already matches the reviewed answer is
verified and retained instead of re-entered; it remains in the completed-field
set for later checks. A fictional browser fixture confirmed no input event on
the matching field while an empty field was filled. A separate fixture still
stopped before changing fields when an existing value conflicted. Twenty-seven
non-ignored form-preview tests passed; this does not establish behavior on all
application sites, and no application was submitted.

The prefill result and attempt event now distinguish values entered by
Waypoint from values that already matched. A fictional browser check confirmed
that an existing selected option and checkbox were retained without change
events. The larger fictional prefill endpoint test passed twice after two
failures at its screenshot-segment assertion; that capture instability remains
unresolved, so these runs do not establish reliable screenshot coverage.

Follow-up diagnostics observed a 15-pixel reported width change after the first
beyond-viewport screenshot and differing focused-field image hashes. Capture
now performs one bounded warm-up screenshot before measuring and retains the
address, dimensions and first-tile change checks. The endpoint fixture passed
four consecutive runs afterward; a changing-page fixture still reported an
incomplete capture, and four narrow/wide capture fixtures passed. The wide
fixture's last-tile assertion was corrected to match the planner's bottom-aligned
overlap. Blinking or changing content can still make a capture incomplete;
these limited runs do not prove universal screenshot reliability.
If the destination changes during the warm-up, the same bounded recovery
guidance now appears as for a destination change during tile capture.

### Document review status consistency (2026-11-03)

An attached document's visual report now requires an explicit complete status
and no top-level error in addition to complete, clean per-page findings. Failed,
partial, missing or contradictory status metadata requires follow-up even if a
report body appears clean. This is an integrity check, not proof of document
quality or of the model's visual accuracy.

### Unsupported document instructions (2026-11-03)

In a fictional template rewrite, Qwen3.5:9b initially proposed a requested
ten-year claim unsupported by a six-month source, with an invalid citation; the
app withheld it. With an unrelated fact available, it instead added that fact
and its evidence reviewer incorrectly called the addition supported, although
the template provenance guard marked the claims uncertain. Drafting now states
explicitly that writing preferences are not evidence. In the subsequent run,
the model returned no rewrite for the unsupported request, and a separate
ordinary fictional rewrite passed. This is not a general hallucination or
evidence-accuracy benchmark; candidate review remains necessary. The model was
unloaded.

The evidence-review prompt now explicitly states that a draft cannot prove its
own claims. In a focused fictional check reproducing the template-source label,
Qwen3.5:9b previously marked an uncited dashboard claim supported; after the
change it marked that claim unsupported while recognizing the cited Python
claim. A normal fictional rewrite check also passed. These observations do not
establish general evidence accuracy; the existing unverified-template guard and
candidate review remain necessary.

The same local evidence check also flagged an unsupported fictional leadership
claim while retaining its cited onboarding claim as supported. Separately,
document visual follow-up now requires the page report's PDF hash to match the
outer saved-review hash; missing or conflicting hashes require review. Focused
tests and a fictional PDF geometry check passed. These checks do not prove
model accuracy or visual quality.

2026-11-03: On source `667c1898052c52fef1cb7dda705ba22fd6a58448`, the resource-gated `qwen3.5:9b` model (Ollama ID prefix `c97eb11d70b1`) passed one fictional two-section wizard test and one fictional multilingual emergency-contact test on an AMD Radeon RX 9070 XT. The wizard used an explicitly simulated user navigation gesture; the model transcribed the filled given name and email and left the sensitive work-authorization field untouched. In the contact fixture, the model mapped the applicant field and flagged both emergency-contact emails for review. These are single runs on two fixtures, not measurements of real-site completion, visual accuracy, autonomous navigation, or peak memory. The test-owned model was unloaded; `ollama ps` was empty afterwards. No application was submitted.

2026-11-03: The live FreeSerp batch test at source `e386f51` requested four pages across two non-personal fictional search phrases and retained 40 results. All four requests succeeded; provider engine availability was not reported. Some results were articles or training pages rather than vacancies, so this measures candidate discovery only, not vacancy precision, application availability or recall.

2026-11-03: A resource-gated `qwen3.5:9b` test on one fictional long career guide containing a specific vacancy initially classified the mixed page as not a vacancy. After revising the mixed-content instruction and placing the labeled middle excerpt first, the same test returned uncertain and the guide alone returned not_vacancy. The classification unit tests passed. This is one development fixture, not held-out evidence of accuracy; text outside the sampled excerpts can still be missed. The test-owned model was unloaded. No application was submitted.

2026-11-03: A shifted fictional vacancy outside the earlier middle excerpt was initially misclassified as not_vacancy by `qwen3.5:9b`. Reusing the repeated beginning excerpt for an earlier region alone did not fix that case. After adding one bounded focused check when sampled output says not_vacancy, the model returned uncertain for both shifted and centered fictional vacancies; the guide alone returned not_vacancy. This is development-set evidence, not held-out accuracy, and unsampled page regions can still be missed. The test-owned model was unloaded.

2026-11-03: Added a second focused region check before a sampled long page is labelled not_vacancy (at most four classification calls). In one additional fictional hospitality-guide fixture with a vacancy in that later region, resource-gated `qwen3.5:9b` returned uncertain; the focused classification tests passed. This is development evidence, not a held-out accuracy measurement; unsampled text and model errors remain possible. The test-owned model was unloaded.

2026-11-03: Replaced overlapping long-page classification samples with up to four consecutive UTF-8-safe sections of at most 4,000 bytes each. A negative classification requires all covered sections to be negative; conflicts, uncertainty and text beyond the four-call limit stay uncertain. The original mixed-page fixtures and the separate fictional later-vacancy fixture passed with resource-gated `qwen3.5:9b`. A boundary test checked exact reconstruction of a multibyte string and refusal to classify a longer-than-budget page as negative. Sections can lose cross-boundary context and this is not a held-out accuracy result. The test-owned model was unloaded.

2026-11-03: Long-page classification now overlaps adjacent 4,096-byte sections by at least 128 bytes, within the existing four-call limit. The boundary unit test reconstructed a UTF-8 fixture and confirmed a short split vacancy was present in an individual section; text beyond the coverage limit remains uncertain. In one resource-gated `qwen3.5:9b` test, both a fictional boundary vacancy and a later vacancy in a career guide returned uncertain. This does not establish accuracy on unseen listings or prevent longer cross-boundary context loss. The test-owned model was unloaded.

2026-11-03: Duplicate candidate comparison recognizes standalone R&D as research and development, including spaces or tabs around the ampersand. Migration 48 queues matching older titles for the existing bounded recheck; candidate pairs still require user review and are not merged. The duplicate suite passed (45 passed, 2 ignored) and store Clippy passed. This handles one abbreviation and does not establish duplicate recall or precision across employers.

2026-11-03: In one fictional three-candidate search without requisition IDs, `qwen3.5:9b` flagged the semantically related repost and omitted a different team, but also flagged a similar opening in another city and incorrectly described its location as identical. Different locations do not by themselves rule out syndication. The saved review reason now puts the literal field comparison before the model explanation; it does not correct or validate that explanation. Duplicate-search tests passed, and the test-owned model was unloaded. This is not a recall or precision measurement.

2026-11-03: A resource-gated `qwen3.5:9b` fictional fit evaluation distinguished explicit lack of production Kubernetes deployment (contradicted) from training alone (unknown); it did not treat either as supported. This is one criterion and two fictional profiles, not evidence of general fit-score calibration or model reliability. The test-owned model was unloaded.

2026-11-03: Reviewed draft exports now compare extractable DOCX text against the generated PDF in the background and report matches, differences or unavailable checks; the attempt event retains the result. This text comparison does not inspect visual layout or prove semantic correctness. On the current development configuration the default PDF worker reported missing extraction tools; a separate temporary runtime with `pypdf==6.10.0` successfully checked one fictional LibreOffice export. The normal export tests, UI checks and Windows web Clippy passed. The app's configured PDF runtime was not changed.

2026-11-03: The draft-export UI now directs users to the existing managed PDF-tool setup when text extraction dependencies are missing. The setup's isolated Windows test installed its pinned PDF dependencies and rendered a fictional PDF successfully; it did not alter the configured runtime. The export result can be regenerated after setup to repeat the text comparison. The UI logic test passed; no rendered-window verification was performed.

2026-11-03: New draft export receipts bind the reviewed text and PDF to their stored hashes, including exports with no template or generated-draft evidence. Lookup rejects changed reviewed text and preserves older receipts without that field. A reviewed-text-only export is identified accurately when linked to an application attempt. This establishes source material for a future PDF recheck; the recheck workflow itself is not yet implemented. Focused export and UI-logic tests passed; no rendered-window verification was performed.

2026-11-03: Added an on-demand background text recheck for newly receipted draft PDFs. It compares the existing PDF with the bound reviewed text, revalidates both before reporting, appends the outcome to the export record when possible, and does not create another document. A fictional endpoint test with an isolated PDF runtime covered a mismatch, replay without duplicate events, and changed-text rejection; UI logic tests and Windows web Clippy passed. Older exports without bound reviewed text cannot use this recheck. The button was not checked in a rendered window; text extraction does not establish visual quality or factual correctness.

2026-11-03: Application history now offers that recheck for records with a compatible export receipt. The listing exposes a hint only; the recheck independently validates the stored receipt and files. Focused receipt, export-record and UI logic tests passed. The history button has not been checked in a rendered window.

2026-11-03: A recheck started from a visible history entry now identifies that specific export attempt, so unrelated unreadable or truncated history does not prevent its verification. The job, attempt, receipt and file hashes are still validated. A fictional endpoint test passed with 51 records and a truncated listing, including replay and changed-text rejection; focused UI checks and Windows web Clippy passed. History itself remains bounded, so older entries outside the listing are not made visible by this change. No rendered-window check was performed.

2026-11-03: Application history now supports pages of 50, sorted by timestamp and attempt ID. The underlying scan still inspects at most 2,000 directory entries across all jobs, so some records may remain unavailable and the interface reports a limited scan. Archive changes can shift page boundaries. A fictional 51-record test checked both pages and direct recheck; UI logic and Windows web Clippy passed. No rendered-window check was performed.

2026-11-03: PDF visual review now covers up to the supported 50 pages sequentially and offers a stop control. Cancellation takes effect between render or model calls; findings from completed pages are retained as an incomplete review. Ten focused model-free Rust tests, the stop-control UI logic test, JavaScript syntax validation and Windows web Clippy passed. The control was not verified in a rendered window, and no live model run was performed for this change. Visual findings remain advisory.

2026-11-03: Starting an already-resident model now checks current available system memory before reporting it ready, including on the second runtime-state check. The existing allocation limits and pre-load estimates are unchanged. Seven focused model-start tests and Windows web Clippy passed; live-model tests were not run. A memory snapshot cannot reserve RAM or prevent pressure from other processes.

2026-11-03: A stop request for the most recently retrieved document visual review now reports that it has already finished instead of returning not found. The active task and most recent completed result remain distinct; unknown task IDs are rejected. Fictional task-state tests and Windows web Clippy passed. This does not interrupt an in-flight model call or validate visual findings.

2026-11-03: Unsent email drafts now put the UTF-8 attachment filename before its ASCII fallback and split long RFC 2231 values into bounded continuations without splitting percent escapes. Python's independent email parser recovered a fictional long Japanese filename and exact attachment bytes from the generated MIME draft; initially it selected the fallback with the old order. Six focused email-draft tests and Windows web Clippy passed. Mail-client interoperability remains unverified; no email was sent.

2026-11-03: Re-ran the fictional two-section browser navigation worker, including earlier-answer and attachment readback and changed-value review. The loopback fixture now counts non-GET requests and observed none in this run. This exercises a manually advanced fictional form without a local model; it does not prove that real sites will avoid network writes or that applications can be completed. Windows web Clippy passed; no real application was contacted or submitted.

2026-11-03: Search cards now surface a provider's ambiguous sponsored marker without treating it as proof of advertising or removing the result. Repeated exact-URL reports propagate the marker within the bounded search results; explicitly marked ads remain omitted. Sixteen fictional parser tests, search UI logic, JavaScript syntax and Windows web Clippy passed. The marker is not retained in the selected-listing attribution or retroactively added to older searches; no live provider or rendered UI was tested in this change.

2026-11-03: The ambiguous provider-sponsored marker now survives candidate selection and the first saved listing capture and is displayed alongside the unverified search attribution. Older records without it remain valid; existing captures are not overwritten. Fictional selection persistence and listing-save/retry endpoint tests, UI logic checks and Windows web Clippy passed. No live provider, rendered UI or employer application was used.

2026-11-03: Fit assessment now leaves supported duration requirements unknown when the cited fact uses a recognizable adjacent English upper-bound phrase such as 'up to six years'; the duration fallback also refuses to retrieve such facts as minimum experience. An unrelated upper bound on team size is not treated as a duration bound in the fictional checks. Seventy-seven focused fit tests, the prior-version staleness test and Windows web Clippy passed. The first test build had a fixture-name error and passed after correction. Existing assessments become stale; phrasing outside these bounded patterns and model judgments still need review. No live model was loaded.

2026-11-03: Form review sessions now retain the selected lesson revisions that informed mapping and recheck the selected set before reviewed prefill. If a lesson is corrected, revoked, disabled, or displaced by a newly selected report, prefill stops for reinspection. A fictional local-browser endpoint test saved and revoked a reviewed lesson, confirmed the field remained unchanged, then completed the existing retry fixture. Thirteen focused experience-related tests and Windows web Clippy passed. Initial fixture runs needed duplicate indexing and a corrected empty-field expectation. This does not prove the model used the lessons correctly or cover real employer forms; no application was submitted.

2026-11-03: When prefill is stopped because its selected lessons changed or cannot be checked, the job is now queued for manual review and an existing attempt records the interruption. Failures to save either are included in the error. A fictional local-browser endpoint test revoked a lesson, verified the untouched field, the review reason and attempt event, then exercised the existing retry path. Windows web Clippy passed. No model or employer page was used.

2026-11-03: A fictional local-browser test filled and read back a field inside three nested accessible frames, then confirmed the old selector was rejected after replacing the outer frame. One test passed; it did not exercise a model, a real employer or submission. This does not establish universal frame coverage. Available system memory fell during concurrent host activity; no additional model or build was started.

2026-11-03: If lesson changes or an unavailable lesson check stop prefill before any action, the interface now says no fields were filled in that attempt and keeps the fill button disabled until reinspection. A fictional UI test covered both reasons and a review-record failure suffix. No rendered-window check, model, build or employer form was used while host memory was low.

2026-11-03: On job-list redraw, observation ages and the old-or-unknown filter now include elapsed time since the last state response. A recent observation crosses into the old category after 72 hours; a backwards or invalid clock becomes unknown. Existing visible cards do not update until the list is redrawn, and neither age nor refresh proves vacancy status. Fictional UI tests covered the threshold, clock rollback and existing filters/sorts. No rendered-window or live-provider test was run; no background timer or model was added.

2026-11-03: Concurrent UI state refreshes now discard an earlier-requested response after a later-requested response has already been applied. If the later request fails, an earlier successful response can still be applied. A fictional asynchronous UI test covered both orderings; this uses client request order rather than a server revision and does not prove snapshot chronology. No rendered-window test or model was run.

2026-11-03: Running all standalone UI checks found three harnesses that had not imported the sponsored-result renderer added earlier. The harnesses now use the actual helper, and page-selection coverage checks that a later sponsored marker reaches both the card and saved selection. The first rerun exposed an outdated expected context; after correction all 145 standalone UI checks passed. These are simulated UI checks, not rendered-browser verification.

2026-11-03: Profile saves now require the revision returned with the editor's initial state. A SQLite transaction compares that revision with the current sealed profile before writing; a stale editor is refused without discarding its form text. The UI requires reloading after copying edits when a conflict occurs, and old open tabs without the revision must reload. Fictional tests covered API conflicts, simultaneous connections, DPAPI-backed profile updates and UI behavior; 146 standalone UI checks and Windows web Clippy passed. An initial concurrency fixture was stopped after it waited on the exclusive workspace lock and was replaced with two store connections. No real candidate information or model was used. The prior hosted supply-chain job reported four vulnerabilities and six denied warnings; it was not remediated in this change.

2026-11-03: Updated the web app's ZIP library from 2.4.2 to 9.2.3 and adapted DOCX entry-name handling; updated Tokio to 1.54.1, async-executor to 1.14.1 and clipboard-win to 5.4.3. The final cargo audit reports no vulnerabilities but still fails on three unmaintained zbus_names/zvariant/zvariant_derive dependencies in the legacy Unix desktop chain. A trial upgrade of eframe did not remove them and was reverted. License, ban and source checks passed. The full Windows web suite passed 679 tests (376 ignored), the ATS group passed, Windows web Clippy passed, and a fictional styled DOCX edit converted to PDF with its output visually inspected. The conversion checks required the existing isolated PDF runtime; initial runs failed when it was absent or pointed at its directory, and the first styled fixture omitted required whitespace preservation before correction. No live model or employer application was used.

2026-11-03: A stale profile editor can now compare with current saved details and explicitly rebase its draft: untouched fields adopt current values, edited fields stay in the draft, and fields changed in both sessions are named for review. The action does not save; the revision check runs again on save. If a saved select option is unavailable, no fields are changed and the revision is not advanced. Fictional UI tests covered conflicts, unavailable options, initial baseline capture and failed saves; all 147 standalone UI checks passed. This was not checked in a rendered window, and no model or employer site was used.

2026-11-03: Profile refresh now retains a selected template ID when its document is unavailable, with a visible option asking the user to replace or clear it. This prevents a refresh from silently turning that selection into an empty value; the server still refuses an unavailable template on save. A simulated UI check covered missing, restored and empty selections and state refresh ordering. No rendered-window check was performed.

2026-11-03: A subsequent isolated rendered-browser check used a fictional template and mocked state refresh. The missing template remained selected with the unavailable label, and a later refresh restored its filename and selection. No page errors were reported. The first fixture attempts needed the hidden form to be awaited as attached and the initial state to finish loading; the corrected run passed. This did not exercise an actual document deletion or a profile save.

2026-11-03: The previous hosted run exposed a stale model-memory test expectation: the current gate requires a 6 GiB model plus 9 GiB system headroom, and the fixture now checks the 15 GiB boundary. The focused test passed locally. The hosted browser fixture job now excludes two explicitly opt-in public-URL probes that require a reviewed URL; local browser fixtures remain selected. The previous run also showed a discovery task concurrency failure, a browser process-termination failure, and three denied unmaintained dependency notices. Those remain unresolved; the new hosted run was still in progress when checked.

2026-11-03: Two discovery lifecycle tests shared the application's single process-wide task slot and could run concurrently under the test harness. They now serialize with a test-only mutex, retaining the production single-task behavior. All three discovery task tests passed locally with normal parallel test scheduling. Hosted confirmation remains pending; this does not resolve the separate browser process-termination or dependency-audit failures.

2026-11-03: The forced-parent browser termination fixture passed once locally on Windows when run alone; no process with a Waypoint browser profile was found immediately afterward. Available system memory was approximately 15.11 GiB before and 14.46 GiB after, not a peak measurement. The earlier hosted failure reported 128 surviving observed process IDs; its cause remains unverified and this single local pass does not close it.

2026-11-03: The termination fixture now reports up to 20 surviving process IDs and executable names on failure, without command lines, before its scoped cleanup. IDs can be reused, so these diagnostics alone do not establish process ownership. The fixture passed again locally; the new failure diagnostics have not been exercised on the hosted runner.

2026-11-03: Saving a profile with an unavailable selected template now identifies whether the CV or cover letter needs a replacement or a cleared selection. A fictional API test verified both messages and that the rejected save leaves the prior profile and revision unchanged. The rendered error path was not checked.

2026-11-03: The Windows web test suite passed 699 tests with 385 ignored, using one build job and two test threads on local source commit `54db3e43`. Ignored live-browser and model checks are not included in that result. The three denied unmaintained notices originate in the older desktop UI's Unix accessibility dependency chain; no dependency was removed or suppressed in this check. Hosted qualification remains separate.

2026-11-03: When two application-inspection snapshots show different loading problems, the change report now retains both disappearing-field and continuing-change warnings, without duplicating either. A focused fictional test covered both observation orders and repeated signals. The form still requires manual review; no real employer page or model was used in this change.

2026-11-03: After that change, the local browser fixture for a form changing at the same URL passed: it still detects the change and requests reinspection without typing or submitting. This fixture does not itself produce both different loading signals. In the hosted run for `352cebc4`, the supply-chain job failed at the audit step; other jobs were still running when checked, and the complete log was not yet available through the run summary.

2026-11-03: Attempt screenshot listings now count unique canonical screenshot references in the bounded event history whose files are missing, inaccessible, linked or outside size limits, and the interface warns about them. A fictional record test covered duplicate references to one missing file; a simulated interface test covered the warning and partial event history. This metadata check does not validate image contents, and an incomplete event scan can miss additional references. The rendered interface was not checked.

2026-11-03: A subsequent isolated rendered-browser check displayed the missing-screenshot warning and partial-history notice using a fictional, mocked response; no page errors were observed. It did not exercise a real missing file through the UI. Hosted CI for commit `352cebc4` completed with browser, build/test, PDF and agent-tool jobs passing; the supply-chain audit failed on the three previously identified unmaintained dependency notices. This result does not cover later commits.

2026-11-03: Screenshot-reference inspection now checks filesystem metadata once per unique canonical reference within the bounded event scan. The existing fictional screenshot viewer test passed; no storage performance measurement was made.

2026-11-03: Screenshot references now also require an accessible file with a PNG signature, using the same bounded check as the screenshot list. A fictional test covered a missing file, an oversized file, a bad signature and duplicate references. A PNG signature is not a full decode or integrity check; later corruption may remain undetected.

2026-11-03: Hosted coverage run `41597789874` for commit `352cebc4` completed successfully. It does not cover later local commits. The audit notices trace through the legacy desktop shell's accessibility dependencies; disabling accessibility solely to make the audit pass would remove functionality and was not done.

2026-11-03: Added an ignored, resource-gated local-model fixture for the lower-bound experience retrieval and independent comparison. It compiles, but was not run: available system memory was approximately 15.91 GiB, only slightly above the existing startup estimate for the model. The fixture supplies the retrieved candidate directly to the separate model check; it is not a full end-to-end fit assessment or an accuracy benchmark.

2026-11-03: Read-only public Lever evaluation used an Apex.AI vacancy and its application route. Isolated crawler captures returned HTTP 200 with expected content; native capture SHA-256 values were `44efe05c7aa06f77954cf44e79ce1d1b9a1037a512531e6c004e68d49c8e505d` and `4b39ddfce8a45303897c031e6127e2d66c8a5fddd903d2e57389299bacc50976`, retained outside Git. The direct application-check worker followed the link, observed 17 fields and reported a possible challenge, hidden required control and obstructing dialog for manual review. No candidate data, model, fill or submission was used. One page does not establish Lever-wide behavior; available memory after the run was about 11.95 GiB and no Waypoint-profile browser was observed. The opt-in read-only diagnostic now admits the policy-permitted Lever host.

2026-11-03: A second read-only check opened the same Lever application route directly and again detected 17 fields and five review signals. A retained initial viewport showed a privacy notice covering the lower part of the form, consistent with an obstruction warning. This single viewport does not establish whether the reported possible challenge or required hidden control is real, and the notice was not dismissed in this check. No local model, candidate data or submission was used.

2026-11-03: In a separate visible temporary browser, the operator dismissed that privacy notice and reinspected the same Lever form. The driver reported 17 fields and a dialog warning before, then 18 fields, no dialog warning and two remaining review reasons afterward. The visible notice was absent after the action. This is one page and does not establish the meaning of the remaining warnings or full form coverage. No candidate data was entered, no application was submitted, and no Waypoint-profile browser remained immediately after the test. The opt-in visible review diagnostic now also accepts the policy-permitted Lever host.

2026-11-03: A repeat of the visible Lever review gave the same 17-to-18 field change and two remaining signals. The existing summary identifies one as a possible CAPTCHA or verification challenge; the other is not identified by that summary. Neither this observation nor the initial viewport proves that a challenge is active. The diagnostic now prints the bounded obstacle summary. No challenge was solved, fields filled or application submitted.

2026-11-03: Mixed-obstacle summaries now explicitly identify ambiguous labels or controls, possible sign-in, and custom controls or field requirements needing review, without echoing page-supplied labels. Twenty-one application-check tests passed; 24 live or browser tests were ignored. This change has not been retested on the Lever page and does not determine the second signal observed there.

2026-11-03: A subsequent visible Lever recheck after dismissing the privacy notice reported 18 fields and identified the remaining signals as a possible challenge and custom controls or field requirements needing review. The precise custom control and whether the challenge is active remain unverified. This was a read-only diagnostic with no candidate data, model, field entry or submission; no Waypoint-profile browser remained immediately afterward.

2026-11-03: A headless diagnostic attempted the previously observed privacy-notice control on the same public form. The driver refused it because the target could submit or navigate, so the notice was not dismissed and the page requires manual review. The first diagnostic treated the refusal as a failure; the corrected diagnostic records this expected guarded outcome and passed. No candidate data was entered, and no application was submitted. This is an observed autonomy limitation, not a reason to bypass the guard.

2026-11-03: A headless fictional loopback diagnostic demonstrated a serious dry-run limitation: a `type=button` labelled Continue ran JavaScript that submitted a fictional form, and the local server received the POST while the click returned success. No external site or candidate data was involved. HTML type checks and after-the-fact network observation do not prevent such writes; do not infer that interacting with a real site is guaranteed not to submit. The diagnostic is excluded from the general ignored-browser CI suite because it intentionally asserts the current unsafe behavior; it must be replaced by a prevention assertion when a complete defense is implemented.

2026-11-03: Reviewed official CDP Fetch and Target documentation with isolated headless captures (SHA-256 `bf189c92e07096d0161a25f61e5215d564bb44ebd93639994a38c79521a6f719` and `521d7c470ea62f9cc11c9b146dc67d30db1c1c05e6c2c06930a0f8b21a01bc79`, retained outside Git). Request-stage Fetch interception can pause requests before sending, and related targets may require recursive auto-attachment. These APIs alone do not establish complete write prevention, especially for existing WebSockets and other channels. An initial Fetch capture with the overly specific marker `requestPaused` was rejected as unexpected content; a later capture with an appropriate marker succeeded. No interception implementation or live-site interaction was performed in this research.

2026-11-03: A separate headless protocol experiment enabled request-stage Fetch interception after loading a fictional loopback page, observed a paused POST, rejected it, and confirmed the loopback server received no POST. This is a single manually serviced top-level request, not an application-level guard. It does not address other targets, GET writes, delayed actions, disconnection, service workers or WebSockets; the prior WebSocket fixture demonstrates a separate gap. No external application was contacted.

2026-11-03: Buffered CDP `Fetch.requestPaused` events now retain bounded routing identifiers, method and response status only, rather than URLs, headers or body. A fictional unit test checked that private fixture data is not retained, and the loopback interception experiment still passed. This prepares protocol handling but does not enable interception in the app or resolve the scripted-submission gap.


## Automatic runtime provisioning and dependency refresh — 2026-11-09

The default Windows package now automatically provisions a pinned headless
browser and converter rather than depending on implicit host installations.
Python/PDF tools and Ollama remain bundled by default. Required Microsoft C++
runtime DLLs are extracted from a verified publisher-signed redistributable
without running its installer, then inventoried beside their consumers. The
OpenSSL build SDK also defaults to a verified repository-local cache; explicit
SDK overrides remain supported. Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7 module
paths/child-exit behavior were exercised and corrected.

PDF setup can now bootstrap Python and all PDF wheels natively into a new
per-user runtime, without an existing Python or pip. It uses exact artifact
digests, bounded archive extraction, an isolated import check and atomic active
runtime selection. Failed setup preserves the previous runtime. The Settings
copy describes automatic setup and the optional custom-interpreter path.

The Rust graph, qualified Python research lock, portable PDF/Node runtime pins
and CI action pins were refreshed to current compatible releases. Compatibility
fixes preserve XML/text behavior, integrity hashes, PDF decompression bounds and
conservative glyph editing. The dependency report records actual checks and
remaining deployment limits: [dependency evidence](docs/DEPENDENCY_REFRESH_2026-11-09.md)
and [runtime setup](docs/RUNTIME_SETUP.md).

Scoped career-history suggestions now select exact saved components for one
explicitly chosen reviewed entry and up to 20 eligible fields. Results are
revision/session/schema-bound and require checked proposals before local answer
editors change. Existing sensitive/model-review blocks remain in force. Local
12B/14B diagnostics had different completeness outcomes; these limits and the
remaining section/provenance work are recorded in [career history](docs/CANDIDATE_HISTORY.md).
No real application interaction or submission and no release occurred.


## Integrated career-history subject and visual checks — 2026-11-09

Contact mapping now distinguishes ordinary candidate career facts from
third-party/sensitive questions while preserving all existing review flags.
Applicant contact reuse for organizations remains blocked, including educational
institutions, and history resolution independently refuses recognizable
other-person labels even when a model chooses a valid candidate component.

The local 12B model completed seven intended contact/history fields on each of
two fictional layouts with different labels and section markup. Every intended
field passed local visual transcription and independent live-value checks;
four restricted fields stayed blank. An initial visual mismatch exposed native
month display formatting. Typed Gregorian month reconciliation now uses ICU/CLDR
data, keeps the original observation, requires full precision and still verifies
the live control. Adversarial checks cover stale values, wrong type, incomplete
selection and ambiguous observations. No actual employer form was used.

The web suite passed 722 tests with 396 ignored; local-model and headless checks
were explicitly selected separately. Source/model records, screenshots and full
results remain private. See [the qualification report](docs/HISTORY_PIPELINE_2026-11-09.md)
for scope, failed diagnostics, dependencies and remaining gates. No release or
application submission occurred.
